246: /// Who a hub belongs to, which is not the same as who is speaking it.
247: ///
248: /// Ownership answers both questions the engine asks about a hub: whether it
249: /// appears to an actor, and whether that actor may lead out of it. Keeping one
250: /// definition of it is what stops the two answers from drifting apart, which is
251: /// how a hub ends up visible but with no way to move on from it.
252: ///
253: /// A hub carrying recipients is private to those recipients, so *they* own it
254: /// and its speaker does not. The speaker may be another character's voice
255: /// entirely, and someone who owns a hub can lead it without speaking it. Without
256: /// recipients the speaker owns the hub. With neither, the hub is open and nobody
257: /// owns it, so it needs no ownership check at all.
258: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
259: enum Owned<'a> {
260:   /// Neither recipients nor a speaker. Reached by everyone, owned by nobody.
261:   Open,
262:   /// Private to these speakers, whoever is speaking it.
263:   Private(&'a [LouderName]),
264:   /// Belongs to one speaker, and reaches everyone if that speaker is public.
265:   Spoken(LouderName),
266: }
267: 
268: impl<'a> Owned<'a> {
269:   fn of(hub: &'a Hub) -> Owned<'a> {
270:     if hub.has_recipients() {
271:       Owned::Private(hub.recipients())
272:     } else if hub.speaker() == LOUDER_NAME_NONE {
273:       Owned::Open
274:     } else {
275:       Owned::Spoken(hub.speaker())
276:     }
277:   }
278: 
279:   /// Whether a hub with this ownership is reached by an actor hearing `heard`.
280:   ///
281:   /// This is the visibility half. `heard` comes from
282:   /// [`Self::speakers_heard_by`], so it already includes the speakers the actor
283:   /// speaks as.
284:   fn reaches(self, context: &LouderContext, heard: &[LouderName]) -> bool {
285:     match self {
286:       Owned::Open => true,
287:       // Private means private: a recipient is reached only by those who hear it,
288:       // and a public recipient is no more open than a private one.
289:       Owned::Private(recipients) => recipients.iter().any(|r| heard.contains(r)),
290:       // A public speaker is heard by everyone, so its hub reaches everyone.
291:       Owned::Spoken(speaker) => context.is_speaker_public(speaker) || heard.contains(&speaker),
292:     }
293:   }
294: 
295:   /// Whether the actor speaks as one of the owners, which is an outright claim
296:   /// that no other actor can take away.
297:   fn spoken_by(self, speak_as: &[LouderName]) -> bool {
298:     match self {
299:       Owned::Open => false,
300:       Owned::Private(recipients) => recipients.iter().any(|r| speak_as.contains(r)),
301:       Owned::Spoken(speaker) => speak_as.contains(&speaker),
302:     }
303:   }
304: 
305:   /// Whether some actor other than `actor` already speaks as one of the owners,
306:   /// so the voice is being played elsewhere and is not this actor's to lead.
307:   fn held_by_other(self, context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, actor: LouderActor) -> bool {
308:     let held = |speaker: &LouderName| {
309:       Simulator::speaker_is_taken_by(context, conversation, *speaker, actor)
310:     };
311: 
312:     match self {
313:       Owned::Open => false,
314:       Owned::Private(recipients) => recipients.iter().any(held),
315:       Owned::Spoken(speaker) => held(&speaker),
316:     }
317:   }
318: }
319: 
320: impl Simulator {
321:   /// Whether `hub` may appear to `actor` at all.
322:   ///
323:   /// This is the visibility half of the rules: auto hubs never appear, debug
324:   /// actors see everything, and everyone else is filtered by the hub's owners
325:   /// and its conditions.
326:   pub fn hub_appears(
327:     context: &LouderContext,
328:     conversation: LouderName,
329:     actor: LouderActor,
330:     position: LouderPosition,
331:   ) -> bool {
332:     let Some(conversation_ref) = context.get_conversation(conversation) else {
333:       return false;
334:     };
335:     let Some(hub) = conversation_ref.hub(position) else {
336:       return false;
337:     };
338:     let Some(entry) = context.get_actor(conversation, actor) else {
339:       return false;
340:     };
341: 
342:     // Auto hubs resolve on their own and are never shown.
343:     if hub.is_auto() {
344:       return entry.is_debug();
345:     }
346: 
347:     let heard = Self::speakers_heard_by(context, conversation, actor);
348:     if !Owned::of(hub).reaches(context, &heard) {
349:       return false;
350:     }
351: 
352:     Self::conditions_hold(context, conversation, actor, position, hub.expressions())
353:   }
354: 
355:   /// Whether every condition on a hub holds.
356:   ///
357:   /// This is a pure predicate: it neither writes story state nor emits events,
358:   /// because the FFI answers "what can this actor see" through a `const` handle
359:   /// and a question must not be able to change the answer. The corresponding
360:   /// [`Self::report_conditions`] events are emitted when an actor actually
361:   /// travels to the hub.
362:   ///
363:   /// The actor and the hub are needed because a condition may ask the engine
364:   /// about the hub it is attached to, as `%speaker` does.
365:   fn conditions_hold(
366:     context: &LouderContext,
367:     conversation: LouderName,
368:     actor: LouderActor,
369:     hub_position: LouderPosition,
370:     expressions: &[LouderExpression],
371:   ) -> bool {
372:     expressions.iter().all(|expression| {
373:       let Some(source) = context.resolve_name(*expression) else {
374:         return true;
375:       };
376: 
377:       // Only the condition half of the expression set gates visibility;
378:       // statements are for the first visit. Parsed once here, because this runs
379:       // for every hub on every choice query.
380:       match super::expression::parse(source) {
381:         Ok(expr) if super::expression::reads_only(&expr) => {
382:           super::expression::condition_holds(context, conversation, actor, hub_position, &expr)
383:         }
384:         Ok(_) => true,
385:         // A condition that cannot be parsed cannot be relied on.
386:         Err(_) => false,
387:       }
388:     })
389:   }
390: 
391:   /// Emit an event for each condition evaluated when an actor lands on a hub.
392:   fn report_conditions(
393:     context: &mut LouderContext,
394:     conversation: LouderName,
395:     actor: LouderActor,
396:     channel: LouderName,
397:     motion: &Motion,
398:     expressions: &[LouderExpression],
399:   ) {
400:     let sources: Vec<(LouderExpression, String)> = expressions
401:       .iter()
402:       .filter_map(|expression| {
403:         context
404:           .resolve_name(*expression)
405:           .map(|source| (*expression, source.to_string()))
406:       })
407:       .collect();
408: 
409:     let mut scope = EvaluationScope::new(conversation, actor, channel);
410:     scope.hub = motion.target_position;
411:     scope.motion = Some(motion.clone());
412:     let evaluator = Evaluator::new(scope);
413: 
414:     for (expression, source) in sources {
415:       if super::expression::classify(&source) != super::expression::ExpressionKind::Condition {
416:         continue;
417:       }
418: 
419:       let outcome = match evaluator.evaluate(context, &source) {
420:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(value)) => value,
421:         Ok(Evaluation::Suspended) => continue,
422:         Err(_) => LouderVariant::Int(0),
423:       };
424: 
425:       let condition = EventData::Condition(ConditionOutcome {
426:         condition: expression,
427:         outcome,
428:       });
429:       let event = LouderEvent {
430:         conversation,
431:         channel,
432:         actor,
433:         data: condition,
434:       };
435:       context.push_event(event);
436:     }
437:   }
438: 
439:   /// Whether a single choice is available to `actor` from `hub`.
440:   ///
441:   /// The choice rules of the engine guide, in four steps:
442:   ///  1. the actor can only decide from a hub that actually appears to them;
443:   ///  2. the choice has to lead somewhere that appears to them — an auto hub
444:   ///     never appears, but a choice may still lead through one, in which case
445:   ///     only its conditions have to hold;
446:   ///  3. they have to own the target: speaking as one of its owners settles the
447:   ///     matter outright, and otherwise listening to an owner, or the owner
448:   ///     being public, settles it as long as nobody else plays the voice;
449:   ///  4. otherwise, the spoke rule lets a lone choice through whenever the voice
450:   ///     is one they can reach, which is what stops linear dialogue handing the
451:   ///     lead back and forth between every actor.
452:   ///
453:   /// Step 3 is what lets a voice heard but not played be led from: a character
454:   /// listening to a speaker owns that speaker's hubs, and would otherwise be
455:   /// shown a hub with no way off it wherever the owning hub carries more than
456:   /// one choice.
457:   ///
458:   /// The spoke rule is deliberately the one case the guide does not qualify with
459:   /// "no other actor has that speaker in its speak as list". Cases 2 and 3 are
460:   /// about a voice nobody is playing, and are refused while somebody else is
461:   /// playing it; case 4 is about a hub with nowhere else to go. An actor shown
462:   /// one choice on a hub it cannot otherwise leave would be stuck there with no
463:   /// way out, so the rule answers that rather than the question of who owns the
464:   /// line. It still needs the voice to be reachable, so it never offers a choice
465:   /// into somewhere the actor cannot see.
466:   ///
467:   /// Ownership is [`Owned`], the same definition [`Self::hub_appears`] uses, so
468:   /// the two can never disagree. A hub an actor is shown but cannot decide from
469:   /// would dead-end a conversation that no interface could recover.
470:   pub fn choice_available(
471:     context: &LouderContext,
472:     conversation: LouderName,
473:     actor: LouderActor,
474:     hub_position: LouderPosition,
475:     choice_index: usize,
476:   ) -> ChoiceAvailableError {
477:     let Some(conversation_ref) = context.get_conversation(conversation) else {
478:       return ChoiceAvailableError::ConversationMissing;
479:     };
480:     let Some(hub) = conversation_ref.hub(hub_position) else {
481:       return ChoiceAvailableError::FromMissing;
482:     };
483:     let Some(choice) = hub.choice(choice_index) else {
484:       return ChoiceAvailableError::ChoiceMissing;
485:     };
486:     let Some(target) = conversation_ref.hub(choice.position()) else {
487:       return ChoiceAvailableError::ToMissing;
488:     };
489:     let Some(entry) = context.get_actor(conversation, actor) else {
490:       return ChoiceAvailableError::ActorMissing;
491:     };
492: 
493:     // Debug actors see every choice regardless of the rules.
494:     if entry.is_debug() {
495:       return ChoiceAvailableError::NoError;
496:     }
497: 
498:     // A spectator sees every hub but is not allowed to decide, so it is only
499:     // given choices when it is also a debug actor.
500:     if entry.is_spectator() {
501:       return ChoiceAvailableError::Spectator;
502:     }
503: 
504:     // An actor may only decide from a hub that appears to them. Auto hubs are
505:     // the exception: they are resolved on the way through and never stood on,
506:     // so an actor passing through one still gets to make the decision there.
507:     if !hub.is_auto() && !Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, actor, hub_position) {
508:       return ChoiceAvailableError::FromInvisibleToActor;
509:     }
510: 
511:     // A choice can only be taken where it leads somewhere real. An auto hub is
512:     // the exception: it never appears to anyone, but entering one is a way of
513:     // moving, and selecting such a choice is how an actor gets into the auto
514:     // logic that decides where it actually lands. Its conditions still gate it:
515:     // "auto hubs never appear" means they are never shown, not that they
516:     // always hold, and a choice must not lead into a hub whose conditions say
517:     // it is not there. This is the half of the visibility rules auto logic is
518:     // left to read, since nothing ever stands on an auto hub to see it.
519:     if target.is_auto() {
520:       if !Self::conditions_hold(context, conversation, actor, target.position(), target.expressions()) {
521:         return ChoiceAvailableError::ToInvisibleToActor;
522:       }
523:     } else if !Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, actor, target.position()) {
524:       return ChoiceAvailableError::ToInvisibleToActor;
525:     }
526: 
527:     // The target's owners decide whether this actor may lead it. For a private
528:     // hub those are the recipients, not the speaker, so owning a hub and speaking
529:     // it come apart: a recipient may lead another character's speech.
530:     let owners = Owned::of(target);
531: 
532:     // Speaking as an owner is an outright claim.
533:     if owners.spoken_by(entry.speak_as()) {
534:       return ChoiceAvailableError::NoError;
535:     }
536: 
537:     // Whether the voice reaches this actor at all: it is theirs to speak, or
538:     // listen to, or is public. Every case below is about a voice that does.
539: 
540:     // --redundant with hub logic
541:     let heard = Self::speakers_heard_by(context, conversation, actor);
542:     if !owners.reaches(context, &heard) {
543:       return ChoiceAvailableError::ToInvisibleToActor;
544:     }
545: 
546:     // The second and third cases: the voice is heard but nobody is playing it,
547:     // so this actor may lead it without stepping on somebody else's line. This
548:     // is what carries a choice out of a multi-choice hub rather than only out of
549:     // a linear one.
550:     if !owners.held_by_other(context, conversation, actor) {
551:       return ChoiceAvailableError::NoError;
552:     }
553: 
554:     // The spoke rule. Somebody else is playing the voice, but this hub has one
555:     // choice and the actor can see where it goes, so refusing it would leave them
556:     // standing on a hub with no way off it.
557:     if !context.rules().contains(DialogueRules::SPOKE) || hub.choice_count() != 1 {
558:       return ChoiceAvailableError::HeldNoSpoke;
559:     }
560: 
561:     ChoiceAvailableError::NoError
562:   }
563: 
564:   /// The indices of the choices available to `actor` from `hub_position`.
565:   pub fn available_choices(
566:     context: &LouderContext,
567:     conversation: LouderName,
568:     actor: LouderActor,
569:     hub_position: LouderPosition,
570:   ) -> Vec<usize> {
571:     let Some(hub) = context
572:       .get_conversation(conversation)
573:       .and_then(|conversation_ref| conversation_ref.hub(hub_position))
574:     else {
575:       return Vec::new();
576:     };
577: 
578:     (0..hub.choice_count())
579:       .filter(|index| Self::choice_available(context, conversation, actor, hub_position, *index) == ChoiceAvailableError::NoError)
580:       .collect()
581:   }
582: 
583:   /// Announce an auto hub to the debug actors in the conversation.
584:   ///
585:   /// Auto hubs resolve silently for the players, since by definition they never
586:   /// appear to anyone. A debug actor exists precisely to watch them go by, so an
587:   /// entered auto hub is announced to those actors and to nobody else. Announced
588:   /// once per move begun from outside, so a follower emulating the same auto hub
589:   /// does not announce it a second time.
590:   fn announce_auto_to_debug(
591:     context: &mut LouderContext,
592:     conversation: LouderName,
593:     auto_hub: LouderPosition,
594:   ) {
595:     let watchers: Vec<(LouderActor, LouderPosition)> = context
596:       .actors(conversation)
597:       .iter()
598:       .filter(|entry| entry.is_debug())
599:       .map(|entry| (entry.id(), entry.position()))
600:       .collect();
601: 
602:     for (actor, position) in watchers {
603:       let motion = Motion {
604:         source_position: position,
605:         target_position: auto_hub,
606:         choice_index: -1,
607:         speaker: Self::hub_speaker_for(context, conversation, actor, auto_hub),
608:         background: false,
609:       };
610:       let event = LouderEvent {
611:         conversation,
612:         channel: LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
613:         actor,
614:         data: EventData::RegularMotion(motion),
615:       };
616:       context.push_event(event);
617:     }
618:   }
619: 
620:   /// Move an actor to `target`, emitting every event the move implies.
621:   ///
622:   /// This is the entry point for a single move. A move sets off a cascade of
623:   /// warps, and the cascade is what stops itself from looping: see
624:   /// [`Self::travel_within`].
625:   pub fn travel(
626:     context: &mut LouderContext,
627:     conversation: LouderName,
628:     actor: LouderActor,
629:     target: LouderPosition,
630:     kind: MotionKind,
631:     fake_channel: LouderName,
632:     fake_priority: i32,
633:   ) -> Result<LouderPosition, SimError> {
634:     Self::travel_within(
635:       context,
636:       conversation,
637:       actor,
638:       target,
639:       kind,
640:       fake_channel,
641:       fake_priority,
642:       None,
643:       false,
644:       Self::NO_CHOICE,
645:       &mut Vec::new(),
646:     )
647:   }
648: 
649:   /// Move an actor and warp whoever it drags along, recording both in `moved`.
650:   ///
651:   /// Two actors sharing a channel each look like a follower of the other, so
652:   /// left unguarded a choice would have the pair trade places forever: the
653:   /// leader moves, the other follows, and then the leader is a follower of the
654:   /// one that just followed it. `moved` holds every actor already placed in
655:   /// this cascade, and an actor already in it does not move again, which also
656:   /// bounds the recursion to at most one move per actor.
657:   ///
658:   /// This is the single way an actor changes position. Choosing and being forced
659:   /// are both just this with a different [`MotionKind`] and a different
660:   /// `chosen`, so the rules of a move live here once rather than once per way of
661:   /// starting one.
662:   ///
663:   /// `emulating` says this move is a follower being carried through a hub, and
664:   /// is what [`Self::resolve_auto`] consults when an auto hub offers that
665:   /// follower nothing it may actually take. `chosen` is the index the actor
666:   /// picked, or [`Self::NO_CHOICE`] when it did not pick anything, and is what a
667:   /// follower replays when it is dragged along.
668:   ///
669:   /// `forced_auto` names a hub this move passes through whether or not its own
670:   /// flag says so. It carries the leader's auto hub down to a follower
671:   /// emulating it, which is what lets two actors resolve the same hub
672:   /// separately.
673:   #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
674:   fn travel_within(
675:     context: &mut LouderContext,
676:     conversation: LouderName,
677:     actor: LouderActor,
678:     target: LouderPosition,
679:     kind: MotionKind,
680:     fake_channel: LouderName,
681:     fake_priority: i32,
682:     forced_auto: Option<LouderPosition>,
683:     emulating: bool,
684:     chosen: i32,
685:     moved: &mut Vec<LouderActor>,
686:   ) -> Result<LouderPosition, SimError> {
687:     // Already placed in this cascade, either as its leader or as someone it
688:     // pulled along, so the actor stays exactly where it is. This is checked
689:     // before resolving auto hubs, since a hub the actor is not going to enter
690:     // is no reason to fail the move.
691:     if moved.contains(&actor) {
692:       return context
693:         .get_actor(conversation, actor)
694:         .map(|entry| entry.position())
695:         .ok_or(SimError::Missing);
696:     }
697: 
698:     // Where the actor is coming from decides whether the hub it lands on counts
699:     // as auto, so this is read before the move changes the position.
700:     let source = context
701:       .get_actor(conversation, actor)
702:       .map(|entry| entry.position())
703:       .ok_or(SimError::Missing)?;
704: 
705:     // Whoever was with this actor where it came from is who it drags along, so
706:     // the channel has to be read before the move changes it.
707:     let from_channel = context
708:       .get_actor(conversation, actor)
709:       .map(|entry| entry.channel())
710:       .unwrap_or(LOUDER_NAME_NONE);
711: 
712:     // The singleplayer rule can make a hub auto for this arrival without the
713:     // flag saying so, and the forced hub wins over either answer, since it is
714:     // the one the move has been told to pass through.
715:     let implicit_auto = forced_auto
716:       .or_else(|| Self::implicit_auto_hub_at(context, conversation, actor, source, target));
717:     let auto_hub = Self::auto_hub_at(context, conversation, target).or(implicit_auto);
718: 
719:     let destination = Self::resolve_auto(
720:       context,
721:       conversation,
722:       actor,
723:       target,
724:       implicit_auto,
725:       emulating,
726:     )?;
727:     moved.push(actor);
728: 
729:     // Auto hubs are entered but never announced, so the hub is remembered here
730:     // and only shown to the debug actors that are watching for it.
731:     if let Some(entered) = auto_hub {
732:       Self::announce_auto_to_debug(context, conversation, entered);
733:     }
734: 
735:     let motion = Motion {
736:       source_position: source,
737:       target_position: destination,
738:       choice_index: chosen,
739:       speaker: Self::apparent_speaker(context, conversation, actor, destination),
740:       background: false,
741:     };
742: 
743:     Self::apply_travel(
744:       context,
745:       conversation,
746:       actor,
747:       motion.clone(),
748:       kind,
749:       fake_channel,
750:       fake_priority,
751:     )?;
752: 
753:     // Everyone who follows this actor does so after it has settled, so that
754:     // the events for this move read in a sensible order.
755:     Self::warp_followers(context, conversation, actor, motion, kind, auto_hub, from_channel, moved);
756:     Ok(destination)
757:   }
758: 
759:   /// Commit a move: update the actor's state, then announce it.
760:   fn apply_travel(
761:     context: &mut LouderContext,
762:     conversation: LouderName,
763:     actor: LouderActor,
764:     motion: Motion,
765:     kind: MotionKind,
766:     fake_channel: LouderName,
767:     fake_priority: i32,
768:   ) -> Result<(), SimError> {
769:     // The hub is read out of the context once and kept here, so that the rest of
770:     // the commit does not have to re-find it while the context is being written
771:     // to, and so that pushing events cannot invalidate it.
772:     let hub = context
773:       .get_conversation(conversation)
774:       .and_then(|c| c.hub(motion.target_position))
775:       .cloned();
776: 
777:     let Some(hub) = hub else {
778:       return Err(SimError::Missing);
779:     };
780: 
781:     let channel = if fake_channel != LOUDER_NAME_NONE {
782:       fake_channel
783:     } else if hub.channel() != LOUDER_NAME_NONE {
784:       hub.channel()
785:     } else {
786:       context.main_channel()
787:     };
788: 
789:     let priority = if fake_priority != 0 {
790:       fake_priority
791:     } else {
792:       hub.priority()
793:     };
794: 
795:     let (channel_before, in_flow) = match context.get_actor(conversation, actor) {
796:       Some(entry) => (entry.channel(), entry.flags().in_flow()),
797:       None => (LOUDER_NAME_NONE, false),
798:     };
799: 
800:     // One borrow for the whole state update, rather than a fresh lookup per
801:     // field: the actor has to be written once, not four times.
802:     let channel_now = match context.get_actor_mut(conversation, actor) {
803:       Some(entry) => {
804:         let state = entry.state_mut();
805:         state.set_position(motion.target_position);
806:         if in_flow {
807:           if channel != channel_before {
808:             state.set_channel(channel);
809:           }
810:           state.set_priority(priority);
811:           channel
812:         } else {
813:           state.clear_flow();
814:           LOUDER_NAME_NONE
815:         }
816:       }
817:       None => return Err(SimError::Missing),
818:     };
819: 
820:     if in_flow && channel_now != channel_before {
821:       let event = LouderEvent {
822:         conversation,
823:         channel: channel_now,
824:         actor,
825:         data: EventData::ChannelChange,
826:       };
827:       context.push_event(event);
828:     }
829: 
830:     // Additional data belongs to the hub, so it replaces the actor's wholesale
831:     // rather than merging, which stops stale keys accumulating.
832:     if !hub.additional_data().is_empty() {
833:       let replacement = hub.additional_data().clone();
834:       let keys = hub.additional_data().sorted_keys();
835: 
836:       if let Some(entry) = context.get_actor_mut(conversation, actor) {
837:         entry.state_mut().replace_additional_data(replacement);
838:       }
839: 
840:       for key in keys {
841:         let event = LouderEvent {
842:           conversation,
843:           channel: channel_now,
844:           actor,
845:           data: EventData::AdditionalDataChange(AdditionalDataChange { key }),
846:         };
847:         context.push_event(event);
848:       }
849:     }
850: 
851:     Self::announce(context, conversation, actor, &motion, kind);
852: 
853:     // Conditions are reported on arrival. The visibility decision itself was
854:     // made by the pure predicate, so this is where the evaluation becomes
855:     // observable.
856:     Self::report_conditions(
857:       context,
858:       conversation,
859:       actor,
860:       channel_now,
861:       &motion,
862:       hub.expressions(),
863:     );
864: 
865:     // Statements and callbacks are one-shot: they run the first time a hub is
866:     // covered, not every time somebody passes through.
867:     let already_covered = context
868:       .get_conversation(conversation)
869:       .map(|c| c.is_covered(motion.target_position))
870:       .unwrap_or(true);
871: 
872:     if !already_covered {
873:       Self::run_first_visit(context, conversation, actor, channel_now, &motion, &hub);
874:       if let Some(c) = context.get_conversation_mut(conversation) {
875:         c.mark_covered(motion.target_position);
876:       }
877:     }
878: 
879:     Ok(())
880:   }
881: 
882:   /// Emit the hub appearance and the appearance of each visible choice.
883:   fn announce(
884:     context: &mut LouderContext,
885:     conversation: LouderName,
886:     actor: LouderActor,
887:     motion: &Motion,
888:     kind: MotionKind,
889:   ) {
890:     let channel = context
891:       .get_actor(conversation, actor)
892:       .map(|entry| entry.channel())
893:       .unwrap_or(LOUDER_NAME_NONE);
894: 
895:     let event = LouderEvent {
896:       conversation,
897:       channel,
898:       actor,
899:       data: kind.data(motion.clone()),
900:     };
901:     context.push_event(event);
902: 
903:     for index in Self::available_choices(context, conversation, actor, motion.target_position) {
904:       let appearance = ChoiceAppearance {
905:         position: motion.target_position,
906:         choice_index: index as i32,
907:       };
908:       let event = LouderEvent {
909:         conversation,
910:         channel,
911:         actor,
912:         data: EventData::ChoiceAppearance(appearance),
913:       };
914:       context.push_event(event);
915:     }
916:   }
917: 
918:   /// Run a hub's statements and callbacks, the first time it is covered.
919:   fn run_first_visit(
920:     context: &mut LouderContext,
921:     conversation: LouderName,
922:     actor: LouderActor,
923:     channel: LouderName,
924:     motion: &Motion,
925:     hub: &Hub,
926:   ) {
927:     let mut scope = EvaluationScope::new(conversation, actor, channel);
928:     scope.hub = motion.target_position;
929:     scope.motion = Some(motion.clone());
930:     let evaluator = Evaluator::new(scope);
931: 
932:     // The source text is copied out because evaluating it needs mutable access to
933:     // the same context that holds it.
934:     let sources: Vec<String> = hub
935:       .expressions()
936:       .iter()
937:       .filter_map(|expression| context.resolve_name(*expression).map(|s| s.to_string()))
938:       .collect();
939: 
940:     for source in sources {
941:       // Only statements run on arrival; conditions were already checked to
942:       // decide whether the hub could appear at all.
943:       if super::expression::classify(&source) != super::expression::ExpressionKind::Statement {
944:         continue;
945:       }
946: 
947:       let _ = evaluator.evaluate(context, &source);
948:     }
949: 
950:     for callback in hub.callbacks() {
951:       let event = LouderEvent {
952:         conversation,
953:         channel,
954:         actor,
955:         data: EventData::Callback(*callback),
956:       };
957:       context.push_event(event);
958:     }
959:   }
960: 
961:   /// The auto hub at `position`, if that hub is one.
962:   ///
963:   /// A move that starts here is resolved by auto logic rather than announced, so
964:   /// the hub itself is never where an actor comes to rest. It still matters: it
965:   /// is what a follower is warped through when it emulates the leader.
966:   fn auto_hub_at(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, position: LouderPosition) -> Option<LouderPosition> {
967:     context
968:       .get_conversation(conversation)
969:       .and_then(|c| c.hub(position))
970:       .filter(|hub| hub.is_auto())
971:       .map(|_| position)
972:   }
973: 
974:   /// Whether `position` behaves as an auto hub for `actor` arriving there from
975:   /// `from`, by the singleplayer rule.
976:   ///
977:   /// Both of the rule's conditions have to hold, and both are about the arrival
978:   /// rather than about the hub alone: `from` branched, and `position` shows to
979:   /// one actor and to nobody else. That is the shape of a consequence of a
980:   /// choice rather than a choice of its own, so it resolves instead of being
981:   /// shown, because there was no other reading available to anyone.
982:   ///
983:   /// How many choices the hub has is deliberately not part of the question. A
984:   /// hub only its own actor can see is not a decision between those choices so
985:   /// much as the outcome of the one that was just made, and a document that
986:   /// routes a private branch through a public one depends on it resolving.
987:   ///
988:   /// Where the actor came from is what keeps the rule from eating the whole
989:   /// conversation, since a hub behind another with a single choice is the same
990:   /// decision as the one before it rather than a new one.
991:   fn implicit_auto_hub_at(
992:     context: &LouderContext,
993:     conversation: LouderName,
994:     actor: LouderActor,
995:     from: LouderPosition,
996:     position: LouderPosition,
997:   ) -> Option<LouderPosition> {
998:     if !context.rules().contains(DialogueRules::SINGLEPLAYER) {
999:       return None;
1000:     }
1001: 
1002:     let conversation_ref = context.get_conversation(conversation)?;
1003: 
1004:     // A hub flagged Auto already resolves on its own, whatever else it is.
1005:     if conversation_ref.hub(position)?.is_auto() {
1006:       return None;
1007:     }
1008: 
1009:     // Reached from somewhere that branched. Without this the rule would resolve
1010:     // every hub an actor walks into on its own, and a conversation would run
1011:     // itself out with no one able to say anything along the way.
1012:     if conversation_ref.hub(from)?.choice_count() < 2 {
1013:       return None;
1014:     }
1015: 
1016:     // Private to this actor. A hub somebody else could see is one they may be
1017:     // waiting on, and deciding it on their behalf is not a default to make.
1018:     if !Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, actor, position) {
1019:       return None;
1020:     }
1021: 
1022:     let seen_by_others = context.actors(conversation).iter().any(|entry| {
1023:       // Only actors in the flow are deciding anything, so a hub showing to a
1024:       // spectator or a debug actor does not make it a shared one.
1025:       entry.id() != actor
1026:         && entry.flags().in_flow()
1027:         && Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, entry.id(), position)
1028:     });
1029:     if seen_by_others {
1030:       return None;
1031:     }
1032: 
1033:     // The hub must be spoken by the actor that made the choice.
1034:     let hub = conversation_ref.hub(position)?;
1035:     let speak_as: Vec<LouderName> = context
1036:       .get_actor(conversation, actor)
1037:       .map(|entry| entry.speak_as().to_vec())
1038:       .unwrap_or_default();
1039:     let hub_speaker = hub.speaker_for(&speak_as);
1040:     if hub_speaker == LOUDER_NAME_NONE || !speak_as.contains(&hub_speaker) {
1041:       return None;
1042:     }
1043: 
1044:     Some(position)
1045:   }
1046: 
1047:   /// Pull other actors along after `leader` has made a choice.
1048:   ///
1049:   /// An actor follows when it shares a channel with the leader, the hub the
1050:   /// leader landed on could appear to it, and its priority does not exceed the
1051:   /// leader's.
1052:   ///
1053:   /// The shared channel is the one the leader was *leaving*, not the one it
1054:   /// arrived in. A leader that walks into a hub carrying a channel drags along
1055:   /// whoever was with it where it came from, and the follower stays in the old
1056:   /// channel; that divergence is the point of the guide's worked example.
1057:   ///
1058:   /// Where the follower lands depends on whether the leader passed through auto
1059:   /// logic on the way, named by `auto_hub`:
1060:   ///  - If it did, the follower is warped to that same auto hub and resolves it
1061:   ///    as itself, taking the first branch available to *it*. This is what lets
1062:   ///    two actors leaving one hub end up in different places, and it does not
1063:   ///    depend on the auto hub being available to the follower, since appearing
1064:   ///    is not the thing being emulated. Where no branch is available to the
1065:   ///    follower, it takes the first branch that appears to it, and if none
1066:   ///    appears either, it goes where the leader went.
1067:   ///  - Otherwise, when the leader moved by choosing, the follower replays the
1068:   ///    same choice from wherever it was standing.
1069:   ///  - Otherwise the follower simply goes where the leader went.
1070:   #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1071:   fn warp_followers(
1072:     context: &mut LouderContext,
1073:     conversation: LouderName,
1074:     actor: LouderActor,
1075:     motion: Motion,
1076:     kind: MotionKind,
1077:     auto_hub: Option<LouderPosition>,
1078:     from_channel: LouderName,
1079:     moved: &mut Vec<LouderActor>,
1080:   ) {
1081:     let (leader_priority, leader_flags) = match context.get_actor(conversation, actor) {
1082:       Some(entry) => (entry.priority(), entry.flags()),
1083:       None => return,
1084:     };
1085: 
1086:     // Actors outside the flow neither follow nor pull others along.
1087:     if !leader_flags.in_flow() {
1088:       return;
1089:     }
1090: 
1091:     // Who shared this actor's channel, as of before the move. Collected up
1092:     // front because the loop below moves them and would otherwise be reading
1093:     // the conversation while writing to it.
1094:     let followers: Vec<(LouderActor, LouderPosition, i32)> = context
1095:       .actors(conversation)
1096:       .iter()
1097:       .filter(|entry| entry.id() != actor)
1098:       .filter(|entry| entry.flags().in_flow())
1099:       .filter(|entry| entry.channel() == from_channel)
1100:       .map(|entry| (entry.id(), entry.position(), entry.priority()))
1101:       .collect();
1102: 
1103:     for (follower, from, priority) in followers {
1104:       // Anyone this cascade has already placed is where they belong, and
1105:       // following them again is what would otherwise ping-pong between two
1106:       // actors sharing a channel.
1107:       if moved.contains(&follower) {
1108:         continue;
1109:       }
1110: 
1111:       if priority > leader_priority {
1112:         // Too important to be dragged along, but it still sees the hub in the
1113:         // background, without choices.
1114:         if Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, follower, motion.target_position) {
1115:           let background = Motion {
1116:             source_position: from,
1117:             target_position: motion.target_position,
1118:             choice_index: Self::NO_CHOICE,
1119:             speaker: Self::hub_speaker_for(context, conversation, follower, motion.target_position),
1120:             background: true,
1121:           };
1122:           let event = LouderEvent {
1123:             conversation,
1124:             // The follower did not move, so it is still in the channel it shared
1125:             // with the leader.
1126:             channel: from_channel,
1127:             actor: follower,
1128:             data: EventData::RegularMotion(background),
1129:           };
1130:           context.push_event(event);
1131:         }
1132:         continue;
1133:       }
1134: 
1135:       // Leaving an auto hub is what makes the follower emulate rather than
1136:       // replay: the leader resolved that hub as itself, and the follower gets
1137:       // to resolve it as itself too.
1138:       //
1139:       // What decides whether this follower comes along is its own landing, not
1140:       // the leader's. The leader may have ended up somewhere the follower
1141:       // cannot hear at all, and that is precisely the case where diverging
1142:       // matters, so gating on the leader's landing would skip exactly the
1143:       // followers emulation exists for.
1144:       if let Some(hub) = auto_hub {
1145:         // Emulation can still come up empty, when no branch of the auto hub leads
1146:         // anywhere this follower can hear. There is nothing for it to emulate in
1147:         // that case, so it goes where the leader went rather than being left
1148:         // behind.
1149:         let destination = if Self::resolve_auto(context, conversation, follower, hub, Some(hub), true)
1150:           .is_ok()
1151:         {
1152:           hub
1153:         } else if Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, follower, motion.target_position) {
1154:           motion.target_position
1155:         } else {
1156:           continue;
1157:         };
1158: 
1159:         Self::drag_along(context, conversation, follower, destination, Some(hub), moved);
1160:         continue;
1161:       }
1162: 
1163:       if !Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, follower, motion.target_position) {
1164:         continue;
1165:       }
1166: 
1167:       let destination = if kind == MotionKind::Regular && motion.choice_index >= 0 {
1168:         Self::replayed_destination(context, conversation, follower, from, &motion)
1169:       } else {
1170:         motion.target_position
1171:       };
1172: 
1173:       Self::drag_along(context, conversation, follower, destination, None, moved);
1174:     }
1175:   }
1176: 
1177:   /// Where a follower ends up when it replays the leader's choice.
1178:   ///
1179:   /// The follower prefers the choice the leader took, and falls back to whatever
1180:   /// it can actually see from where it stands, which is what lets the two end up
1181:   /// on different branches of the same hub.
1182:   fn replayed_destination(
1183:     context: &LouderContext,
1184:     conversation: LouderName,
1185:     follower: LouderActor,
1186:     from: LouderPosition,
1187:     motion: &Motion,
1188:   ) -> LouderPosition {
1189:     let Some(hub) = context.get_conversation(conversation).and_then(|c| c.hub(from)) else {
1190:       return motion.target_position;
1191:     };
1192: 
1193:     let replay = Self::available_choices(context, conversation, follower, from);
1194:     let leader_choice = motion.choice_index as usize;
1195:     let index = match replay.iter().find(|index| **index == leader_choice) {
1196:       Some(index) => *index,
1197:       None => match replay.first() {
1198:         Some(index) => *index,
1199:         None => return motion.target_position,
1200:       },
1201:     };
1202: 
1203:     hub.choice(index)
1204:       .map(|choice| choice.position())
1205:       .unwrap_or(motion.target_position)
1206:   }
1207: 
1208:   /// Warp a follower to `destination`, leaving it where it is if it cannot be
1209:   /// placed there.
1210:   ///
1211:   /// The failure is swallowed on purpose: the leader's move has already been
1212:   /// applied, so reporting a refusal now would hand the host a refusal for
1213:   /// something that has already happened.
1214:   ///
1215:   /// `forced_auto` is the hub the follower is being carried through, if any. It
1216:   /// is passed on rather than recomputed because the follower is arriving at
1217:   /// that hub by emulation rather than by a choice, and the rule it would
1218:   /// otherwise be judged by is about the choice.
1219:   fn drag_along(
1220:     context: &mut LouderContext,
1221:     conversation: LouderName,
1222:     follower: LouderActor,
1223:     destination: LouderPosition,
1224:     forced_auto: Option<LouderPosition>,
1225:     moved: &mut Vec<LouderActor>,
1226:   ) {
1227:     let _ = Self::travel_within(
1228:       context,
1229:       conversation,
1230:       follower,
1231:       destination,
1232:       MotionKind::Warp,
1233:       LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
1234:       0,
1235:       forced_auto,
1236:       true,
1237:       Self::NO_CHOICE,
1238:       moved,
1239:     );
1240:   }
1241: 
1242:   /// Select `choice_index` for `actor`, moving it to the linked hub.
1243:   pub fn select_choice(
1244:     context: &mut LouderContext,
1245:     conversation: LouderName,
1246:     actor: LouderActor,
1247:     choice_index: i32,
1248:   ) -> Result<LouderPosition, SimError> {
1249:     if choice_index < 0 {
1250:       return Err(SimError::NoSuchChoice);
1251:     }
1252: 
1253:     let position = context
1254:       .get_actor(conversation, actor)
1255:       .map(|entry| entry.position())
1256:       .ok_or(SimError::Missing)?;
1257: 
1258:     let choice_index = choice_index as usize;
1259:     if Self::choice_available(context, conversation, actor, position, choice_index) != ChoiceAvailableError::NoError {
1260:       return Err(SimError::NoSuchChoice);
1261:     }
1262: 
1263:     let destination = context
1264:       .get_conversation(conversation)
1265:       .and_then(|c| c.hub(position))
1266:       .and_then(|hub| hub.choice(choice_index))
1267:       .map(|choice| choice.position())
1268:       .ok_or(SimError::NoSuchChoice)?;
1269: 
1270:     // Choosing is an ordinary move whose followers get to replay the choice, so
1271:     // the index travels with the move rather than being applied here.
1272:     Self::travel_within(
1273:       context,
1274:       conversation,
1275:       actor,
1276:       destination,
1277:       MotionKind::Regular,
1278:       LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
1279:       0,
1280:       None,
1281:       false,
1282:       choice_index as i32,
1283:       &mut Vec::new(),
1284:     )
1285:   }
1286: 
1287:   /// Follow auto hubs from `destination` without emitting anything.
1288:   ///
1289:   /// Auto hubs resolve by taking the first choice available to the actor, so a
1290:   /// document that links two auto hubs to each other would walk that pair
1291:   /// forever. Each hub is therefore walked at most once, and arriving back at
1292:   /// a hub already walked is reported as a dead end, which is what an auto hub
1293:   /// with no way out amounts to.
1294:   ///
1295:   /// `implicit_auto` names a hub at `destination` that is auto for this actor
1296:   /// without being flagged, which is what the singleplayer rule produces and
1297:   /// what a follower emulating a leader's auto hub is told to walk through.
1298:   ///
1299:   /// It applies to that one hub and no further. The hubs behind it were reached
1300:   /// by auto logic rather than by a choice, so no choice was made there and
1301:   /// there is nothing for the rule to be about; treating them as consequences of
1302:   /// a choice too would walk an actor off the end of any private branch that
1303:   /// ends somewhere they cannot hear.
1304:   ///
1305:   /// `emulating` marks the actor as a follower walking through a hub the leader
1306:   /// left through, which is what the guide calls emulation. A follower is not
1307:   /// deciding anything, so it may have no claim on any branch and still be
1308:   /// entitled to an outcome: when no choice is available to it, the first choice
1309:   /// whose linked hub would appear to it is taken instead. Only then is it a
1310:   /// dead end. An actor that picked its way here gets no such fallback, since a
1311:   /// hub it cannot leave through really is a dead end for it.
1312:   fn resolve_auto(
1313:     context: &LouderContext,
1314:     conversation: LouderName,
1315:     actor: LouderActor,
1316:     destination: LouderPosition,
1317:     implicit_auto: Option<LouderPosition>,
1318:     emulating: bool,
1319:   ) -> Result<LouderPosition, SimError> {
1320:     let mut current = destination;
1321:     let mut walked: Vec<LouderPosition> = Vec::new();
1322: 
1323:     loop {
1324:       let is_auto = context
1325:         .get_conversation(conversation)
1326:         .and_then(|c| c.hub(current))
1327:         .map(|hub| hub.is_auto() || implicit_auto == Some(current))
1328:         .unwrap_or(false);
1329: 
1330:       if !is_auto {
1331:         return Ok(current);
1332:       }
1333: 
1334:       if walked.contains(&current) {
1335:         return Err(SimError::DeadEnd);
1336:       }
1337:       walked.push(current);
1338: 
1339:       let available = Self::available_choices(context, conversation, actor, current);
1340:       let next = match available.first() {
1341:         Some(next) => *next,
1342:         None => match Self::first_appearing_choice(context, conversation, actor, current) {
1343:           Some(next) if emulating => next,
1344:           _ => return Err(SimError::DeadEnd),
1345:         },
1346:       };
1347: 
1348:       current = context
1349:         .get_conversation(conversation)
1350:         .and_then(|c| c.hub(current))
1351:         .and_then(|hub| hub.choice(next))
1352:         .map(|choice| choice.position())
1353:         .ok_or(SimError::NoSuchChoice)?;
1354:     }
1355:   }
1356: 
1357:   /// The first choice on `hub_position` leading somewhere that appears to
1358:   /// `actor`.
1359:   ///
1360:   /// This is the emulation fallback, and the reason it can exist is that a
1361:   /// follower is not deciding anything: it is being carried through the auto hub
1362:   /// the leader passed, and where it lands only has to be somewhere it can
1363:   /// hear. Every branch the leader could take is the leader's to take, so the
1364:   /// follower is left with branches it is shown but not given, and taking the
1365:   /// first of those is what lets the two actors end up apart instead of one of
1366:   /// them being left standing.
1367:   fn first_appearing_choice(
1368:     context: &LouderContext,
1369:     conversation: LouderName,
1370:     actor: LouderActor,
1371:     hub_position: LouderPosition,
1372:   ) -> Option<usize> {
1373:     let hub = context
1374:       .get_conversation(conversation)
1375:       .and_then(|conversation_ref| conversation_ref.hub(hub_position))?;
1376: 
1377:     (0..hub.choice_count()).find(|index| {
1378:       hub.choice(*index)
1379:         .is_some_and(|choice| Self::hub_appears(context, conversation, actor, choice.position()))
1380:     })
1381:   }

5: /// Where a story state variable is stored, decided by its name.
6: ///
7: /// Two independent rules combine into the four cases of the engine guide: a
8: /// leading underscore means "not saved", and an uppercase first letter means
9: /// "shared across conversations".
10: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
11: pub enum VariableScope {
12:   /// Saved, and private to the conversation it was written in.
13:   LocalSaved,
14:   /// Saved, and visible to every conversation.
15:   SharedSaved,
16:   /// Discarded on save, and private to the conversation.
17:   LocalUnsaved,
18:   /// Discarded on save, and visible to every conversation.
19:   SharedUnsaved,
20: }
21: 
22: /// Classify a variable by the naming rules of the engine guide.
23: ///
24: /// `_a` and `__1` are local and unsaved, `_A` and `__Shared` are shared and
25: /// unsaved, `a` is local and saved, and `A` is shared and saved. A name with
26: /// no letters at all falls back to local and saved.
27: pub fn classify_variable(name: &str) -> VariableScope {
28:   let significant = name.trim_start_matches('_');
29:   let shared = significant
30:     .chars()
31:     .next()
32:     .is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_uppercase());
33:   let saved = !name.starts_with('_');
34: 
35:   match (shared, saved) {
36:     (false, true) => VariableScope::LocalSaved,
37:     (true, true) => VariableScope::SharedSaved,
38:     (false, false) => VariableScope::LocalUnsaved,
39:     (true, false) => VariableScope::SharedUnsaved,
40:   }
41: }
42: 
43: /// A variable bucket: one of the four scopes, holding name/variant pairs.
44: pub type VariableMap = Map<LouderName, LouderVariant>;
45: 
46: /// The contents of one loaded conversation: its hubs, and the story state that
47: /// is private to it.
48: ///
49: /// Story state that is shared across conversations is held by the
50: /// [`LouderContext`](super::context::LouderContext) instead, so that loading
51: /// and unloading conversations does not disturb it.
52: ///
53: /// A loaded conversation is handed out as a copy; what the engine holds
54: /// changes through the context.
55: #[derive(Clone, Default)]
56: pub struct Conversation {
57:   name: LouderName,
58:   hubs: Map<LouderPosition, Hub>,
59: 
60:   local_saved: VariableMap,
61:   local_unsaved: VariableMap,
62: 
63:   covered: Set<LouderPosition>,
64: }
65:

72:       local_unsaved: Map::new(),

74:     }
75:   }

79:   }

108:     match scope {

111:       _ => unreachable!("shared story state is owned by the context"),

1: //! The rules a host turns on and off.
2: //!
3: //! The rules themselves are a set of bits. A host reads and writes the whole
4: //! configuration at once through
5: //! [`LouderContext::rules`](super::context::LouderContext) and
6: //! [`LouderContext::set_rules`](super::context::LouderContext), and gets the
7: //! names to hand it from [`DialogueRules::from_name`] and
8: //! [`DialogueRules::names`] rather than hard-coding the bits.
9: 
10: bitflags::bitflags! {
11:   /// The dialogue rules a host can turn on and off.
12:   ///
13:   /// Both are on by default, because each is what makes the engine usable
14:   /// without every conversation having to be written around its absence. They
15:   /// are gathered here rather than left as separate switches so that a host
16:   /// reads and writes the whole configuration in one call, and so that adding a
17:   /// rule does not add another pair of engine calls.
18:   #[repr(C)]
19:   #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
20:   pub struct DialogueRules: i32 {
21:     /// The spoke rule. A lone choice on a hub may be taken by an actor who
22:     /// neither speaks nor hears the voice behind it, since there is nobody else
23:     /// to hand it to and no branch for it to be lost on. Without it, linear
24:     /// dialogue hands the lead back and forth between every actor instead of
25:     /// letting one of them carry it.
26:     const SPOKE = 0b00000001;
27: 
28:     /// The singleplayer rule. A hub that only the actor who just chose can see,
29:     /// reached from a hub that had several choices, resolves on its own instead
30:     /// of being shown. How many choices it carries is beside the point: it is
31:     /// the consequence of a choice rather than one, so there is nothing in it
32:     /// for that actor to decide. This is the "continue" of a more traditional
33:     /// singleplayer dialogue system, made implicit, so that a private branch
34:     /// can be routed through without being written as a decision.
35:     const SINGLEPLAYER = 0b00000010;
36:   }
37: }
38: 
39: impl DialogueRules {
40:   /// The name of each rule set, in declaration order.
41:   ///
42:   /// [`DialogueRules::from_name`] is the way back the other way, and returns
43:   /// `None` for a name this build does not know, so a host carrying a
44:   /// configuration written for a newer engine is not stopped by one.
45:   pub fn names(&self) -> Vec<&'static str> {
46:     let mut names: Vec<&'static str> = Vec::new();
47:     if self.contains(DialogueRules::SPOKE) {
48:       names.push("SPOKE");
49:     }
50:     if self.contains(DialogueRules::SINGLEPLAYER) {
51:       names.push("SINGLEPLAYER");
52:     }
53:     names
54:   }
55: }

109: /// Multi-character operators, longest first so that `<=` wins over `<`.
110: const OPERATORS: &[&str] = &[
111:   "<<=", ">>=", "&&", "||", "==", "!=", "<=", ">=", "<<", ">>", "++", "--", "+=", "-=", "*=", "/=",
112:   "%=", "&=", "|=", "^=", "+", "-", "*", "/", "%", "=", "<", ">", "!", "&", "|", "^", "(", ")", ",",
113: ];

115: pub fn tokenize(source: &str) -> Result<Vec<Token>, ParseError> {
116:   let bytes: Vec<char> = source.chars().collect();
117:   let mut tokens = Vec::new();
118:   let mut i = 0;
119: 
120:   while i < bytes.len() {
121:     let c = bytes[i];
122: 
123:     if c.is_whitespace() {
124:       i += 1;
125:       continue;
126:     }
127: 
128:     // A `$` immediately followed by `(` opens an ask.
129:     if c == '$' {
130:       if i + 1 < bytes.len() && bytes[i + 1] == '(' {
131:         tokens.push(Token::Ask);
132:         i += 2;
133:         continue;
134:       }
135:       return Err(ParseError::new("expected `(` after `$`", i));
136:     }
137: 
138:     if c.is_ascii_digit() {
139:       let start = i;
140:       let mut is_float = false;
141:       while i < bytes.len() && (bytes[i].is_ascii_digit() || bytes[i] == '.') {
142:         if bytes[i] == '.' {
143:           // Only treat `.` as part of the number when a digit follows, so that
144:           // `1.foo` does not silently become a float.
145:           if is_float || i + 1 >= bytes.len() || !bytes[i + 1].is_ascii_digit() {
146:             break;
147:           }
148:           is_float = true;
149:         }
150:         i += 1;
151:       }
152: 
153:       let text: String = bytes[start..i].iter().collect();
154:       if is_float {
155:         let value = text.parse::<f32>().map_err(|_| ParseError::new("malformed float", start))?;
156:         tokens.push(Token::Float(value));
157:       } else {
158:         // Values that do not fit an i32 fall back to a float rather than
159:         // failing the whole expression.
160:         match text.parse::<i32>() {
161:           Ok(value) => tokens.push(Token::Int(value)),
162:           Err(_) => {
163:             let value = text.parse::<f32>().map_err(|_| ParseError::new("malformed number", start))?;
164:             tokens.push(Token::Float(value));
165:           }
166:         }
167:       }
168:       continue;
169:     }
170: 
171:     if c == '_' || c.is_alphabetic() {
172:       let start = i;
173:       while i < bytes.len() && (bytes[i] == '_' || bytes[i].is_alphanumeric()) {
174:         i += 1;
175:       }
176:       tokens.push(Token::Ident(bytes[start..i].iter().collect()));
177:       continue;
178:     }
179: 
180:     if c == '"' || c == '\'' {
181:       let quote = c;
182:       i += 1;
183:       let mut value = String::new();
184:       loop {
185:         if i >= bytes.len() {
186:           return Err(ParseError::new("unterminated string", start_of(&bytes, i)));
187:         }
188:         if bytes[i] == '\\' && i + 1 < bytes.len() {
189:           let escaped = bytes[i + 1];
190:           value.push(match escaped {
191:             'n' => '\n',
192:             't' => '\t',
193:             'r' => '\r',
194:             other => other,
195:           });
196:           i += 2;
197:           continue;
198:         }
199:         if bytes[i] == quote {
200:           i += 1;
201:           break;
202:         }
203:         value.push(bytes[i]);
204:         i += 1;
205:       }
206:       tokens.push(Token::Str(value));
207:       continue;
208:     }
209: 
210:     // A `%` that follows an operand is the modulo operator. One that does not
211:     // opens a special variable, which is how an expression can ask the engine
212:     // about itself without the two spellings colliding. A compound assignment
213:     // is a modulo that happens to be followed by an `=`, so it is left to the
214:     // operator table below.
215:     if c == '%' && !ends_operand(tokens.last()) {
216:       let start = i;
217:       i += 1;
218:       if i >= bytes.len() || !(bytes[i] == '_' || bytes[i].is_alphabetic()) {
219:         return Err(ParseError::new("expected a variable name after `%`", start));
220:       }
221:       let name_start = i;
222:       while i < bytes.len() && (bytes[i] == '_' || bytes[i].is_alphanumeric()) {
223:         i += 1;
224:       }
225:       let name: String = bytes[name_start..i].iter().collect();
226:       tokens.push(Token::Special(format!("%{name}")));
227:       continue;
228:     }
229: 
230:     let rest: String = bytes[i..].iter().collect();
231:     match OPERATORS.iter().find(|op| rest.starts_with(**op)) {
232:       Some(op) => {
233:         tokens.push(Token::Op(op));
234:         i += op.chars().count();
235:       }
236:       None => return Err(ParseError::new(format!("unexpected character `{c}`"), i)),
237:     }
238:   }
239: 
240:   tokens.push(Token::Eof);
241:   Ok(tokens)
242: }
243: 
244: fn start_of(bytes: &[char], i: usize) -> usize {
245:   if i >= bytes.len() {
246:     bytes.len()
247:   } else {
248:     i
249:   }
250: }

252: /// Whether a token ends an operand, and so can be followed by an operator.
253: ///
254: /// This is what tells a `%` that opens a special variable from one that is the
255: /// modulo operator: the operator follows an operand, and a special variable
256: /// does not.
257: fn ends_operand(token: Option<&Token>) -> bool {
258:   matches!(
259:     token,
260:     Some(Token::Ident(_))
261:       | Some(Token::Int(_))
262:       | Some(Token::Float(_))
263:       | Some(Token::Str(_))
264:       | Some(Token::Special(_))
265:       | Some(Token::Op(")"))
266:   )
267: }
