269: struct Parser {
270:   tokens: Vec<Token>,
271:   position: usize,
272: }
273: 
274: impl Parser {
275:   fn peek(&self) -> &Token {
276:     self.tokens.get(self.position).unwrap_or(&Token::Eof)
277:   }
278: 
279:   fn next(&mut self) -> Token {
280:     let token = self.peek().clone();
281:     self.position += 1;
282:     token
283:   }
284: 
285:   fn eat_op(&mut self, op: &str) -> bool {
286:     if matches!(self.peek(), Token::Op(found) if *found == op) {
287:       self.position += 1;
288:       return true;
289:     }
290:     false
291:   }
292: 
293:   fn expect_op(&mut self, op: &str) -> Result<(), ParseError> {
294:     if self.eat_op(op) {
295:       return Ok(());
296:     }
297:     Err(ParseError::new(format!("expected `{op}`"), self.position))
298:   }
299: 
300:   fn parse_expression(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
301:     self.parse_binary(0)
302:   }
303: 
304:   /// Precedence climbing. Each level lists its operators from loosest to
305:   /// tightest binding.
306:   fn parse_binary(&mut self, level: usize) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
307:     const LEVELS: &[&[(&str, BinOp)]] = &[
308:       &[("||", BinOp::Or)],
309:       &[("&&", BinOp::And)],
310:       &[("|", BinOp::BitOr)],
311:       &[("^", BinOp::BitXor)],
312:       &[("&", BinOp::BitAnd)],
313:       &[("==", BinOp::Eq), ("!=", BinOp::Ne)],
314:       &[("<=", BinOp::Le), (">=", BinOp::Ge), ("<", BinOp::Lt), (">", BinOp::Gt)],
315:       &[("<<", BinOp::Shl), (">>", BinOp::Shr)],
316:       &[("+", BinOp::Add), ("-", BinOp::Sub)],
317:       &[("*", BinOp::Mul), ("/", BinOp::Div), ("%", BinOp::Mod)],
318:     ];
319: 
320:     if level >= LEVELS.len() {
321:       return self.parse_unary();
322:     }
323: 
324:     let mut left = self.parse_binary(level + 1)?;
325:     loop {
326:       let op = match self.peek() {
327:         Token::Op(found) => *found,
328:         _ => break,
329:       };
330: 
331:       // Match the operator against this level, longest first within the level.
332:       let matched = LEVELS[level]
333:         .iter()
334:         .find(|(candidate, _)| *candidate == op)
335:         .map(|(_, bin)| *bin);
336: 
337:       let Some(bin) = matched else { break };
338:       self.position += 1;
339:       let right = self.parse_binary(level + 1)?;
340:       left = Expr::Binary(bin, Box::new(left), Box::new(right));
341:     }
342: 
343:     Ok(left)
344:   }
345: 
346:   fn parse_unary(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
347:     if self.eat_op("!") {
348:       return Ok(Expr::Not(Box::new(self.parse_unary()?)));
349:     }
350: 
351:     if self.eat_op("-") {
352:       return Ok(Expr::Neg(Box::new(self.parse_unary()?)));
353:     }
354: 
355:     if self.eat_op("++") {
356:       if let Token::Ident(name) | Token::Special(name) = self.next() {
357:         return Ok(Expr::PreInc(name, 1));
358:       }
359:       return Err(ParseError::new("`++` needs a variable", self.position));
360:     }
361: 
362:     if self.eat_op("--") {
363:       if let Token::Ident(name) | Token::Special(name) = self.next() {
364:         return Ok(Expr::PreInc(name, -1));
365:       }
366:       return Err(ParseError::new("`--` needs a variable", self.position));
367:     }
368: 
369:     self.parse_postfix()
370:   }
371: 
372:   fn parse_postfix(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
373:     let mut expr = self.parse_primary()?;
374: 
375:     loop {
376:       if let Token::Ident(name) = self.peek().clone() {
377:         if (name == "true" || name == "false") && !matches!(expr, Expr::Var(_)) {
378:           self.position += 1;
379:           expr = Expr::Bool(name == "true");
380:           continue;
381:         }
382:       }
383: 
384:       if self.eat_op("++") {
385:         if let Expr::Var(name) | Expr::Special(name) = expr {
386:           expr = Expr::PostInc(name, 1);
387:           continue;
388:         }
389:         return Err(ParseError::new("`++` needs a variable", self.position));
390:       }
391: 
392:       if self.eat_op("--") {
393:         if let Expr::Var(name) | Expr::Special(name) = expr {
394:           expr = Expr::PostInc(name, -1);
395:           continue;
396:         }
397:         return Err(ParseError::new("`--` needs a variable", self.position));
398:       }
399: 
400:       break;
401:     }
402: 
403:     Ok(expr)
404:   }
405: 
406:   fn parse_primary(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
407:     match self.next() {
408:       Token::Int(value) => Ok(Expr::Int(value)),
409:       Token::Float(value) => Ok(Expr::Float(value)),
410:       Token::Str(value) => Ok(Expr::Str(value)),
411:       Token::Ask => {
412:         let mut args = Vec::new();
413:         if !self.eat_op(")") {
414:           loop {
415:             args.push(self.parse_expression()?);
416:             if self.eat_op(",") {
417:               continue;
418:             }
419:             self.expect_op(")")?;
420:             break;
421:           }
422:         }
423:         Ok(Expr::Ask(args))
424:       }
425:       Token::Ident(name) => {
426:         if name == "true" || name == "false" {
427:           return Ok(Expr::Bool(name == "true"));
428:         }
429: 
430:         // A leading identifier may turn out to be a compound assignment.
431:         if let Token::Op(op) = self.peek().clone() {
432:           if matches!(op, "=" | "+=" | "-=" | "*=" | "/=" | "%=") {
433:             self.position += 1;
434:             let value = self.parse_expression()?;
435:             return Ok(Expr::Assign(name, Box::new(value)));
436:           }
437:         }
438: 
439:         if self.eat_op("(") {
440:           let mut args = Vec::new();
441:           if !self.eat_op(")") {
442:             loop {
443:               args.push(self.parse_expression()?);
444:               if self.eat_op(",") {
445:                 continue;
446:               }
447:               self.expect_op(")")?;
448:               break;
449:             }
450:           }
451:           return Ok(Expr::Call(name, args));
452:         }
453: 
454:         Ok(Expr::Var(name))
455:       }
456:       Token::Special(name) => {
457:         // A special variable may turn out to be a write to it.
458:         if let Token::Op(op) = self.peek().clone()
459:           && matches!(op, "=" | "+=" | "-=" | "*=" | "/=" | "%=")
460:         {
461:           self.position += 1;
462:           let value = self.parse_expression()?;
463:           return Ok(Expr::Assign(name, Box::new(value)));
464:         }
465: 
466:         Ok(Expr::Special(name))
467:       }
468:       Token::Op("(") => {
469:         let inner = self.parse_expression()?;
470:         self.expect_op(")")?;
471:         Ok(inner)
472:       }
473:       Token::Eof => Err(ParseError::new("expression ended early", self.position)),
474:       other => Err(ParseError::new(format!("unexpected token {other:?}"), self.position)),
475:     }
476:   }
477: }
478: 
479: pub fn parse(source: &str) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
480:   let tokens = tokenize(source)?;
481:   let mut parser = Parser {
482:     tokens,
483:     position: 0,
484:   };
485:   let expr = parser.parse_expression()?;
486:   if parser.peek() != &Token::Eof {
487:     return Err(ParseError::new("trailing tokens after expression", parser.position));
488:   }
489:   Ok(expr)
490: }
491: 
492: /// Whether this expression only reads story state.
493: ///
494: /// This is the distinction the engine guide draws between conditions and
495: /// statements: a condition is re-run every time a hub is checked, while a
496: /// statement runs once when the hub is first covered.
497: pub fn reads_only(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
498:   !matches!(expr, Expr::Assign(..) | Expr::PreInc(..) | Expr::PostInc(..))
499: }
500: 
501: /// Which operations an expression performs.
502: ///
503: /// See [`reads_only`], which is the same question asked of an expression that
504: /// has already been parsed. An expression that does not parse is reported as a
505: /// condition, so that whatever is asking falls back on its own rule for an
506: /// expression it cannot read.
507: pub fn classify(source: &str) -> ExpressionKind {
508:   match parse(source) {
509:     Ok(expr) if reads_only(&expr) => ExpressionKind::Condition,
510:     Ok(_) => ExpressionKind::Statement,
511:     Err(_) => ExpressionKind::Condition,
512:   }
513: }
514: 
515: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
516: pub enum ExpressionKind {
517:   Condition,
518:   Statement,
519: }
520: 
521: /// What happened while evaluating one expression.
522: pub enum Evaluation {
523:   /// The expression produced a value.
524:   Value(LouderVariant),
525:   /// The expression raised an ask and cannot finish until the host answers.
526:   Suspended,
527: }
528: 
529: /// Identifies who an expression is being evaluated for, so that story state,
530: /// events, and asks can all be attributed correctly.
531: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
532: pub struct EvaluationScope {
533:   pub conversation: LouderName,
534:   pub actor: LouderActor,
535:   pub channel: LouderName,
536:   /// The hub the evaluation is about.
537:   ///
538:   /// A special variable is read from the hub in hand, so that `%speaker` says
539:   /// who would speak *this* hub rather than whichever one comes first.
540:   pub hub: LouderPosition,
541:   /// The motion that triggered this evaluation, if any.
542:   pub motion: Option<Motion>,
543: }
544: 
545: impl EvaluationScope {
546:   pub fn new(conversation: LouderName, actor: LouderActor, channel: LouderName) -> EvaluationScope {
547:     EvaluationScope {
548:       conversation,
549:       actor,
550:       channel,
551:       hub: super::hub::BLANK_POSITION,
552:       motion: None,
553:     }
554:   }
555: 
556:   fn event(&self, data: EventData) -> LouderEvent {
557:     LouderEvent {
558:       conversation: self.conversation,
559:       channel: self.channel,
560:       actor: self.actor,
561:       data,
562:     }
563:   }
564: }
565: 
566: /// Functions callable from an expression.
567: ///
568: /// The engine has no built-in behaviour for these: the host program supplies
569: /// them, which is what makes asks and game-specific queries possible without
570: /// the engine knowing anything about the game.
571: pub trait ExpressionHost {
572:   /// Handle a call to `name`. Return `None` to report an unknown function.
573:   fn call(&mut self, name: &str, args: &[LouderVariant]) -> Option<LouderVariant>;
574: }
575: 
576: impl ExpressionHost for LouderContext {
577:   fn call(&mut self, _name: &str, _args: &[LouderVariant]) -> Option<LouderVariant> {
578:     return None;
579:   }
580: }
581: 
582: /// Evaluates expressions against a loaded conversation.
583: ///
584: /// Evaluation is deliberately restartable. Asks suspend the whole expression,
585: /// so an expression that raises an ask is evaluated again from the beginning
586: /// once the verdict arrives. To make that safe, every write to story state is
587: /// deferred until the expression has evaluated completely, so a retry cannot
588: /// apply the same assignment twice.
589: #[derive(Default, Clone)]
590: pub struct Evaluator {
591:   scope: EvaluationScope,
592: }
593: 
594: impl Evaluator {
595:   pub fn new(scope: EvaluationScope) -> Evaluator {
596:     Evaluator { scope }
597:   }
598: 
599:   pub fn scope(&self) -> &EvaluationScope {
600:     &self.scope
601:   }
602: 
603:   /// Evaluate one expression, recording conditions and statements as events.
604:   pub fn evaluate(
605:     &self,
606:     context: &mut LouderContext,
607:     source: &str,
608:   ) -> Result<Evaluation, ParseError> {
609:     let expr = parse(source)?;
610:     self.eval(context, &expr)
611:   }
612: 
613:   /// Evaluate one expression that has already been parsed.
614:   pub fn evaluate_expr(
615:     &self,
616:     context: &mut LouderContext,
617:     expr: &Expr,
618:   ) -> Result<Evaluation, ParseError> {
619:     self.eval(context, expr)
620:   }
621: 
622:   fn eval(
623:     &self,
624:     context: &mut LouderContext,
625:     expr: &Expr,
626:   ) -> Result<Evaluation, ParseError> {
627:     let node = expr;
628:     match node {
629:       Expr::Int(value) => Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::Int(*value))),
630:       Expr::Float(value) => Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::Float(*value))),
631:       Expr::Bool(value) => Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::from_bool(*value))),
632:       Expr::Str(value) => {
633:         let name = context.intern_name(value);
634:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::String(name)))
635:       }
636: 
637:       Expr::Var(name) => {
638:         let key = context.intern_name(name);
639:         let value = context.get_state(self.scope.conversation, key);
640:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(value))
641:       }
642: 
643:       Expr::Special(name) => Ok(Evaluation::Value(self.special_value(context, name))),
644: 
645:       Expr::Neg(inner) => match self.eval(context, inner)? {
646:         Evaluation::Suspended => Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
647:         Evaluation::Value(value) => Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::Int(-value.as_int()))),
648:       },
649: 
650:       Expr::Not(inner) => match self.eval(context, inner)? {
651:         Evaluation::Suspended => Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
652:         Evaluation::Value(value) => {
653:           let truthy = self.truthy(context, &value);
654:           Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::from_bool(!truthy)))
655:         }
656:       },
657: 
658:       Expr::Binary(op, lhs, rhs) => {
659:         let left = match self.eval(context, lhs)? {
660:           Evaluation::Suspended => return Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
661:           Evaluation::Value(value) => value,
662:         };
663:         let right = match self.eval(context, rhs)? {
664:           Evaluation::Suspended => return Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
665:           Evaluation::Value(value) => value,
666:         };
667:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(self.apply(context, *op, &left, &right)))
668:       }
669: 
670:       // The write happens only after the right hand side has fully evaluated,
671:       // so that a suspension retries without having already assigned.
672:       Expr::Assign(name, value) => {
673:         let computed = match self.eval(context, value)? {
674:           Evaluation::Suspended => return Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
675:           Evaluation::Value(computed) => computed,
676:         };
677: 
678:         // A special variable is not story state. The engine applies a write to
679:         // `%speaker` to the move that made it, and the hub keeps the identity it
680:         // had, so the write itself changes nothing here.
681:         if is_special(name) {
682:           return Ok(Evaluation::Value(computed));
683:         }
684: 
685:         let key = context.intern_name(name);
686:         context.set_state(self.scope.conversation, key, computed.clone());
687:         let statement = EventData::Statement(key);
688:         context.push_event(self.scope.event(statement));
689:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(computed))
690:       }
691: 
692:       Expr::PreInc(name, step) => {
693:         // A special variable is read from the engine, and there is nothing to
694:         // count, so the increment is worth nothing.
695:         if is_special(name) {
696:           return Ok(Evaluation::Value(self.special_value(context, name)));
697:         }
698: 
699:         let key = context.intern_name(name);
700:         let current = context.get_state(self.scope.conversation, key);
701:         let updated = current.arith(if *step > 0 { ArithOp::Add } else { ArithOp::Sub }, &LouderVariant::Int(step.abs()));
702:         context.set_state(self.scope.conversation, key, updated.clone());
703:         let statement = EventData::Statement(key);
704:         context.push_event(self.scope.event(statement));
705:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(updated))
706:       }
707: 
708:       Expr::PostInc(name, step) => {
709:         if is_special(name) {
710:           return Ok(Evaluation::Value(self.special_value(context, name)));
711:         }
712: 
713:         let key = context.intern_name(name);
714:         let current = context.get_state(self.scope.conversation, key);
715:         let updated = current.arith(if *step > 0 { ArithOp::Add } else { ArithOp::Sub }, &LouderVariant::Int(step.abs()));
716:         context.set_state(self.scope.conversation, key, updated);
717:         let statement = EventData::Statement(key);
718:         context.push_event(self.scope.event(statement));
719:         Ok(Evaluation::Value(current))
720:       }
721: 
722:       Expr::Call(name, args) => {
723:         let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
724:         for arg in args {
725:           match self.eval(context, arg)? {
726:             Evaluation::Suspended => return Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
727:             Evaluation::Value(value) => values.push(value),
728:           }
729:         }
730: 
731:         let resolved: Vec<LouderVariant> = values;
732:         match context.call(name, &resolved) {
733:           Some(value) => Ok(Evaluation::Value(value)),
734:           None => {
735:             // An unknown function evaluates to 0 rather than aborting the whole
736:             // conversation, matching how unset variables behave.
737:             Ok(Evaluation::Value(LouderVariant::Int(0)))
738:           }
739:         }
740:       }
741: 
742:       Expr::Ask(args) => {
743:         let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
744:         for arg in args {
745:           match self.eval(context, arg)? {
746:             Evaluation::Suspended => return Ok(Evaluation::Suspended),
747:             Evaluation::Value(value) => values.push(value),
748:           }
749:         }
750: 
751:         let callback = context.intern_name(&args_to_text(args));
752:         let motion = self.scope.motion.clone().unwrap_or(Motion {
753:           source_position: super::hub::LouderPosition::MIN,
754:           target_position: super::hub::LouderPosition::MIN,
755:           choice_index: -1,
756:           speaker: LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
757:           background: false,
758:         });
759: 
760:         context.push_ask(LouderAsk {
761:           conversation: self.scope.conversation,
762:           channel: self.scope.channel,
763:           actor: self.scope.actor,
764:           motion,
765:           ask: callback,
766:         });
767: 
768:         Ok(Evaluation::Suspended)
769:       }
770:     }
771:   }
772: 
773:   fn truthy(&self, context: &LouderContext, value: &LouderVariant) -> bool {
774:     let text = match value {
775:       LouderVariant::String(name) => context.resolve_name(*name),
776:       _ => None,
777:     };
778:     value.is_truthy(text)
779:   }
780: 
781:   /// The value of a special variable, read from the engine.
782:   ///
783:   /// A special variable is not story state, so it is read from the hub and the
784:   /// actor in hand rather than from the state table.
785:   fn special_value(&self, context: &LouderContext, name: &str) -> LouderVariant {
786:     special_value(
787:       context,
788:       self.scope.conversation,
789:       self.scope.actor,
790:       self.scope.hub,
791:       name,
792:     )
793:   }
794: 
795:   fn apply(&self, context: &mut LouderContext, op: BinOp, left: &LouderVariant, right: &LouderVariant) -> LouderVariant {
796:     // `+` concatenates when either side is a string, which is what lets text be
797:     // built up inside expressions. This is the one operator the two evaluation
798:     // passes cannot share, because building a string means interning it.
799:     if op == BinOp::Add && (left.kind() == VariantKind::String || right.kind() == VariantKind::String) {
800:       return self.intern(context, &join_strings(context, left, right));
801:     }
802: 
803:     apply_binary(context, op, left, right)
804:   }
805: 
806:   fn intern(&self, context: &mut LouderContext, text: &str) -> LouderVariant {
807:     let name = context.intern_name(text);
808:     LouderVariant::String(name)
809:   }
810: }
811: 
812: /// The text of `left + right` where either side is a string.
813: fn join_strings(context: &LouderContext, left: &LouderVariant, right: &LouderVariant) -> String {
814:   let mut joined = String::new();
815:   for operand in [left, right] {
816:     match operand {
817:       LouderVariant::String(name) => {
818:         if let Some(text) = context.resolve_name(*name) {
819:           joined.push_str(text);
820:         }
821:       }
822:       other => joined.push_str(&other.as_int().to_string()),
823:     }
824:   }
825:   joined
826: }
827: 
828: /// Every binary operator except `+` on two strings.
829: ///
830: /// Both evaluation passes come through here, so an operator cannot mean one
831: /// thing while a hub is being checked for visibility and another while an actor
832: /// is travelling onto it.
833: fn apply_binary(
834:   context: &LouderContext,
835:   op: BinOp,
836:   left: &LouderVariant,
837:   right: &LouderVariant,
838: ) -> LouderVariant {
839:   // Equality between two strings compares their text; every other comparison
840:   // falls back to the numeric rules.
841:   if matches!(op, BinOp::Eq | BinOp::Ne) && left.kind() == VariantKind::String && right.kind() == VariantKind::String {
842:     let a = context.resolve_name(string_id(left)).unwrap_or("");
843:     let b = context.resolve_name(string_id(right)).unwrap_or("");
844:     let equal = a == b;
845:     return LouderVariant::from_bool(if op == BinOp::Eq { equal } else { !equal });
846:   }
847: 
848:   // The guide's truthiness rules: 0, false and the empty string are all falsy.
849:   let truthy = |value: &LouderVariant| {
850:     let text = match value {
851:       LouderVariant::String(name) => context.resolve_name(*name),
852:       _ => None,
853:     };
854:     value.is_truthy(text)
855:   };
856: 
857:   match op {
858:     BinOp::Add => left.arith(ArithOp::Add, right),
859:     BinOp::Sub => left.arith(ArithOp::Sub, right),
860:     BinOp::Mul => left.arith(ArithOp::Mul, right),
861:     BinOp::Div => left.arith(ArithOp::Div, right),
862:     BinOp::Mod => left.arith(ArithOp::Mod, right),
863:     BinOp::Eq => LouderVariant::from_bool(left.cmp_numeric(CmpOp::Eq, right)),
864:     BinOp::Ne => LouderVariant::from_bool(left.cmp_numeric(CmpOp::Ne, right)),
865:     BinOp::Lt => LouderVariant::from_bool(left.cmp_numeric(CmpOp::Lt, right)),
866:     BinOp::Le => LouderVariant::from_bool(left.cmp_numeric(CmpOp::Le, right)),
867:     BinOp::Gt => LouderVariant::from_bool(left.cmp_numeric(CmpOp::Gt, right)),
868:     BinOp::Ge => LouderVariant::from_bool(left.cmp_numeric(CmpOp::Ge, right)),
869:     BinOp::And => LouderVariant::from_bool(truthy(left) && truthy(right)),
870:     BinOp::Or => LouderVariant::from_bool(truthy(left) || truthy(right)),
871:     BinOp::BitAnd => LouderVariant::Int(left.as_int() & right.as_int()),
872:     BinOp::BitOr => LouderVariant::Int(left.as_int() | right.as_int()),
873:     BinOp::BitXor => LouderVariant::Int(left.as_int() ^ right.as_int()),
874:     BinOp::Shl => LouderVariant::Int(left.as_int().wrapping_shl(right.as_int() as u32)),
875:     BinOp::Shr => LouderVariant::Int(left.as_int().wrapping_shr(right.as_int() as u32)),
876:   }
877: }
878: 
879: /// The value of a special variable.
880: ///
881: /// Special variables are read from the engine rather than from story state, so
882: /// they need to know who is asking and which hub is being asked about.
883: pub fn special_value(
884:   context: &LouderContext,
885:   conversation: LouderName,
886:   actor: LouderActor,
887:   hub: LouderPosition,
888:   name: &str,
889: ) -> LouderVariant {
890:   match name {
891:     // The speaker that would speak this hub, seen from where the actor stands.
892:     SPEAKER_VARIABLE => {
893:       let speaker = super::simulator::Simulator::hub_speaker_for(context, conversation, actor, hub);
894:       LouderVariant::String(speaker)
895:     }
896:     // A special variable the engine does not know is worth nothing, which is
897:     // how an unset variable behaves.
898:     _ => LouderVariant::Int(0),
899:   }
900: }
901: 
902: /// Evaluate a condition without touching the engine.
903: ///
904: /// Conditions are the read-only half of an expression set: by construction
905: /// they contain no assignments, so they can be evaluated against a shared
906: /// context. That matters because the FFI exposes choice and hub queries through
907: /// a `const` handle, and asking "what can this actor see" must not be able to
908: /// change the conversation.
909: ///
910: /// The actor and the hub are needed because a condition may ask the engine
911: /// about the hub it is attached to, as `%speaker` does.
912: ///
913: /// Anything that would need to mutate resolves to 0: an ask has no verdict
914: /// yet, and an unknown function has no implementation.
915: ///
916: /// The result is the condition's own value, not its truthiness, so that this
917: /// can be used for nested subexpressions. Use [`condition_holds`] for the
918: /// yes-or-no answer.
919: pub fn eval_condition(
920:   context: &LouderContext,
921:   conversation: LouderName,
922:   actor: LouderActor,
923:   hub: LouderPosition,
924:   expr: &Expr,
925: ) -> LouderVariant {
926:   match expr {
927:     Expr::Int(value) => LouderVariant::Int(*value),
928:     Expr::Float(value) => LouderVariant::Float(*value),
929:     Expr::Bool(value) => LouderVariant::from_bool(*value),
930:     Expr::Str(value) => LouderVariant::String(lookup(context, value)),
931:     Expr::Var(name) => context.get_state(conversation, lookup(context, name)),
932:     Expr::Special(name) => special_value(context, conversation, actor, hub, name),
933: 
934:     Expr::Neg(inner) => {
935:       let value = eval_condition(context, conversation, actor, hub, inner);
936:       LouderVariant::Int(-value.as_int())
937:     }
938:     Expr::Not(inner) => {
939:       let value = eval_condition(context, conversation, actor, hub, inner);
940:       let text = string_text(context, &value);
941:       LouderVariant::from_bool(!value.is_truthy(text.as_deref()))
942:     }
943: 
944:     Expr::Binary(op, lhs, rhs) => {
945:       let left = eval_condition(context, conversation, actor, hub, lhs);
946:       let right = eval_condition(context, conversation, actor, hub, rhs);
947:       apply_readonly(context, *op, &left, &right)
948:     }
949: 
950:     // A statement in a condition position cannot happen: classification sends
951:     // these to the statement pass, so treating them as their own value is a
952:     // safety net rather than a real path.
953:     Expr::Assign(_, value) => eval_condition(context, conversation, actor, hub, value),
954:     Expr::PreInc(name, _) | Expr::PostInc(name, _) => {
955:       if is_special(name) {
956:         special_value(context, conversation, actor, hub, name)
957:       } else {
958:         context.get_state(conversation, lookup(context, name))
959:       }
960:     }
961: 
962:     Expr::Call(_, args) => {
963:       // The engine ships no built-in functions, and a read-only pass has
964:       // nowhere to route them, so a call contributes nothing here.
965:       let _ = args;
966:       LouderVariant::Int(0)
967:     }
968: 
969:     // An ask has no verdict while a decision is still being made.
970:     Expr::Ask(_) => LouderVariant::Int(0),
971:   }
972: }
973: 
974: /// Whether a condition holds, applying the truthiness rules of the engine
975: /// guide: 0, false, and the empty string are all falsy.
976: pub fn condition_holds(
977:   context: &LouderContext,
978:   conversation: LouderName,
979:   actor: LouderActor,
980:   hub: LouderPosition,
981:   expr: &Expr,
982: ) -> bool {
983:   let value = eval_condition(context, conversation, actor, hub, expr);
984:   let text = string_text(context, &value);
985:   value.is_truthy(text.as_deref())
986: }
987: 
988: fn string_text(context: &LouderContext, value: &LouderVariant) -> Option<String> {
989:   match value {
990:     LouderVariant::String(name) => context.resolve_name(*name).map(|t| t.to_string()),
991:     _ => None,
992:   }
993: }
994: 
995: fn lookup(context: &LouderContext, text: &str) -> LouderName {
996:   // A literal that was never interned cannot equal any stored value, so
997:   // [`LOUDER_NAME_NONE`] stands in for it. Empty strings intern to the same
998:   // sentinel, which keeps `x == ""` working.
999:   match context.find_name(&LouderNameContent::new(text)) {
1000:     LouderContextResult::Found(name) => name,
1001:     LouderContextResult::NotFound(_) => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
1002:   }
1003: }
1004: 
1005: fn apply_readonly(
1006:   context: &LouderContext,
1007:   op: BinOp,
1008:   left: &LouderVariant,
1009:   right: &LouderVariant,
1010: ) -> LouderVariant {
1011:   if op == BinOp::Add && (left.kind() == VariantKind::String || right.kind() == VariantKind::String) {
1012:     // A read-only pass cannot intern a freshly built string, so the concatenation
1013:     // resolves to the name-less string, which compares equal only to itself.
1014:     // Conditions that depend on built text are evaluated for real during
1015:     // travel, where interning is available.
1016:     let _ = join_strings(context, left, right);
1017:     return LouderVariant::String(LOUDER_NAME_NONE);
1018:   }
1019: 
1020:   apply_binary(context, op, left, right)
1021: }
1022: 
1023: fn string_id(value: &LouderVariant) -> LouderName {
1024:   match value {
1025:     LouderVariant::String(name) => *name,
1026:     _ => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
1027:   }
1028: }
1029: 
1030: /// Whether an expression writes anything or asks anything.
1031: ///
1032: /// The engine reads the value of a special variable before the move it belongs
1033: /// to is announced, and an expression that writes or asks would then do so
1034: /// twice: once there, and once when the hub's own statements run.
1035: fn has_side_effects(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
1036:   match expr {
1037:     Expr::Assign(..) | Expr::PreInc(..) | Expr::PostInc(..) | Expr::Ask(_) => true,
1038:     Expr::Neg(inner) | Expr::Not(inner) => has_side_effects(inner),
1039:     Expr::Binary(_, lhs, rhs) => has_side_effects(lhs) || has_side_effects(rhs),
1040:     Expr::Call(_, args) => args.iter().any(has_side_effects),
1041:     _ => false,
1042:   }
1043: }
1044: 
1045: /// The special variable an expression writes to, and the value it writes.
1046: ///
1047: /// The engine has one writable special variable, `%speaker`, and a write to it
1048: /// belongs to the move that made it rather than to the hub: it changes who the
1049: /// hub appears to speak as for that move, and leaves the hub itself alone.
1050: ///
1051: /// The value is only read when it can be read without side effects, since the
1052: /// write is evaluated before the move is announced and a second write to story
1053: /// state would be counted twice.
1054: pub fn special_write(
1055:   context: &mut LouderContext,
1056:   scope: &EvaluationScope,
1057:   expr: &Expr,
1058: ) -> Option<(String, LouderVariant)> {
1059:   let Expr::Assign(name, value) = expr else {
1060:     return None;
1061:   };
1062: 
1063:   if !is_special(name) || has_side_effects(value) {
1064:     return None;
1065:   }
1066: 
1067:   let evaluator = Evaluator::new(scope.clone());
1068:   match evaluator.evaluate_expr(context, value) {
1069:     Ok(Evaluation::Value(value)) => Some((name.clone(), value)),
1070:     _ => None,
1071:   }
1072: }
1073: 
1074: /// Reconstruct the text of an ask's arguments for the host to read back.
1075: fn args_to_text(args: &[Expr]) -> String {
1076:   args.iter().map(expr_to_text).collect::<Vec<String>>().join(" ")
1077: }
1078: 
1079: fn expr_to_text(expr: &Expr) -> String {
1080:   match expr {
1081:     Expr::Int(value) => value.to_string(),
1082:     Expr::Float(value) => value.to_string(),
1083:     Expr::Bool(value) => value.to_string(),
1084:     Expr::Str(value) => value.clone(),
1085:     Expr::Var(name) => name.clone(),
1086:     Expr::Special(name) => name.clone(),
1087:     Expr::Ask(args) => format!("($({}))", args_to_text(args)),
1088:     Expr::Neg(inner) => format!("-{}", expr_to_text(inner)),
1089:     Expr::Not(inner) => format!("!{}", expr_to_text(inner)),
1090:     Expr::Call(name, args) => {
1091:       format!("{}({})", name, args.iter().map(expr_to_text).collect::<Vec<String>>().join(", "))
1092:     }
1093:     Expr::Binary(op, lhs, rhs) => format!(
1094:       "({} {} {})",
1095:       expr_to_text(lhs),
1096:       bin_op_text(*op),
1097:       expr_to_text(rhs)
1098:     ),
1099:     Expr::Assign(name, value) => format!("{name} = {}", expr_to_text(value)),
1100:     // Pre versus post, and up versus down, are separate facts, so both are
1101:     // rendered: the node carries one as its variant and the other as the sign of
1102:     // its step.
1103:     Expr::PreInc(name, step) => format!("{}{name}", step_text(*step)),
1104:     Expr::PostInc(name, step) => format!("{name}{}", step_text(*step)),
1105:   }
1106: }
1107: 
1108: fn step_text(step: i32) -> &'static str {
1109:   if step < 0 {
1110:     "--"
1111:   } else {
1112:     "++"
1113:   }
1114: }
1115: 
1116: fn bin_op_text(op: BinOp) -> &'static str {
1117:   match op {
1118:     BinOp::Add => "+",
1119:     BinOp::Sub => "-",
1120:     BinOp::Mul => "*",
1121:     BinOp::Div => "/",
1122:     BinOp::Mod => "%",
1123:     BinOp::Eq => "==",
1124:     BinOp::Ne => "!=",
1125:     BinOp::Lt => "<",
1126:     BinOp::Le => "<=",
1127:     BinOp::Gt => ">",
1128:     BinOp::Ge => ">=",
1129:     BinOp::And => "&&",
1130:     BinOp::Or => "||",
1131:     BinOp::BitAnd => "&",
1132:     BinOp::BitOr => "|",
1133:     BinOp::BitXor => "^",
1134:     BinOp::Shl => "<<",
1135:     BinOp::Shr => ">>",
1136:   }
1137: }
1138:

10: /// One move of an actor from one hub to another.
11: ///
12: /// Every event a move produces carries the same motion, which is how a host
13: /// reads where an actor came from and where it went.
14: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
15: #[repr(C)]
16: pub struct Motion {

18:   pub target_position: LouderPosition,

20: 
21:   /// The speaker the hub appears to have, for this move.
22:   ///
23:   /// A hub with a speaker of its own speaks as that speaker. A hub without one
24:   /// is anonymous, and the voice of the actor making the move is projected onto
25:   /// it for the length of the move, so the same hub carries a different
26:   /// speaker for each actor that walks onto it.
27:   pub speaker: LouderName,
28: 
29:   pub background: bool,
30: }

60: #[repr(C)]
61: pub struct LoadFailure {

63:   pub error: i32,
64: }

66: /// What an [`LouderEvent`] is about.
67: ///
68: /// A tagged union cannot be read as one value by a host that has to switch on
69: /// it, so this is the discriminant of an [`EventData`] as an `int`, and the
70: /// payload is read through the accessors that match it. A host that does not
71: /// recognise a value is looking at something a newer engine produced.
72: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
73: #[repr(C)]
74: pub enum EventData {
75:   None,
76:   RegularMotion(Motion),
77:   Warp(Motion),
78:   ForceMotion(Motion),
79:   ChoiceAppearance(ChoiceAppearance),
80: 
81:   ChannelChange,
82:   AdditionalDataChange(AdditionalDataChange),
83:   SpeakerChange(LouderName),

95:   ConversationRemoved(LouderName),

97:   /// A record of a loaded document was unusable. This is not a motion and is
98:   /// never attached to one, so a host can tell it apart without inspecting the
99:   /// fields of an event it did not expect.
100:   LoadError(LoadFailure),
101: }

103: impl EventData {
104:   /// Which variant this is, as an `int`.
105:   ///
106:   /// Written out rather than left to the compiler so that the numbering is part
107:   /// of the engine's vocabulary and not a property of the declaration order.
108:   pub fn kind(&self) -> i32 {
109:     match self {
110:       EventData::None => 0,
111:       EventData::RegularMotion(_) => 1,
112:       EventData::Warp(_) => 2,
113:       EventData::ForceMotion(_) => 3,
114:       EventData::ChoiceAppearance(_) => 4,
115: 
116:       EventData::ChannelChange => 5,
117:       EventData::AdditionalDataChange(_) => 6,
118:       EventData::SpeakerChange(_) => 7,
119:       EventData::TextChange(_) => 8,
120: 
121:       EventData::Statement(_) => 9,
122:       EventData::Condition(_) => 10,
123:       EventData::Callback(_) => 11,
124: 
125:       EventData::ActorAdded => 12,
126:       EventData::ActorRemoved => 13,
127:       EventData::SpeakerAdded(_) => 14,
128:       EventData::SpeakerRemoved(_) => 15,
129:       EventData::ConversationAdded(_) => 16,
130:       EventData::ConversationRemoved(_) => 17,
131: 
132:       EventData::LoadError(_) => 18,
133:     }
134:   }
135: 
136:   /// The motion this event carries, if it is about one.
137:   pub fn motion(&self) -> Option<&Motion> {
138:     match self {
139:       EventData::RegularMotion(motion) | EventData::Warp(motion) | EventData::ForceMotion(motion) => Some(motion),
140:       _ => None,
141:     }
142:   }
143: 
144:   /// The name this event carries, whatever kind of name that is.
145:   ///
146:   /// A speaker, a text, an expression, and a callback are all names, so one
147:   /// accessor reads all of them; which of those it was says `kind`.
148:   pub fn name(&self) -> LouderName {
149:     match self {
150:       EventData::SpeakerChange(name)
151:       | EventData::TextChange(name)
152:       | EventData::SpeakerAdded(name)
153:       | EventData::SpeakerRemoved(name)
154:       | EventData::ConversationAdded(name)
155:       | EventData::ConversationRemoved(name)
156:       | EventData::Statement(name)
157:       | EventData::Callback(name) => *name,
158:       _ => super::name::LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
159:     }
160:   }
161: }

1: //! The questions a host asks about what a conversation holds.
2: //!
3: //! There is no cursor and no iterator to keep: every question is asked again
4: //! with the previous answer as a starting point, and the answer walks further
5: //! along. The walking lives here, so that the boundary functions in the crate
6: //! root are left with nothing but the declaration of what may be asked, and so
7: //! that one definition of "the next one" serves every list in the engine.
8: //!
9: //! A list built from a predicate comes back in ascending order, which is what
10: //! makes that walk terminate: a host passes the last id it saw and gets either a
11: //! larger one or the blank that tells it the list is done.
12: 
13: use crate::louder::simulator::ChoiceAvailableError::{self};
14: 
15: use super::actor::{Actor, ActorFlags, LouderActor, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE};
16: use super::context::LouderContext;
17: use super::hub::{Choice, Hub, HubFlags, LouderPosition, BLANK_POSITION};
18: use super::name::{LouderName, LOUDER_NAME_NONE};
19: use super::simulator::Simulator;
20: use super::speaker::SpeakerMap;

22: /// The next name after `start` in a list, or [`LOUDER_NAME_NONE`].
23: ///
24: /// Name ids are content hashes and carry no ordering, so this walks the list
25: /// by position rather than comparing ids: `start` names the element already
26: /// seen, and the element after it is the next one. A blank `start` begins at
27: /// the front, and a `start` that is not in the list ends the iteration.
28: fn next_name_after(items: &[LouderName], start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
29:   if start == LOUDER_NAME_NONE {
30:     return items.first().copied().unwrap_or(LOUDER_NAME_NONE);
31:   }
32: 
33:   match items.iter().position(|item| *item == start) {
34:     Some(index) => items.get(index + 1).copied().unwrap_or(LOUDER_NAME_NONE),
35:     None => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
36:   }
37: }
38: 
39: /// The smallest entry of `items` that is greater than `start`, or `blank`.
40: ///
41: /// Actors and hub positions are registered as non-negative integers in
42: /// ascending order, so the iterators can walk a sorted list by comparison rather
43: /// than by remembering where they got to. The blank is the -1 that stands for
44: /// "not found" on both of them, and being below every real entry it doubles as
45: /// "start from the beginning".
46: fn next_after<T: Copy + Ord>(items: &[T], start: T, blank: T) -> T {
47:   items
48:     .iter()
49:     .copied()
50:     .find(|item| *item > start)
51:     .unwrap_or(blank)
52: }
53: 
54: /// Whether `flags` satisfies a filter.
55: ///
56: /// With `exact` set the flags must match exactly; otherwise every bit named in
57: /// `include` must be present.
58: fn flags_match(flags_bits: i32, include: i32, exact: i32) -> bool {
59:   if exact != 0 {
60:     return flags_bits == include;
61:   }
62:   (flags_bits & include) == include
63: }
64: 
65: /// The ids of the actors matching `predicate`, in ascending order.
66: fn matching_actors<F>(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, predicate: F) -> Vec<LouderActor>
67: where
68:   F: Fn(&Actor) -> bool,
69: {
70:   context
71:     .actors(conversation)
72:     .iter()
73:     .filter(|entry| predicate(entry))
74:     .map(|entry| entry.id())
75:     .collect()
76: }
77: 
78: /// The positions of every hub of `conversation` satisfying `predicate`, in
79: /// ascending order.
80: ///
81: /// All six hub iterators are this with a different question, so the question is
82: /// the only thing each of them has to say for itself. The sort is here rather
83: /// than in each caller because the hub map has no order of its own, and these
84: /// functions are documented to walk positions upwards.
85: fn matching_hubs(
86:   context: &LouderContext,
87:   conversation: LouderName,
88:   predicate: impl Fn(&Hub) -> bool,
89: ) -> Vec<LouderPosition> {
90:   let Some(conversation_ref) = context.get_conversation(conversation) else {
91:     return Vec::new();
92:   };
93: 
94:   let mut positions: Vec<LouderPosition> = conversation_ref
95:     .hubs()
96:     .values()
97:     .filter(|hub| predicate(hub))
98:     .map(|hub| hub.position())
99:     .collect();
100:   positions.sort_unstable();
101:   positions
102: }
103: 
104: /// The speakers `speaker` is listening to, one after another.
105: pub fn next_speaker_listen_to(context: &LouderContext, speaker: LouderName, start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
106:   match context.get_speaker(speaker) {
107:     Some(entry) => next_name_after(entry.listening(), start),
108:     None => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
109:   }
110: }
111: 
112: /// The speakers an actor speaks as, one after another.
113: pub fn next_actor_speak_as(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, actor: LouderActor, start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
114:   match context.get_actor(conversation, actor) {
115:     Some(entry) => next_name_after(entry.speak_as(), start),
116:     None => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
117:   }
118: }
119: 
120: /// The base speakers an actor listens to, one after another.
121: ///
122: /// These are the actor's own `listen_to` speakers, without the ones reachable
123: /// through who it speaks as.
124: pub fn next_actor_listening_base(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, actor: LouderActor, start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
125:   match context.get_actor(conversation, actor) {
126:     Some(entry) => next_name_after(entry.listen_to(), start),
127:     None => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
128:   }
129: }
130: 
131: /// The speakers an actor listens to, one after another.
132: ///
133: /// The actor's `listen_to` speakers plus the listening speakers of the speakers
134: /// it speaks as.
135: pub fn next_actor_listening(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, actor: LouderActor, start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
136:   let speakers = Simulator::speakers_heard_by(context, conversation, actor);
137:   next_name_after(&speakers, start)

140: /// The tags of a hub, one after another.
141: pub fn next_hub_tag(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, position: LouderPosition, start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
142:   match context.hub(conversation, position) {
143:     Some(hub) => next_name_after(hub.tags(), start),
144:     None => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
145:   }

148: /// The recipients of a hub, one after another.
149: ///
150: /// The walk ends straight away for a hub with no recipients, which is how a hub
151: /// that is not restricted reads.
152: pub fn next_hub_recipient(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, position: LouderPosition, start: LouderName) -> LouderName {
153:   match context.hub(conversation, position) {
154:     Some(hub) => next_name_after(hub.recipients(), start),
155:     None => LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
156:   }
157: }
158: 
159: /// The actors in a channel.
160: ///
161: /// Actors outside the flow have no channel and are never returned.
162: pub fn next_actor_in_channel(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, channel: LouderName, start: LouderActor) -> LouderActor {
163:   let actors = matching_actors(context, conversation, |entry| entry.channel() == channel);
164:   next_after(&actors, start, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE)
165: }
166: 
167: /// The actors in a channel at one position.
168: pub fn next_actor_in_channel_and_position(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, channel: LouderName, position: LouderPosition, start: LouderActor) -> LouderActor {
169:   let actors = matching_actors(context, conversation, |entry| {
170:     entry.channel() == channel && entry.position() == position
171:   });
172:   next_after(&actors, start, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE)
173: }

175: /// The actors speaking as a speaker.
176: pub fn next_actor_speaking_as(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, speaker: LouderName, start: LouderActor) -> LouderActor {
177:   let actors = matching_actors(context, conversation, |entry| entry.speak_as().contains(&speaker));
178:   next_after(&actors, start, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE)
179: }

182: ///
183: /// This covers `listen_to` plus the listening actors derived from the listening

185: pub fn next_actor_listening_to(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, speaker: LouderName, start: LouderActor) -> LouderActor {
186:   let actors = matching_actors(context, conversation, |entry| {
187:     Simulator::speakers_heard_by(context, conversation, entry.id()).contains(&speaker)
188:   });

190: }

192: /// The actors carrying certain flags.
193: pub fn next_actor_from_flags(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, include: ActorFlags, exact: i32, start: LouderActor) -> LouderActor {
194:   let wanted = include.bits();
195:   let actors = matching_actors(context, conversation, |entry| {
196:     flags_match(entry.flags().bits(), wanted, exact)
197:   });
198:   next_after(&actors, start, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE)
199: }

201: /// The hubs in a channel.
202: ///
203: /// Hubs that name no channel belong to the default channel, so searching for it
204: /// also finds them.
205: pub fn next_hub_by_channel(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, channel: LouderName, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
206:   let main = context.main_channel();
207:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| match hub.channel() {
208:     LOUDER_NAME_NONE => main == channel,
209:     hub_channel => hub_channel == channel,
210:   });
211:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
212: }

214: /// The hubs spoken by a speaker.

216:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| hub.speaker() == speaker);

225: pub fn next_hub_heard_by(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, speaker: LouderName, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
226:   let mut heard = vec![speaker];

235: /// The hubs with a priority within the given bounds.

239:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| {
240:     hub.priority() >= priority_min && hub.priority() <= priority_max
241:   });

251:     let tags = hub.tags();
252:     include.iter().all(|tag| tags.contains(tag)) && (!exact || tags.len() == include.len())
253:   });

257: /// The hubs carrying certain flags.
258: pub fn next_hub_from_flags(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, include: HubFlags, exact: i32, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
259:   let wanted = include.bits();
260:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| {
261:     flags_match(hub.flags().bits(), wanted, exact)
262:   });
263:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
264: }

266: /// The choice on a hub that `actor` is allowed to take, if there is one.
267: ///
268: /// The engine's rules decide whether a choice can be taken, so a lookup through
269: /// this answers "may this actor choose that" and not merely "is there a choice
270: /// there". Both the choice text and the hub it leads to are read through here,
271: /// which is what keeps a host from being shown a choice the engine would refuse.
272: pub fn available_choice(
273:   context: &LouderContext,
274:   conversation: LouderName,
275:   position: LouderPosition,
276:   actor: LouderActor,
277:   choice_index: usize,
278: ) -> Option<&Choice> {
279:   if Simulator::choice_available(context, conversation, actor, position, choice_index) != ChoiceAvailableError::NoError {
280:     return None;
281:   }
282: 
283:   context
284:     .hub(conversation, position)
285:     .and_then(|hub| hub.choice(choice_index))
286: }

26: #[derive(Default, PartialEq, Eq, Clone)]
27: pub struct LouderNameContent {

29: }
30:

37:

41:   }

47: 
48: pub enum LouderContextResult {

50:   NotFound(LouderName),
51: }
52: 
53: pub trait LouderNameContext {
54:   fn find_name(&self, name_context: &LouderNameContent) -> LouderContextResult;
55: 
56:   fn has_name(&self, name: &LouderName) -> bool;
57: 
58:   fn get_name_content(&self, name: &LouderName) -> Option<&LouderNameContent>;
59: 
60:   fn add_name(&mut self, name_context: &LouderNameContent) -> LouderContextResult;
61: }
62: 
63: /// Name lookup that can also create names, which only the engine itself needs.
64: ///
65: /// This is kept apart from [`LouderNameContext`] because the program driving
66: /// the engine should look names up and read them back, but should assign their
67: /// ids through [`crate::declare_name`] instead of growing the table behind the
68: /// engine's back.
69: pub trait LouderInternContext: LouderNameContext {
70:   /// The id of `text`, assigning a new one if it has not been declared.
71:   fn intern_name(&mut self, text: &str) -> LouderName;
72: 
73:   /// The id of `text`, or [`LOUDER_NAME_NONE`] if `text` is empty.
74:   fn intern_optional(&mut self, text: &str) -> LouderName;
75: 
76:   /// Intern every entry of a delimited list.
77:   fn intern_list(&mut self, parts: &[&str]) -> Vec<LouderName>;
78: 
79:   /// The content of `name` as a UTF-8 string slice.
80:   fn resolve_name(&self, name: LouderName) -> Option<&str>;
81: }
82: 
83: impl LouderInternContext for LouderContext {
84:   fn intern_name(&mut self, text: &str) -> LouderName {
85:     let content = LouderNameContent::new(text);
86:     match self.add_name(&content) {
87:       LouderContextResult::Found(name) => name,
88:       LouderContextResult::NotFound(name) => name,
89:     }
90:   }
91: 
92:   fn intern_optional(&mut self, text: &str) -> LouderName {
93:     if text.is_empty() {
94:       return LOUDER_NAME_NONE;
95:     }
96: 
97:     self.intern_name(text)
98:   }
99: 
100:   fn intern_list(&mut self, parts: &[&str]) -> Vec<LouderName> {
101:     let mut names = Vec::with_capacity(parts.len());
102:     for part in parts {
103:       names.push(self.intern_optional(part));
104:     }
105:     names
106:   }
107: 
108:   fn resolve_name(&self, name: LouderName) -> Option<&str> {
109:     self.get_name_content(&name).and_then(|c| c.as_str())
110:   }
111: }

113: /// Split on commas and/or runs of whitespace.
114: ///
115: /// Tags are documented as "separated by commas or whitespace", so both
116: /// separators are accepted and empty entries are dropped.
117: pub fn split_inline(text: &str) -> Vec<&str> {
118:   text.split(|c: char| c == ',' || c.is_whitespace())
119:     .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
120:     .collect()
121: }
122: 
123: /// Split on newlines, dropping empty entries.
124: ///
125: /// Expressions, callbacks, recipients, and a speaker's listening list are all
126: /// documented as newline separated. `\r` is tolerated so documents saved with
127: /// Windows line endings load the same way.
128: pub fn split_lines(text: &str) -> Vec<&str> {
129:   text.split('\n')
130:     .map(|s| s.trim_end_matches('\r').trim())
131:     .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
132:     .collect()
133: }

79: #[repr(C)]
80: pub enum LouderVariant {

82:   Float(f32),

86: impl Default for LouderVariant {
87:   /// Uninitialized variables read as 0.

92: 
93: impl LouderVariant {
94:   /// The value an unset variable reads as.
95:   pub fn zero() -> LouderVariant {
96:     LouderVariant::Int(0)
97:   }
98: 
99:   pub fn from_bool(value: bool) -> LouderVariant {
100:     LouderVariant::Int(if value { 1 } else { 0 })
101:   }
102:

105:   }

115:   /// The discriminant of this variant, for callers that branch on the kind

120:       LouderVariant::Float(_) => VariantKind::Float,
121:       LouderVariant::String(_) => VariantKind::String,
122:     }
123:   }
124: 
125:   /// This variant read as an integer.

127:   /// Floats truncate and strings go to zero, per the engine guide's rule that
128:   /// variants can always be treated as integers.
129:   pub fn as_int(&self) -> i32 {
130:     match self {
131:       LouderVariant::Int(i) => *i,
132:       LouderVariant::Float(f) => *f as i32,
133:       LouderVariant::String(_) => 0,
134:     }
135:   }
136: 
137:   /// This variant read as a float.
138:   ///
139:   /// Strings go to zero, matching [`Self::as_int`].
140:   pub fn as_float(&self) -> f32 {
141:     match self {
142:       LouderVariant::Int(i) => *i as f32,
143:       LouderVariant::Float(f) => *f,
144:       LouderVariant::String(_) => 0.0,
145:     }
146:   }
147: 
148:   /// Whether this variant counts as true in a condition.
149:   ///
150:   /// An empty string and the string `"0"` are both false, which is what lets a
151:   /// condition written as a bare string behave like a number.
152:   ///
153:   /// `text` is the already-resolved content of a string variant; the caller
154:   /// reads it out of the context, since a [`LouderName`] is meaningless
155:   /// without it. It is ignored for the numeric kinds.
156:   pub fn is_truthy(&self, text: Option<&str>) -> bool {
157:     match self {
158:       LouderVariant::Int(i) => *i != 0,
159:       LouderVariant::Float(f) => *f != 0.0,
160:       LouderVariant::String(_) => match text {
161:         None => false,
162:         Some(t) => !t.is_empty() && t != "0",
163:       },
164:     }
165:   }
166: }
167: 
168: /// Which kind of data a [`LouderVariant`] holds.
169: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
170: pub enum VariantKind {
171:   Int,
172:   Float,
173:   String,
174: }
175: 
176: /// Arithmetic operators available to expressions.
177: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
178: pub enum ArithOp {
179:   Add,
180:   Sub,
181:   Mul,
182:   Div,
183:   Mod,
184: }
185: 
186: /// Relational operators available to expressions.
187: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
188: pub enum CmpOp {
189:   Eq,
190:   Ne,
191:   Lt,
192:   Le,
193:   Gt,
194:   Ge,
195: }
196: 
197: impl LouderVariant {
198:   /// Apply a numeric operator, coercing both sides as the engine guide
199:   /// describes.
200:   ///
201:   /// The result is a float when either operand is a float and an integer
202:   /// otherwise. Division and modulo by zero yield 0 rather than panicking,
203:   /// because a malformed expression must not take down the host program.
204:   ///
205:   /// String operands are *not* handled here: they coerce to 0, so the
206:   /// expression evaluator performs concatenation before calling this.
207:   pub fn arith(&self, op: ArithOp, other: &LouderVariant) -> LouderVariant {
208:     let both_int = self.kind() != VariantKind::Float && other.kind() != VariantKind::Float;
209: 
210:     if both_int {
211:       let a = self.as_int();
212:       let b = other.as_int();
213:       let result = match op {
214:         ArithOp::Add => a.wrapping_add(b),
215:         ArithOp::Sub => a.wrapping_sub(b),
216:         ArithOp::Mul => a.wrapping_mul(b),
217:         ArithOp::Div => if b == 0 { 0 } else { a.wrapping_div(b) },
218:         ArithOp::Mod => if b == 0 { 0 } else { a.wrapping_rem(b) },
219:       };
220:       return LouderVariant::Int(result);
221:     }
222: 
223:     let a = self.as_float();
224:     let b = other.as_float();
225:     let result = match op {
226:       ArithOp::Add => a + b,
227:       ArithOp::Sub => a - b,
228:       ArithOp::Mul => a * b,
229:       ArithOp::Div => a / b,
230:       ArithOp::Mod => a % b,
231:     };
232:     LouderVariant::Float(result)
233:   }
234: 
235:   /// Compare two variants numerically.
236:   ///
237:   /// Equality between two strings is *not* handled here, because resolving it
238:   /// requires reading the strings back out of the context. The evaluator
239:   /// handles that case before falling back to this.
240:   pub fn cmp_numeric(&self, op: CmpOp, other: &LouderVariant) -> bool {
241:     if self.kind() != VariantKind::Float && other.kind() != VariantKind::Float {
242:       let a = self.as_int();
243:       let b = other.as_int();
244:       return match op {
245:         CmpOp::Eq => a == b,
246:         CmpOp::Ne => a != b,
247:         CmpOp::Lt => a < b,
248:         CmpOp::Le => a <= b,
249:         CmpOp::Gt => a > b,
250:         CmpOp::Ge => a >= b,
251:       };
252:     }
253: 
254:     let a = self.as_float();
255:     let b = other.as_float();
256:     match op {
257:       CmpOp::Eq => a == b,
258:       CmpOp::Ne => a != b,
259:       CmpOp::Lt => a < b,
260:       CmpOp::Le => a <= b,
261:       CmpOp::Gt => a > b,
262:       CmpOp::Ge => a >= b,
263:     }
264:   }
265: }

11: /// An error code returned by load operations.
12: ///
13: /// Standard JSON syntax errors are returned, as well as errors relating to the
14: /// shape of the loaded document.
15: pub type LoadError = i32;
16: 
17: /// The document loaded without incident.
18: pub const LOUDER_LOAD_OK: LoadError = 0;
19: /// The document is not valid JSON.
20: pub const LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON: LoadError = 1;
21: /// The document parsed, but is not a list of records.
22: pub const LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_A_LIST: LoadError = 2;
23: /// A record was not an object.
24: pub const LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT: LoadError = 3;
25: /// The conversation name was already occupied by a loaded conversation.
26: pub const LOUDER_LOAD_ALREADY_LOADED: LoadError = 4;
27: 
28: /// Read `bytes` as the text of a document.
29: ///
30: /// JSON is text, so the engine has to know what the bytes say before it can
31: /// parse them, and this is the one place where it does: UTF-8, because that is
32: /// what the editor writes. This is a check on the input, not an encoding scheme
33: /// the engine offers. Nothing here is converted, and a caller that holds bytes
34: /// in some other encoding is expected to decode them on its own side rather than
35: /// ask the engine to guess.
36: pub fn decode(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<&str, LoadError> {
37:   std::str::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|_| LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON)
38: }
39: 
40: /// The keys of a hub record that are part of the schema. Anything else is
41: /// additional data.
42: const HUB_KEYS: &[&str] = &[
43:   "type",
44:   "conversation_id",
45:   "speaker",
46:   "text",
47:   "tags",
48:   "choices",
49:   "expressions",
50:   "callbacks",
51:   "flags",
52:   "recipients",
53:   "channel",
54:   "priority",
55: ];
56: 
57: /// The keys of a speaker record that are part of the schema.
58: const SPEAKER_KEYS: &[&str] = &["type", "name", "flags", "listening"];
59: 
60: /// Load a conversation from a JSON document.
61: ///
62: /// The document must be a list of objects. Records whose `type` is `hub`
63: /// become hubs, `connection` records become choices, and `camera` records are
64: /// ignored. Problems with individual records are reported through events
65: /// rather than failing the whole load, so one malformed hub does not cost the
66: /// caller the rest of the conversation.
67: pub fn load_conversation(
68:   context: &mut LouderContext,
69:   conversation: LouderName,
70:   document: &str,
71: ) -> LoadError {
72:   if context.get_conversation(conversation).is_some() {
73:     return LOUDER_LOAD_ALREADY_LOADED;
74:   }
75: 
76:   let records = match parse_records(context, conversation, document) {
77:     Ok(records) => records,
78:     Err(error) => return error,
79:   };
80: 
81:   let mut loaded = Conversation::new(conversation);
82:   // Connections are collected before being resolved because a choice takes its
83:   // text from the hub it links to, which may not have been read yet.
84:   let mut connections: Map<LouderPosition, Vec<(usize, LouderPosition)>> = Map::new();
85: 
86:   for record in records {
87:     let Some(object) = record.as_object() else {
88:       report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, BLANK_POSITION);
89:       continue;
90:     };
91: 
92:     match record_type(object) {
93:       Some("hub") => {
94:         let position = position_of(object);
95:         let hub = read_hub(context, object);
96:         if loaded.add_hub(hub) {
97:           // Two hubs claimed the same position; the later one won.
98:           report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, position);
99:         }
100:       }
101:       Some("connection") => match read_connection(object) {
102:         Some((from, port, to)) => connections.entry(from).or_default().push((port, to)),
103:         None => report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, position_of(object)),
104:       },
105:       // The camera record only remembers where the editor was scrolled to.
106:       Some("camera") => {}
107:       _ => report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, position_of(object)),
108:     }
109:   }
110: 
111:   // Ports are numbered in the document and may arrive out of order, so each
112:   // group is sorted and compacted into dense indices. A gap in the numbering
113:   // therefore closes up rather than leaving a hole choices cannot be indexed
114:   // through.
115:   for (from, mut ports) in connections {
116:     ports.sort_unstable_by_key(|(port, _)| *port);
117:     ports.dedup_by_key(|(port, _)| *port);
118: 
119:     for (_, to) in ports {
120:       let target_text = match loaded.hub(to) {
121:         Some(hub) => hub.text().to_string(),
122:         None => {
123:           // The choice points at a position that does not exist.
124:           report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, from);
125:           continue;
126:         }
127:       };
128: 
129:       let target_text_name = context.intern_optional(&target_text);
130:       let Some(hub) = loaded.hub_mut(from) else {
131:         continue;
132:       };
133:       hub.push_choice(Choice::new(target_text, target_text_name, to));
134:     }
135:   }
136: 
137:   context.add_conversation(loaded);
138:   LOUDER_LOAD_OK
139: }
140: 
141: /// Load the speaker config from a JSON document.
142: ///
143: /// The config is a singleton: it is not tied to any one conversation, so a
144: /// speaker declared here is visible from all of them. Records replace any
145: /// speaker of the same name rather than failing, so a config can be reloaded.
146: ///
147: /// A record the engine cannot read is reported and skipped rather than costing
148: /// the caller the rest of the config. The config belongs to no conversation, so
149: /// its events carry no conversation name.
150: pub fn load_config(context: &mut LouderContext, document: &str) -> LoadError {
151:   let records = match parse_records(context, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, document) {
152:     Ok(records) => records,
153:     Err(error) => return error,
154:   };
155: 
156:   for record in records {
157:     let Some(object) = record.as_object() else {
158:       report(context, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, BLANK_POSITION);
159:       continue;
160:     };
161: 
162:     if record_type(object) != Some("speaker") {
163:       continue;
164:     }
165: 
166:     let name_text = string_field(object, "name");
167:     if name_text.is_empty() {
168:       // A speaker with no name is not a speaker the host could ever ask for.
169:       report(context, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, BLANK_POSITION);
170:       continue;
171:     }
172: 
173:     let name = context.intern_name(&name_text);
174:     let flags = SpeakerFlags::from_bits_truncate(int_field(object, "flags"));
175:     let listening = context.intern_list(&split_lines(&string_field(object, "listening")));
176:     let additional = read_additional(context, object, SPEAKER_KEYS);
177: 
178:     context.set_speaker(Speaker::new(name, flags, listening, additional));
179:   }
180: 
181:   LOUDER_LOAD_OK
182: }
183: 
184: fn record_type(object: &JsonMap<String, Value>) -> Option<&str> {
185:   object.get("type").and_then(Value::as_str)
186: }
187: 
188: /// Read a scalar field as text.
189: ///
190: /// Values that the schema calls numbers are still accepted as strings, so that
191: /// documents saved by an older editor revision keep loading.
192: fn string_field(object: &JsonMap<String, Value>, key: &str) -> String {
193:   match object.get(key) {
194:     Some(Value::String(text)) => text.clone(),
195:     Some(Value::Number(number)) => number.to_string(),
196:     Some(Value::Bool(value)) => value.to_string(),
197:     _ => String::new(),
198:   }
199: }
200: 
201: fn int_field(object: &JsonMap<String, Value>, key: &str) -> i32 {
202:   match object.get(key) {
203:     Some(Value::Number(number)) => number.as_i64().unwrap_or(0) as i32,
204:     Some(Value::String(text)) => text.trim().parse::<f64>().unwrap_or(0.0) as i32,
205:     Some(Value::Bool(value)) => {
206:       if *value {
207:         1
208:       } else {
209:         0
210:       }
211:     }
212:     _ => 0,
213:   }
214: }
215: 
216: fn position_of(object: &JsonMap<String, Value>) -> LouderPosition {
217:   int_field(object, "conversation_id")
218: }
219: 
220: fn read_connection(
221:   object: &JsonMap<String, Value>,
222: ) -> Option<(LouderPosition, usize, LouderPosition)> {
223:   if !object.contains_key("from_id") || !object.contains_key("to_id") {
224:     return None;
225:   }
226: 
227:   let from = int_field(object, "from_id");
228:   let port = int_field(object, "from_port");
229:   let to = int_field(object, "to_id");
230: 
231:   if port < 0 || to < 0 {
232:     return None;
233:   }
234: 
235:   Some((from, port as usize, to))
236: }
237: 
238: fn read_hub(context: &mut LouderContext, object: &JsonMap<String, Value>) -> Hub {
239:   let position = position_of(object);
240: 
241:   let speaker = context.intern_optional(&string_field(object, "speaker"));
242: 
243:   let text = string_field(object, "text");
244:   let text_name = context.intern_optional(&text);
245: 
246:   let tags = context.intern_list(&split_inline(&string_field(object, "tags")));
247:   let recipients = context.intern_list(&split_lines(&string_field(object, "recipients")));
248:   let channel = context.intern_optional(&string_field(object, "channel"));
249: 
250:   let expressions_text = string_field(object, "expressions");
251:   let expressions = context.intern_list(&split_lines(&expressions_text));
252:   let expressions_joined = context.intern_optional(&expressions_text);
253: 
254:   let callbacks_text = string_field(object, "callbacks");
255:   let callbacks = context.intern_list(&split_lines(&callbacks_text));
256:   let callbacks_joined = context.intern_optional(&callbacks_text);
257: 
258:   let flags = HubFlags::from_bits_truncate(int_field(object, "flags"));
259:   let priority = int_field(object, "priority");
260:   let additional = read_additional(context, object, HUB_KEYS);
261: 
262:   Hub::new(
263:     position,
264:     speaker,
265:     text,
266:     text_name,
267:     tags,
268:     recipients,
269:     channel,
270:     priority,
271:     expressions,
272:     expressions_joined,
273:     callbacks,
274:     callbacks_joined,
275:     flags,
276:     additional,
277:   )
278: }
279: 
280: /// Turn a JSON value into a variant.
281: ///
282: /// Objects and arrays have no variant of their own, so they are interned as
283: /// their compact JSON text, which keeps them readable without inventing a
284: /// fourth variant kind.
285: fn read_variant(context: &mut LouderContext, value: &Value) -> LouderVariant {
286:   match value {
287:     Value::Null => LouderVariant::Int(0),
288:     Value::Bool(value) => LouderVariant::from_bool(*value),
289:     Value::Number(number) => match number.as_i64() {
290:       Some(value) => LouderVariant::Int(value as i32),
291:       None => LouderVariant::Float(number.as_f64().unwrap_or(0.0) as f32),
292:     },
293:     Value::String(text) => LouderVariant::String(context.intern_optional(text)),
294:     other => LouderVariant::String(context.intern_optional(&other.to_string())),
295:   }
296: }
297: 
298: /// Report a load problem as an event, so that one bad record does not abort the
299: /// whole load.
300: ///
301: /// `position` is the record at fault, or [`BLANK_POSITION`] when the problem was
302: /// with the document as a whole.
303: fn report(context: &mut LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, error: LoadError, position: LouderPosition) {
304:   context.push_event(LouderEvent {
305:     conversation,
306:     channel: LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
307:     actor: super::actor::LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE,
308:     data: EventData::LoadError(LoadFailure { position, error }),
309:   });
310: }
311: 
312: /// Parse `document` as the list of records both loaders expect.
313: ///
314: /// A problem with the document as a whole is reported as a load error event
315: /// before the error is returned, so that a host reading events rather than
316: /// return codes still learns that the load failed and why.
317: fn parse_records(
318:   context: &mut LouderContext,
319:   conversation: LouderName,
320:   document: &str,
321: ) -> Result<Vec<Value>, LoadError> {
322:   let parsed: Value = match serde_json::from_str(document) {
323:     Ok(parsed) => parsed,
324:     Err(_) => {
325:       report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON, BLANK_POSITION);
326:       return Err(LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON);
327:     }
328:   };
329: 
330:   match parsed {
331:     Value::Array(records) => Ok(records),
332:     _ => {
333:       report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_A_LIST, BLANK_POSITION);
334:       Err(LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_A_LIST)
335:     }
336:   }
337: }
338: 
339: /// Everything on a record the schema does not name, read as a homogeneous
340: /// object.
341: ///
342: /// `schema` is the set of fields the loader has already taken, so this picks up
343: /// the user-defined properties without any of them being listed twice.
344: fn read_additional(
345:   context: &mut LouderContext,
346:   object: &JsonMap<String, Value>,
347:   schema: &[&str],
348: ) -> HomogeneousObject {
349:   let mut additional = HomogeneousObject::new();
350:   for (key, value) in object {
351:     if schema.contains(&key.as_str()) {
352:       continue;
353:     }
354:     let key_name = context.intern_name(key);
355:     additional.set(key_name, read_variant(context, value));
356:   }
357:   additional
358: }

1: //! Writing what the engine holds back out as JSON.
2: //!
3: //! This is the other half of [`super::json_loader`]: documents come in through
4: //! that module and leave through this one, in the shape a save file or a
5: //! debugging dump wants. Nothing here parses. Every document is assembled from
6: //! the context as it stands, and both are written in a fixed order so that
7: //! asking twice gives the same bytes.
8: 
9: use super::context::LouderContext;
10: use super::name::{LouderInternContext, LouderName};
11: use super::object::LouderVariant;
12: 
13: /// Escape `text` as a JSON string, quotes included.
14: fn quote(text: &str) -> String {
15:   let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len() + 2);
16:   out.push('"');
17:   for c in text.chars() {
18:     match c {
19:       '"' => out.push_str("\\\""),
20:       '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
21:       '\n' => out.push_str("\\n"),
22:       '\r' => out.push_str("\\r"),
23:       '\t' => out.push_str("\\t"),
24:       c if (c as u32) < 0x20 => out.push_str(&format!("\\u{:04x}", c as u32)),
25:       c => out.push(c),
26:     }
27:   }
28:   out.push('"');
29:   out
30: }
31: 
32: /// Render a variant as JSON, resolving strings against the context.
33: fn variant(context: &LouderContext, value: &LouderVariant) -> String {
34:   match value {
35:     LouderVariant::Int(i) => i.to_string(),
36:     LouderVariant::Float(f) => {
37:       // Whole floats print without a trailing `.0` so that round-tripping an
38:       // integer stored as a float does not change how it reads.
39:       if f.fract() == 0.0 && f.is_finite() {
40:         format!("{}", *f as i64)
41:       } else {
42:         f.to_string()
43:       }
44:     }
45:     LouderVariant::String(name) => match context.resolve_name(*name) {
46:       Some(text) => quote(text),
47:       None => quote(""),
48:     },
49:   }
50: }
51: 
52: /// Render a set of name/variant pairs as a JSON object, in ascending key order.
53: fn object(
54:   context: &LouderContext,
55:   keys: &[LouderName],
56:   values: impl Fn(LouderName) -> LouderVariant,
57: ) -> String {
58:   let mut out = String::from("{");
59:   for (index, key) in keys.iter().enumerate() {
60:     if index > 0 {
61:       out.push(',');
62:     }
63:     let name = match context.resolve_name(*key) {
64:       Some(text) => quote(text),
65:       None => quote(""),
66:     };
67:     out.push_str(&format!("{}:{}", name, variant(context, &values(*key))));
68:   }
69:   out.push('}');
70:   out
71: }
72: 
73: /// Render a list of names as a JSON array of strings.
74: ///
75: /// A name with nothing behind it is written as null rather than as an empty
76: /// string, so that a list entry the engine cannot account for stays visible as
77: /// a gap instead of reading as text.
78: fn name_list(context: &LouderContext, names: &[LouderName]) -> String {
79:   let mut out = String::from("[");
80:   for (index, name) in names.iter().enumerate() {
81:     if index > 0 {
82:       out.push(',');
83:     }
84:     match context.resolve_name(*name) {
85:       Some(text) => out.push_str(&quote(text)),
86:       None => out.push_str("null"),
87:     }
88:   }
89:   out.push(']');
90:   out
91: }
92: 
93: /// The whole story state of a conversation, as a JSON object.
94: ///
95: /// Every bucket is included: the conversation's own saved and unsaved variables,
96: /// and the shared ones.
97: pub fn state(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName) -> String {
98:   let keys = context.all_state_keys(conversation);
99:   object(context, &keys, |key| context.get_state(conversation, key))
100: }
101: 
102: /// The story state and every actor of a conversation, as a JSON document.
103: ///
104: /// `None` when the conversation is not loaded, since there is then nothing to
105: /// describe. The document has a `state` object and an `actors` array.
106: pub fn snapshot(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName) -> Option<String> {
107:   context.get_conversation(conversation)?;
108: 
109:   let state = state(context, conversation);
110: 
111:   let mut actors = String::from("[");
112:   let entries = context.actors(conversation);
113:   for (index, entry) in entries.iter().enumerate() {
114:     if index > 0 {
115:       actors.push(',');
116:     }
117: 
118:     let channel = match context.resolve_name(entry.channel()) {
119:       Some(text) => quote(text),
120:       None => "null".to_string(),
121:     };
122: 
123:     let additional = object(context, &entry.additional().sorted_keys(), |key| {
124:       entry.additional_get(key).cloned().unwrap_or_default()
125:     });
126: 
127:     actors.push_str(&format!(
128:       "{{\"id\":{},\"position\":{},\"channel\":{},\"priority\":{},\"flags\":{},\"speak_as\":{},\"listen_to\":{},\"additional\":{}}}",
129:       entry.id(),
130:       entry.position(),
131:       channel,
132:       entry.priority(),
133:       entry.flags().bits(),
134:       name_list(context, entry.speak_as()),
135:       name_list(context, entry.listen_to()),
136:       additional
137:     ));
138:   }
139:   actors.push(']');
140: 
141:   Some(format!("{{\"state\":{},\"actors\":{}}}", state, actors))
142: }
