1: //! The vocabulary of the C boundary.
2: //!
3: //! Everything here exists for the host rather than for the engine: how an
4: //! operation reports that the thing it was asked for is not there, how an
5: //! optional value is handed back, and how bytes cross between a caller's buffer
6: //! and a Rust value. The engine itself never picks an error code or writes into
7: //! memory it does not own, so all of that is kept together in one place and the
8: //! entry points in the crate root are left with nothing to decide.
9: //!
10: //! None of it is reachable from GDScript. The three buffer helpers below exist
11: //! because a C caller owns its own memory and the engine does not; Godot hands
12: //! over a [`String`] that is already copied, so an adapter can take and return
13: //! text directly and never need to ask these questions. [`LouderError`] is the
14: //! one piece of vocabulary that survives, and an adapter is free to name its
15: //! codes however it likes.
16: //!
17: //! Bytes are bytes. Nothing here knows what encoding a caller wrote them in or
18: //! reads them back out, because the engine never displays anything and so has no
19: //! opinion on the matter.
20: 
21: use super::name::{LouderName, LouderNameContent};
22: 
23: /// An error code returned by most operations.
24: ///
25: /// - [`LouderError::Ok`]: no error.
26: /// - [`LouderError::Missing`]: the requested object does not exist.
27: #[repr(usize)]
28: pub enum LouderError {
29:   Ok = 0,
30:   Missing,
31: }
32: 
33: /// Custom Option type exposed to the C FFI
34: #[repr(C)]
35: pub enum LouderOption<T> {
36:   None,
37:   Some(T)
38: }
39: 
40: /// Copy `bytes` into `buffer`, truncating if needed, and null terminate.
41: ///
42: /// Returns the number of bytes written, excluding the terminator. The bytes are
43: /// copied through unchanged, so whatever the caller put in is what comes back
44: /// out; the terminator is a convenience for callers treating the buffer as C
45: /// text, and the return value is what a caller should trust instead.
46: pub(crate) fn write_bytes(buffer: *mut u8, max_size: usize, bytes: &[u8]) -> usize {
47:   if buffer.is_null() {
48:     return 0;
49:   }
50: 
51:   let length = bytes.len().min(max_size);
52: 
53:   unsafe {
54:     if length > 0 {
55:       std::ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(bytes.as_ptr(), buffer, length);
56:     }
57:     *buffer.add(length) = 0;
58:   }
59: 
60:   length
61: }
62: 
63: /// Copy the `max_size` bytes at `buffer` into a name.
64: ///
65: /// `None` if the pointer is null, which is how a caller that has nothing to say
66: /// is told apart from one that said an empty name.
67: pub(crate) fn read_name_content(buffer: *const u8, max_size: usize) -> Option<LouderNameContent> {
68:   if buffer.is_null() {
69:     return None;
70:   }
71: 
72:   Some(LouderNameContent {
73:     bytes: unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(buffer, max_size).to_vec() },
74:   })
75: }
76: 
77: /// Copy the `count` names at `names` into a list.
78: ///
79: /// `None` if the arguments do not describe a list: a negative count is refused
80: /// outright rather than read as an enormous one, and so is a null pointer
81: /// claiming to carry entries. A zero count is an empty list either way, since a
82: /// host with nothing to pass may legitimately pass nothing at all.
83: pub(crate) fn read_name_list(names: *const LouderName, count: i32) -> Option<Vec<LouderName>> {
84:   if count < 0 || (names.is_null() && count > 0) {
85:     return None;
86:   }
87: 
88:   if count == 0 {
89:     return Some(Vec::new());
90:   }
91: 
92:   Some(unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(names, count as usize) }.to_vec())
93: }

1: //! The callback vocabulary.
2: //!
3: //! A callback is named rather than carried: a hub lists the callbacks it should
4: //! run, and the engine interns each of those names and announces them through an
5: //! [`EventData::Callback`](super::event::EventData) event. The host looks the
6: //! name back up and calls whatever it means, which is what keeps the engine from
7: //! having to know anything about the game.
8: //!
9: //! There is no type here, since a callback is only a name: what a host reads
10: //! back out of one is in [`LouderNameContent`](super::name::LouderNameContent),
11: //! and where a callback is announced is in

1: //! The macro an adapter crate uses to turn its `impl` blocks into Godot
2: //! classes, and the feature that turns it off.
3: //!
4: //! The engine declares no Godot classes. What it does instead is hand an adapter
5: //! the one piece of syntax that makes declaring them possible:
6: //! [`louder_rust::godot_api!`](macro@crate::godot_api). An adapter writes
7: //! ordinary inherent `impl` blocks inside it, and the macro puts `#[godot_api]`
8: //! on each one, which registers every `#[func]` method in it with the class the
9: //! block's `Self` names.
10: //!
11: //! ```
12: //! # #[cfg(feature = "godot-bindings")]
13: //! louder_rust::godot_api! {
14: //!   impl Hub {
15: //!     #[func]
16: //!     pub fn get_speaker(&self) -> LouderName {
17: //!       self.inner.speaker()
18: //!     }
19: //!   }
20: //! }
21: //! ```
22: //!
23: //! # Why the classes are not a macro too
24: //!
25: //! The obvious shape is a macro on each `struct`, wrapping the declaration and
26: //! standing in for `#[derive(GodotClass)] #[class(init, tool)]`. That does not
27: //! work here, because a class has to be declared as a plain `struct` with
28: //! attributes the compiler and the derive macro can both see. Writing the two
29: //! attributes out is therefore not a workaround but the only way to say it:
30: //!
31: //! ```
32: //! #[derive(Clone)]
33: //! #[derive(::godot::prelude::GodotClass)]
34: //! #[class(init, tool)]
35: //! pub struct Hub {
36: //!   inner: louder_rust::louder::hub::Hub,
37: //! }
38: //! ```
39: //!
40: //! # Naming
41: //!
42: //! A registered method is named after the Rust function it came from, so
43: //! `pub fn get_speaker` is `get_speaker` in GDScript and nothing else is needed
44: //! to say so. An adapter never writes `#[func(rename = "...")]`: godot-rust
45: //! reads that value as a token stream, so a string literal arrives carrying its
46: //! own quotes and the method ends up registered under `"add_actor"`, reachable
47: //! only through `call`. An adapter that needs a method to read differently in
48: //! GDScript names the Rust function that way instead.
49: //!
50: //! `#[func]` is left in the source either way. `#[godot_api]` consumes it before
51: //! the compiler ever sees it, so it never reaches the build as a dangling
52: //! attribute.
53: //!
54: //! # One block per class
55: //!
56: //! A class may have only one `#[godot_api]` block, since a second would register
57: //! itself over the first. An `IRefCounted` implementation, which is how a class
58: //! with a constructor Godot should be able to call gets one, gets its own.
59: 
60: /// Declares an `impl` block as part of a class's Godot API.
61: ///
62: /// Every `#[func]` method in the block is registered with the class the block's
63: /// `Self` names, which is how an adapter exposes the engine to GDScript.
64: #[cfg(feature = "godot-bindings")]
65: #[macro_export]
66: macro_rules! godot_api {
67:   ( $( #[$attr:meta] )* impl $( $rest:tt )* ) => {
68:     $( #[$attr] )*
69:     #[::godot::prelude::godot_api]
70:     impl $( $rest )*
71:   };
72: }

1: # Schema
2: 
3: The editor can export conversations in json. Conversations are lists of objects
4: with the following formats:
5: 
6: # Hub
7: A position in the conversation.
8:  - conversation_id: a number uniquely identifying this hub
9:  - speaker: the speaker who "says" this hub. Default ""
10:  - text: the text content of this hub. Default ""
11:  - tags: tags that can be used to search for this hub. Separated by commas or whitespace. Default ""
12:  - choices: number of choices going out from this hub. Default 0
13:  - expressions: newline-separated code snippets that can be used to check against the story state before visiting this hub, or check against the story state after visiting this hub for the first time. Default ""
14:  - callbacks: newline-separated command-line style callbacks to the engine to do arbitrary actions after visiting this hub for the first time. Default ""
15:  - flags: integer bitflags of this hub. Affects dialogue flow and can be used to search for hubs by flags. Default 0
16:  - recipients: newline separated speakers. If not empty, overrides dialogue rules about privacy and displays this hub to only these speakers. Default ""
17:  - channel: if not empty, name of the channel this hub changes to. Default ""
18:  - priority: if not 0, the story priority of this hub compared to others in the channel. Default 0
19: 
20: # Connection
21: A connection between two hubs.
22:  - from_id: id of the hub this connection originates from
23:  - from_port: port on the hub this connection originates from
24:  - to_id: the id of the hub this connection connects to
25: 
26: # Speaker
27:  - name: the unique name of this speaker.
28:  - flags: flags of this speaker. Affects how players using this speaker can interact with a conversation. Default 0.
29:  - listening: newline separated list of speakers players using this speaker are considered as "listening to", in addition to the speakers they are already listening to.
30: 
31: # Camera
32:  - position:x: x position of the camera in the editor when last saved
33:  - position:y: y position of the camera in the editor when last saved
34:  - zoom: zoom in the editor when last saved
35: 
36: # Other notes
37: All fields may be empty to indicate default values.
38: 
39: Additional properties are also allowed in the schema and will be read by the engine as "user defined properties" or "additional data". Currently, the editor does not have strong plans to support this but the architecture supports quick experimental changes to the schema, and this additional data system ensures the engine and users of the engine have access to new/renamed fields immediately.

34: // The integer a variant carries, or -1 if it is carrying something else.
35: int32_t AsInt(LouderVariant variant) {
36:   if (variant.tag != LouderVariant::Tag::Int) {
37:     return -1;
38:   }
39:   return variant.int_._0;
40: }

42: // An event with nothing attached to it, carrying only the data given.
43: LouderEvent BareEvent(EventData data) {
44:   LouderEvent event{};
45:   event.conversation = LOUDER_NAME_NONE;
46:   event.channel = LOUDER_NAME_NONE;
47:   event.actor = LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE;
48:   event.data = data;
49:   return event;
50: }

52: // Read a name back out of the engine as a std::string. The engine hands over the
53: // bytes it was given, so the decoding on this side is all there is to it.
54: std::string ReadName(LouderName name) {
55:   std::vector<uint8_t> buffer(4096, 0);
56:   size_t written = read_name(g_ctx, name, buffer.data(), buffer.size() - 1);
57:   return std::string(reinterpret_cast<char*>(buffer.data()), written);
58: }
59: 
60: // Look a name up without creating it.
61: LouderName Lookup(const std::string& text) {
62:   return get_named_id(g_ctx, reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(text.data()), text.size());
63: }
64: 
65: // Declare a name, reusing it if the engine already knows it.
66: LouderName Declare(const std::string& text) {
67:   LouderName existing = get_named_id(g_ctx, reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(text.data()), text.size());
68:   if (existing != LOUDER_NAME_NONE) {
69:     return existing;
70:   }
71:   return declare_name(g_ctx, reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(text.data()), text.size());
72: }

74: std::string ReadJson(LouderName (*fn)(const LouderContext*, LouderName, uint8_t*, size_t),
75:                     LouderName conversation) {
76:   std::vector<uint8_t> buffer(65536, 0);
77:   int32_t written = fn(g_ctx, conversation, buffer.data(), buffer.size() - 1);
78:   if (written < 0) {
79:     return "<error>";
80:   }
81:   return std::string(reinterpret_cast<char*>(buffer.data()), written);
82: }
83: 
84: // Every queued event, drained from index 0 upwards.
85: std::vector<LouderEvent> DrainEvents() {
86:   std::vector<LouderEvent> events;
87:   for (size_t i = 0; ; ++i) {
88:     LouderOption<LouderEvent> option = get_event(g_ctx, i);
89:     if (option.tag == LouderOption<LouderEvent>::Tag::None) {
90:       break;
91:     }
92:     events.push_back(option.some._0);
93:   }
94:   flush_events(g_ctx);
95:   return events;
96: }
97: 
98: int CountEventsOfKind(const std::vector<LouderEvent>& events, EventData::Tag tag) {
99:   int count = 0;
100:   for (const auto& event : events) {
101:     if (event.data.tag == tag) {
102:       ++count;
103:     }
104:   }
105:   return count;
106: }
107: 
108: // The move an actor made onto a hub, if it made one.
109: //
110: // Every kind of move carries the same motion, so the search covers all of them:
111: // what a test is looking for is where somebody went and who the hub was heard
112: // as while they were going there.
113: bool FindMotion(const std::vector<LouderEvent>& events, LouderActor actor, LouderPosition target,
114:                 Motion* found) {
115:   for (const auto& event : events) {
116:     if (event.actor != actor) {
117:       continue;
118:     }
119: 
120:     const Motion* motion = nullptr;
121:     if (event.data.tag == EventData::Tag::RegularMotion) {
122:       motion = &event.data.regular_motion._0;
123:     } else if (event.data.tag == EventData::Tag::Warp) {
124:       motion = &event.data.warp._0;
125:     } else if (event.data.tag == EventData::Tag::ForceMotion) {
126:       motion = &event.data.force_motion._0;
127:     }
128: 
129:     if (motion != nullptr && motion->target_position == target) {
130:       *found = *motion;
131:       return true;
132:     }
133:   }
134: 
135:   return false;
136: }
137: 
138: // Every load error in the queue, as (position, error) pairs.
139: //
140: // The order matters only in that it is the order the loader reported them in,
141: // which is the order the records appear in the document.
142: std::vector<LoadFailure> DrainLoadErrors() {
143:   std::vector<LoadFailure> failures;
144:   for (const auto& event : DrainEvents()) {
145:     if (event.data.tag == EventData::Tag::LoadError) {
146:       failures.push_back(event.data.load_error._0);
147:     }
148:   }
149:   return failures;
150: }

152: // The rules the engine is running, as it reports them back.
153: DialogueRules CurrentRules() { return get_dialogue_rules(g_ctx); }
154: 
155: // Run `body` with only `rules` switched on, putting the old ones back after.
156: //
157: // The rules live on the context rather than on a conversation, so a test that
158: // changed them without restoring would be answering for every test after it.
159: template <typename Body>
160: void WithRules(DialogueRules rules, Body body) {
161:   DialogueRules previous = get_dialogue_rules(g_ctx);
162:   CHECK(set_dialogue_rules(g_ctx, rules) == 0, "the rules should be settable");
163:   body();
164:   set_dialogue_rules(g_ctx, previous);
165: }

171:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":9,"text":"The end","callbacks":"exit","flags":0},

186: // A private hub whose speaker is not its recipient. Hub 5 is spoken by A but
187: // addressed to B, so B owns it and may lead it without speaking it.
188: //
189: // Every speaker here is non-public, so nothing reaches an actor by default: an
190: // actor only sees or leads what it actually hears. That way a passing test
191: // cannot be an accident of a public speaker leaking the hub.
192: const char* PrivateJson() {
193:   return R"([
194:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":0,"speaker":"A","text":"ask","tags":"start","flags":0},
195:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":5,"speaker":"A","text":"aside","recipients":"B","choices":"1","flags":0},
196:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":9,"speaker":"C","text":"secret","flags":0},
197:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":0,"to_id":5},
198:     {"type":"connection","from_id":5,"from_port":0,"to_id":9}
199:   ])";
200: }
201: 
202: // B listens to A and to C, and both for the same reason: an actor may only
203: // decide from a hub that appears to it, and may only take a choice that leads
204: // somewhere that appears to it. So B hears A's speech in order to stand on hub 0
205: // and walk into the aside addressed to it, and hears C in order to lead out of
206: // that aside and into the secret. A and C hear nobody but themselves, which is
207: // what leaves them unable to hear anything else.
208: const char* PrivateConfigJson() {
209:   return R"([
210:     {"type":"speaker","name":"A","flags":0},
211:     {"type":"speaker","name":"B","flags":0,"listening":"A\nC"},
212:     {"type":"speaker","name":"C","flags":0}
213:   ])";
214: }

337: // A hub is gated by its conditions, and a condition is allowed to combine
338: // values. The guide's truthiness rules are 0, false and the empty string, so a
339: // string that is neither empty nor "0" is true even where it stands next to a
340: // number. Visibility is decided by the read-only pass and reported by the
341: // travelling pass, so both have to agree: this checks that they do.
342: void TestConditionsUseTheGuidesTruthinessRules() {
343:   std::printf("condition truthiness\n");
344: 
345:   clear_config(g_ctx);
346:   const char* config = R"([{"type":"speaker","name":"A","flags":1}])";
347:   CHECK(load_conversation_config(g_ctx, reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(const_cast<char*>(config)),
348:                                  std::strlen(config)) == 0,
349:         "the config should load");
350: 
351:   const char* document = R"([
352:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":0,"speaker":"A","text":"start","flags":0},
353:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":1,"speaker":"A","text":"shown","expressions":"gate && 1","flags":0},
354:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":2,"speaker":"A","text":"hidden","expressions":"gate && 1","flags":0},
355:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":0,"to_id":1},
356:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":1,"to_id":2}
357:   ])";
358: 
359:   LouderName conversation = Declare("condition conversation");
360:   CHECK(load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation,
361:                                reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(const_cast<char*>(document)),
362:                                std::strlen(document)) == 0,
363:         "the conversation should load");
364: 
365:   LouderName speaker[1] = {Lookup("A")};
366:   LouderActor actor = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speaker, 1, nullptr, 0);
367:   CHECK(actor == 0, "the actor should get id 0, got %d", actor);
368: 
369:   LouderName gate = Declare("gate");
370: 
371:   // An unwritten variable reads as 0, so neither hub appears.
372:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor) == 0,
373:         "an unwritten gate should close both choices, saw %d",
374:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor));
375: 
376:   // A non-empty string is true, so both conditions hold.
377:   set_story_state(g_ctx, conversation, gate, make_variant_string(Declare("hello")));
378:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor) == 2,
379:         "a non-empty string should be truthy next to a number, saw %d choices",
380:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor));
381: 
382:   // The empty string is false, which is what the guide says about it.
383:   set_story_state(g_ctx, conversation, gate, make_variant_string(Declare("")));
384:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor) == 0,
385:         "an empty string should be falsy, saw %d choices",
386:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor));
387: 
388:   // And so is the string "0", which is what lets a bare string act like a number.
389:   set_story_state(g_ctx, conversation, gate, make_variant_string(Declare("0")));
390:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor) == 0,
391:         "the string \"0\" should be falsy, saw %d choices",
392:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor));
393: 
394:   remove_conversation(g_ctx, conversation);
395:   clear_config(g_ctx);
396: }

432:   for (LouderActor id = next_actor_speaking_as(g_ctx, conversation, a, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE);
433:        id != LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE; id = next_actor_speaking_as(g_ctx, conversation, a, id)) {
434:     found.push_back(id);
435:   }
436:   CHECK(found.size() == 1 && found[0] == actor_a, "only the first actor speaks as A");
437: 
438:   // A spectator sees the hub but is not given choices to decide with.
439:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, spectator) == 0,
440:         "a spectator should see no choices, saw %d", get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, spectator));
441: 
442:   // Unless it is also a debug actor.
443:   LouderActor debug = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags_SPECTATOR | ActorFlags_DEBUG,
444:                                 nullptr, 0, nullptr, 0);
445:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, debug) == 1,
446:         "a spectator that is also a debug actor should see the choice");
447: 
448:   CHECK(debug == 3, "the debug actor should get id 3, got %d", debug);
449: 
450:   // An exact match needs every bit to agree, so the debug actor is only found
451:   // by naming both of its flags.
452:   CHECK(next_actor_from_flags(g_ctx, conversation, ActorFlags_DEBUG, 1, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE) == LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE,
453:         "an exact DEBUG-only match should not find a spectator that is also debug");
454:   CHECK(next_actor_from_flags(g_ctx, conversation,
455:                               ActorFlags_SPECTATOR | ActorFlags_DEBUG, 1,
456:                               LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE) == debug,
457:         "the debug spectator should be found by an exact match on both flags");
458: 
459:   // A non-exact match only requires the named bits to be present.
460:   CHECK(next_actor_from_flags(g_ctx, conversation, ActorFlags_DEBUG, 0, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE) == debug,
461:         "a containing match on DEBUG should find the debug spectator");
462:   CHECK(next_actor_from_flags(g_ctx, conversation, ActorFlags_SPECTATOR, 1, LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE) == spectator,
463:         "the plain spectator should be found by an exact flag match");
464: 
465:   CHECK(remove_actor(g_ctx, conversation, actor_b) == 0, "removing an actor should succeed");
466:   CHECK(remove_actor(g_ctx, conversation, actor_b) != 0, "removing it twice should fail");
467:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor_b) == -1, "a removed actor should not resolve");
468: }
469: 
470: void TestChoices() {
471:   std::printf("choices and events\n");
472: 
473:   LouderName conversation = Declare("choice conversation");
474:   std::string json = ConversationJson();
475:   LoadError error = load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation,
476:                                            reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&json[0]), json.size());
477:   CHECK(error == 0, "loading the conversation returned %d", error);
478: 
479:   LouderName a = Lookup("A");
480:   LouderName speak_a[1] = {a};
481:   LouderActor actor = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_a, 1, nullptr, 0);
482:   CHECK(actor == 0, "the actor should get id 0");
483: 
484:   flush_events(g_ctx);
485: 
486:   // Hub 0 links to hub 5, which is also spoken by A, so the choice is available.
487:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor) == 1,
488:         "the actor should see exactly one choice, saw %d", get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor));
489:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor, 0) == 5, "choice 0 should link to hub 5");
490:   CHECK(ReadName(get_hub_choice_text(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor, 0)) == "World",
491:         "a choice's text comes from the hub it links to");
492:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor, 5) == -1, "an out of range choice should not resolve");
493: 
494:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 1) != 0, "an unavailable choice should be refused");
495:   flush_events(g_ctx);
496: 
497:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 0) == 0, "selecting the available choice should succeed");
498:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor) == 5, "the actor should have moved to hub 5");
499: 
500:   std::vector<LouderEvent> events = DrainEvents();
501:   int appearances = 0;
502:   int choice_appearances = 0;
503:   for (const auto& event : events) {
504:     if (event.data.tag == EventData::Tag::RegularMotion) {
505:       if (event.data.regular_motion._0.target_position == 5) {
506:         ++appearances;
507:       }
508:     }
509:     if (event.data.tag == EventData::Tag::ChoiceAppearance) {
510:       ++choice_appearances;
511:     }
512:   }
513:   CHECK(appearances == 1, "one hub appearance should be emitted, saw %d", appearances);
514:   CHECK(choice_appearances == 1, "one choice appearance should be emitted, saw %d", choice_appearances);
515: 
516:   // Taking the last choice reaches the hub carrying the callback.
517:   flush_events(g_ctx);
518:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 0) == 0, "the actor should be able to continue");
519:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor) == 9, "the actor should have reached hub 9");
520: 
521:   events = DrainEvents();
522:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Callback) == 1,
523:         "the callback should fire once, saw %d", CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Callback));
524: 
525:   // Callbacks are one-shot: revisiting the hub must not fire them again.
526:   flush_events(g_ctx);
527:   force_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 5, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, 0);
528:   force_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 9, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, 0);
529:   events = DrainEvents();
530:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Callback) == 0,
531:         "a revisited hub should not refire its callback, saw %d",
532:         CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Callback));
533: }
534: 
535: // Hubs 1 and 2 are Auto and linked to each other, so an actor entering either
536: // one walks the pair forever unless the engine refuses to re-enter an auto hub
537: // it has already resolved through. Hub 4 is the control: an Auto hub with an
538: // exit, which should still resolve. Hub 6 is the third shape: an Auto hub with
539: // no choices at all, which has nowhere to resolve to.
540: const char* AutoCycleJson() {
541:   return R"([
542:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":0,"text":"Start","flags":0},
543:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":1,"text":"Loop A","flags":1},
544:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":2,"text":"Loop B","flags":1},
545:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":3,"text":"Out","flags":0},
546:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":4,"text":"Step","flags":1},
547:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":6,"text":"Nowhere","flags":1},
548:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":0,"to_id":1},
549:     {"type":"connection","from_id":1,"from_port":0,"to_id":2},
550:     {"type":"connection","from_id":2,"from_port":0,"to_id":1},
551:     {"type":"connection","from_id":4,"from_port":0,"to_id":3}
552:   ])";
553: }
554: 
555: // Hub 0 has five choices, and no two actors are offered all of them. Hub 4 is
556: // Whisper's private voice, so it reaches only an actor that listens to Whisper,
557: // which leaves a hole in the middle of the available indices rather than a
558: // suffix. That is the shape a host has to be careful about: the visible list is
559: // numbered from zero, but the engine's indices are not.
560: const char* GappedChoicesJson() {
561:   return R"([
562:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":0,"speaker":"A","text":"start","flags":0},
563:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":1,"speaker":"A","text":"a","flags":0},
564:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":2,"speaker":"B","text":"b","flags":0},
565:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":3,"speaker":"C","text":"c","flags":0},
566:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":4,"speaker":"Whisper","text":"whisper","flags":0},
567:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":5,"speaker":"A","text":"e","flags":0},
568:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":0,"to_id":1},
569:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":1,"to_id":2},
570:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":2,"to_id":3},
571:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":3,"to_id":4},
572:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":4,"to_id":5}
573:   ])";
574: }
575: 
576: // Only B listens to Whisper. A and C hear the public voices and nothing else.
577: const char* GappedChoicesConfigJson() {
578:   return R"([
579:     {"type":"speaker","name":"A","flags":1},
580:     {"type":"speaker","name":"B","flags":1,"listening":"Whisper"},
581:     {"type":"speaker","name":"C","flags":1},
582:     {"type":"speaker","name":"Whisper","flags":0}
583:   ])";
584: }
585: 
586: // Two actors in one channel choosing against each other. Actor 0 moving makes
587: // actor 1 a follower, and actor 1 moving would make actor 0 a follower again,
588: // so an unguarded engine recurses until the stack gives out.
589: void TestWarpRecursion() {
590:   std::printf("warp recursion\n");
591: 
592:   LouderName conversation = Declare("warp conversation");
593:   std::string json = ConversationJson();
594:   LoadError error = load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation,
595:                                            reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&json[0]), json.size());
596:   CHECK(error == 0, "loading the conversation returned %d", error);
597: 
598:   LouderName a = Lookup("A");
599:   LouderName b = Lookup("B");
600:   LouderName speak_a[1] = {a};
601:   LouderName speak_b[1] = {b};
602: 
603:   // Both start on the main channel at the same hub, which is the setup from the
604:   // engine guide: they can each see where the other lands.
605:   LouderActor first = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_a, 1, nullptr, 0);
606:   LouderActor second = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_b, 1, nullptr, 0);
607:   CHECK(first == 0 && second == 1, "the two actors should get ids 0 and 1, got %d and %d", first, second);
608:   CHECK(ReadName(get_actor_channel(g_ctx, conversation, second)) == "main",
609:         "both actors should start on the main channel");
610: 
611:   flush_events(g_ctx);
612: 
613:   // Hub 5 has no speaker, so it is public and appears to the second actor. The
614:   // move must terminate rather than trading the pair back and forth.
615:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, first, 0) == 0, "the choice should be accepted");
616:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, first) == 5, "the chooser should have moved to hub 5");
617:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, second) == 5,
618:         "the follower should have been warped to hub 5, saw %d",
619:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, second));
620: 
621:   // The warp is reported as a warp rather than as the follower choosing, which
622:   // is how a host tells the two apart.
623:   std::vector<LouderEvent> events = DrainEvents();
624:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Warp) == 1,
625:         "the follower should be announced with one warp event, saw %d",
626:         CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Warp));
627:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::RegularMotion) == 1,
628:         "the chooser should be announced with one regular motion, saw %d",
629:         CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::RegularMotion));
630: 
631:   // Having settled together, neither should pull the other anywhere on the
632:   // next choice, and the cascade should still terminate.
633:   flush_events(g_ctx);
634:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, second, 0) == 0, "the second actor should be able to choose");
635:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, first) == 9, "the first actor should follow to hub 9");
636:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, second) == 9, "the second actor should be at hub 9");
637:   events = DrainEvents();
638:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Warp) == 1,
639:         "exactly one actor should have been warped, saw %d",
640:         CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Warp));
641: 
642:   // A third actor forced into the same channel starts a fresh cascade, and the
643:   // guide says the others follow it, so both are expected to be pulled along
644:   // exactly once each.
645:   flush_events(g_ctx);
646:   LouderActor third = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_b, 1, nullptr, 0);
647:   CHECK(force_position(g_ctx, conversation, third, 5, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, 0) == 0,
648:         "a third actor should be able to move into the settled flow");
649:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, third) == 5, "the forced actor should be at hub 5");
650:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, first) == 5,
651:         "the first actor should have followed to hub 5, saw %d",
652:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, first));
653:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, second) == 5,
654:         "the second actor should have followed to hub 5, saw %d",
655:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, second));
656: 
657:   events = DrainEvents();
658:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Warp) == 2,
659:         "each of the two settled actors should be warped exactly once, saw %d",
660:         CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::Warp));
661:   CHECK(CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::ForceMotion) == 1,
662:         "only the forced actor should report a forced motion, saw %d",
663:         CountEventsOfKind(events, EventData::Tag::ForceMotion));
664: }
665: 
666: // An auto hub that leads back to itself, directly or through a partner, has no
667: // exit. The engine has to give up on it rather than resolving it forever.
668: void TestAutoCycle() {
669:   std::printf("auto hub cycle\n");
670: 
671:   LouderName conversation = Declare("auto cycle conversation");
672:   std::string json = AutoCycleJson();
673:   LoadError error = load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation,
674:                                            reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&json[0]), json.size());
675:   CHECK(error == 0, "loading the conversation returned %d", error);
676: 
677:   LouderName speak_a[1] = {Lookup("A")};
678:   LouderActor actor = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_a, 1, nullptr, 0);
679:   CHECK(actor == 0, "the actor should get id 0");
680: 
681:   flush_events(g_ctx);
682: 
683:   // Hub 0 links to the auto cycle, so entering it walks 1 -> 2 -> 1 and has to
684:   // stop rather than spin.
685:   CHECK(force_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 1, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, 0) != 0,
686:         "entering a closed cycle of auto hubs should be refused");
687:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor) == 0,
688:         "a refused move should leave the actor where it was, saw %d",
689:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor));
690: 
691:   // The refusal is about the cycle, not about auto hubs generally: hub 4 is
692:   // auto as well but leads out to hub 3, so it resolves normally.
693:   flush_events(g_ctx);
694:   CHECK(force_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 4, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, 0) == 0,
695:         "an auto hub with an exit should resolve");
696:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor) == 3,
697:         "resolving through hub 4 should land on hub 3, saw %d",
698:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor));
699: 
700:   // An auto hub is never something an actor can stand on, but choosing *into* one
701:   // is how the auto logic gets entered. It is offered, and the move is then
702:   // refused only because this hub has no way out.
703:   flush_events(g_ctx);
704:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor) == 1,
705:         "a choice into an auto hub should be offered, since entering one is how the actor moves, "
706:         "saw %d", get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor));
707:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 0) != 0,
708:         "but choosing into a closed cycle of auto hubs should be refused");
709:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor) == 3,
710:         "a refused choice should leave the actor where it was, saw %d",
711:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor));
712: 
713:   // Hub 6 is auto with no choices at all, so there is nothing for it to resolve
714:   // to. That is a dead end rather than a cycle, and it has to be refused the
715:   // same way: no state may be left in which an actor is standing somewhere it
716:   // can never leave.
717:   flush_events(g_ctx);
718:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 6, actor) == 0,
719:         "the premise is an auto hub with nothing to resolve to, saw %d choices",
720:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 6, actor));
721:   CHECK(force_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor, 6, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, 0) != 0,
722:         "entering an auto hub with no exit at all should be refused");
723:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor) == 3,
724:         "and the refusal should leave the actor where it was, saw %d",
725:         get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor));
726: }
727: 
728: // A host discovers which choices to show by probing indices, because the engine
729: // has no call for a hub's raw choice count and refuses an out-of-range index and
730: // an unavailable one identically. The contract that makes that possible is that
731: // get_hub_choice_count agrees with how many indices probing actually finds, so it
732: // is checked at every hub for every kind of actor rather than on one lucky path.
733: void TestChoiceCountAgreesWithProbing() {
734:   std::printf("choice count agrees with probing\n");
735: 
736:   LouderName conversation = Declare("probing conversation");
737:   std::string json = GappedChoicesJson();
738:   CHECK(load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation, reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&json[0]),
739:                                json.size()) == 0,
740:         "loading the gapped conversation returned %d", 0);
741: 
742:   LouderName a = Lookup("A");
743:   LouderActor speaking = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), &a, 1, nullptr, 0);
744:   LouderActor debug = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags_SPECTATOR | ActorFlags_DEBUG,
745:                                 nullptr, 0, nullptr, 0);
746: 
747:   int hubs_offering_choices = 0;
748:   for (LouderPosition position = 0; position < 16; ++position) {
749:     for (LouderActor actor : {speaking, debug}) {
750:       const int32_t expected = get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, position, actor);
751:       int32_t found = 0;
752:       for (int32_t index = 0; index < 64; ++index) {
753:         if (get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, position, actor, index) != -1) {
754:           ++found;
755:         }
756:       }
757:       CHECK(found == expected,
758:             "at hub %d for actor %d the engine counts %d choices but probing finds %d", position,
759:             actor, expected, found);
760:       if (expected > 0) {
761:         ++hubs_offering_choices;
762:       }
763:     }
764:   }
765:   CHECK(hubs_offering_choices > 0, "the fixture should have hubs that offer choices at all");
766: }
767: 
768: // The engine's choice indices are positions in the hub's full choice list, and
769: // the ones available to an actor need not be contiguous. So a host cannot use
770: // the position on screen as the index it passes back: the second thing A is shown
771: // below is engine index 4.
772: void TestAvailableChoiceIndicesCanHaveGaps() {
773:   std::printf("available choice indices can have gaps\n");
774: 
775:   clear_config(g_ctx);
776:   const char* config = GappedChoicesConfigJson();
777:   CHECK(load_conversation_config(g_ctx, reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(const_cast<char*>(config)),
778:                                  std::strlen(config)) == 0,
779:         "the gapped config should load");
780: 
781:   LouderName conversation = Declare("gapped conversation");
782:   std::string json = GappedChoicesJson();
783:   CHECK(load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation, reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&json[0]),
784:                                json.size()) == 0,
785:         "loading the gapped conversation returned %d", 0);
786: 
787:   LouderName speak_a[1] = {Lookup("A")};
788:   LouderName speak_b[1] = {Lookup("B")};
789:   LouderName speak_c[1] = {Lookup("C")};
790: 
791:   // All three actors are added before anything is asked, because who is in the
792:   // conversation is part of the answer: once an actor is playing a voice, nobody
793:   // else may lead it.
794:   LouderActor actor_a = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_a, 1, nullptr, 0);
795:   LouderActor actor_b = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_b, 1, nullptr, 0);
796:   LouderActor actor_c = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_c, 1, nullptr, 0);
797:   CHECK(actor_a == 0 && actor_b == 1 && actor_c == 2, "the actors should be 0, 1 and 2, got %d, %d, %d",
798:         actor_a, actor_b, actor_c);
799: 
800:   // A speaks as A, so it leads both of A's branches, at indices 0 and 4. B and C
801:   // hold their own voices and Whisper is not A's to hear, so the three indices
802:   // in between are closed to it. What A is shown is two choices, the second of
803:   // which is engine index 4.
804:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_a) == 2,
805:         "A should be offered two of the five, saw %d", get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0,
806:                                                                               actor_a));
807:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_a, 0) == 1,
808:         "A's first choice should lead to hub 1");
809:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_a, 3) == -1,
810:         "index 3 leads to Whisper's voice, which A does not hear");
811:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_a, 4) == 5,
812:         "and A's second choice is engine index 4, not 1, and leads to hub 5");
813: 
814:   // B leads its own voice and Whisper's, at indices 1 and 3, and is refused A's
815:   // and C's even though both speakers are public.
816:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_b) == 2,
817:         "B should be offered two of the five, saw %d", get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0,
818:                                                                               actor_b));
819:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_b, 1) == 2,
820:         "B's first choice should lead to hub 2");
821:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_b, 3) == 4,
822:         "B listens to Whisper, so its second choice is engine index 3 and leads to hub 4");
823: 
824:   // C holds only its own voice, and is left with the single choice at index 2.
825:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_c) == 1,
826:         "C should be offered just its own, saw %d", get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0,
827:                                                                          actor_c));
828:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_link(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_c, 2) == 3,
829:         "C's only choice should be engine index 2, leading to hub 3");
830: 
831:   remove_conversation(g_ctx, conversation);
832:   clear_config(g_ctx);
833: }
834: 
835: void TestPrivateHubIsOwnedByItsRecipients() {
836:   std::printf("private hub ownership\n");
837: 
838:   // This fixture only means anything under its own config. Another one would
839:   // leave speaker C undefined, and an undefined speaker is public, so every hub
840:   // below would reach every actor and the blocks would pass for reasons that
841:   // have nothing to do with ownership. Loading it here, rather than inheriting
842:   // whatever an earlier test left, is what makes the rest of this meaningful.
843:   clear_config(g_ctx);
844:   CHECK(load_conversation_config(
845:             g_ctx,
846:             reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(const_cast<char*>(PrivateConfigJson())),
847:             std::strlen(PrivateConfigJson())) == 0,
848:         "the private hub config should load");
849: 
850:   LouderName conversation = Declare("private conversation");
851:   std::string json = PrivateJson();
852:   CHECK(load_conversation_json(g_ctx, conversation,
853:                                reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(&json[0]), json.size()) == 0,
854:         "loading the private conversation returned %d", 0);
855: 
856:   LouderName a = Lookup("A");
857:   LouderName b = Lookup("B");
858:   LouderName c = Lookup("C");
859:   CHECK(c != LOUDER_NAME_NONE, "hub 9's speaker should be interned");
860: 
861:   // The flags alone cannot show that the config above is the one in effect: an

869:   CHECK(get_speaker_flags(g_ctx, c).bits != SpeakerFlags_PUBLIC.bits,

871:   CHECK(get_next_speaker_listen_to(g_ctx, b, LOUDER_NAME_NONE) == a &&

873:         "B should listen to A then C; if this fails then PrivateConfigJson is not the config "
874:         "in effect");

876:   // Hub 5 is spoken by A and addressed to B. With a B actor alone, hub 5 must be

893:     LouderName speak_a[1] = {a};

899:     // The choice into it from hub 0 is equally closed to A.

942: // The worked example from the engine guide's "Example Flows" section, verbatim:
943: //
944: //   Hubs 0, 1, 2, 3, 4.
945: //   Speakers: 2: "Speaker A", 3: "Speaker B"

948: //             2: "Finish Conversation" -> 4

950: //   Channels: 2: "Channel A"
951: //   Tags:     0: "Start"
952: //   Callbacks: 4: "exit"
953: //
954: // Two actors, one speaking as A and one as B, both starting on the start hub in
955: // the main channel.

957:   return R"([
958:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":0,"speaker":"A","text":"Ask something","tags":"Start","flags":0},
959:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":1,"text":"","choices":"2","flags":1},
960:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":2,"speaker":"A","text":"Finish Conversation","channel":"Channel A","choices":"1","flags":0},
961:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":3,"speaker":"B","text":"B's answer","flags":0},
962:     {"type":"hub","conversation_id":4,"text":"The end","callbacks":"exit","flags":0},
963:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":0,"to_id":1},
964:     {"type":"connection","from_id":1,"from_port":0,"to_id":2},
965:     {"type":"connection","from_id":1,"from_port":1,"to_id":3},
966:     {"type":"connection","from_id":2,"from_port":0,"to_id":4}
967:   ])";
968: }
969:

972:     {"type":"speaker","name":"A","flags":1},
973:     {"type":"speaker","name":"B","flags":1},
974:     {"type":"speaker","name":"Channel A","flags":0}
975:   ])";
976: }

978: // The guide's worked example, checked move by move. The point it pins down is
979: // the divergence: both actors choose the same thing from hub 0, both enter auto
980: // hub 1, and auto logic then resolves each of them *separately*, so actor 0 lands
981: // on hub 2 and actor 1 on hub 3. A follower that replayed the leader's choice
982: // instead would have copied hub 2, and the conversation would have collapsed
983: // into one actor talking to itself.
984: void TestExampleFlow() {
985:   std::printf("example flow from the guide\n");
986: 
987:   clear_config(g_ctx);

989:             g_ctx,

991:             std::strlen(ExampleFlowConfigJson())) == 0,
992:         "the example flow config should load");

995:   std::string json = ExampleFlowJson();

998:         "loading the example flow conversation returned %d", 0);

1000:   LouderName a = Lookup("A");
1001:   LouderName b = Lookup("B");

1003:   LouderName speak_b[1] = {b};
1004: 
1005:   // The guide has actor 0 speaking as A and actor 1 speaking as B, both on the
1006:   // start hub.
1007:   LouderActor actor_a = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_a, 1, nullptr, 0);
1008:   LouderActor actor_b = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags(), speak_b, 1, nullptr, 0);
1009:   CHECK(actor_a == 0 && actor_b == 1, "the actors should be 0 and 1, got %d and %d", actor_a, actor_b);
1010: 
1011:   // A debug spectator, as the guide describes, to watch the auto hub arrive.
1012:   LouderActor debug = add_actor(g_ctx, conversation, 0, ActorFlags_SPECTATOR | ActorFlags_DEBUG,
1013:                                 nullptr, 0, nullptr, 0);
1014: 
1015:   flush_events(g_ctx);
1016: 
1017:   // Both actors see hub 0's single choice, and the auto hub it leads to is

1019:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_a) == 1,
1020:         "actor 0 should be offered the choice out of hub 0, saw %d",
1021:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_a));
1022:   CHECK(get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_b) == 1,
1023:         "actor 1 should be offered the same choice, saw %d",
1024:         get_hub_choice_count(g_ctx, conversation, 0, actor_b));
1025: 
1026:   // Client 0 sends the choice at index 0.
1027:   CHECK(select_choice(g_ctx, conversation, actor_a, 0) == 0,
1028:         "actor 0's choice should be accepted");
1029: 
1030:   // Auto logic for actor 0: "(invisible choice 1)" links to hub 2, which is
1031:   // spoken by A, and actor 0 speaks as A, so that branch is its to take.
1032:   CHECK(get_actor_position(g_ctx, conversation, actor_a) == 2,

1: # $ErrorActionPreference is set deliberately, and only here. A caller may have it
2: # on Stop, which turns anything cargo writes to stderr — including its ordinary
3: # "Compiling" and "warning:" lines — into a terminating error, so a script that
4: # worked from one directory fails from another for reasons that have nothing to
5: # do with the build. Every step below is judged on $LASTEXITCODE instead, which
6: # says whether the command actually failed.
7: $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
8: 
9: $engine = $PSScriptRoot
10: 
11: # The engine alone: no adapter, and so no Godot in the build. `louder_godot` is a
12: # separate package in this workspace and is deliberately not built here, since
13: # what the C++ tests exercise is the C boundary and nothing above it.
14: Push-Location $engine
15: cargo build -p louder_rust
16: $buildExit = $LASTEXITCODE
17: Pop-Location
18: if ($buildExit -ne 0) { throw "the engine failed to build with exit code $buildExit" }
19: 
20: $built = Join-Path $engine "target\debug\louder_rust.dll"
21: $header = Join-Path $engine "target\debug\louder_rust.h"
22: 
23: # cbindgen runs on the crate directory, since its config and its output path are
24: # both written relative to one.
25: Push-Location (Join-Path $engine "louder_rust")
26: cbindgen --config cbindgen.toml --crate louder_rust --output (Join-Path $engine "target\debug\louder_rust.h")
27: $cbindgenExit = $LASTEXITCODE
28: Pop-Location
29: if ($cbindgenExit -ne 0) { throw "cbindgen failed with exit code $cbindgenExit" }
30: 
31: Copy-Item -LiteralPath $built -Destination (Join-Path $engine "artifacts\louder_rust.dll") -Force
32: Copy-Item -LiteralPath $header -Destination (Join-Path $engine "artifacts\louder_rust.h") -Force
33: Copy-Item -LiteralPath $built -Destination (Join-Path $engine "tests\louder_rust.dll") -Force
34: Copy-Item -LiteralPath $header -Destination (Join-Path $engine "tests\louder_rust.h") -Force
35: 
36: # Compile the C++ tests against the freshly generated header and DLL, then run
37: # them. The header is an output, so the tests always exercise the current ABI.
38: Push-Location $engine
39: g++ -std=c++17 -I artifacts tests/engine_test.cpp -o artifacts/engine_test.exe artifacts/louder_rust.dll
40: if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "failed to compile the engine tests" }
41: 
42: # The DLL sits beside the test binary, which is where Windows looks first.
43: & artifacts/engine_test.exe
44: if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "engine tests failed with exit code $LASTEXITCODE" }
45: Pop-Location

Read file C:\Users\friba\Documents\lang_experiments\godot_experiments\custom-graph\engine\Cargo.toml, lines 1-14
1: # The engine and the adapters that expose it.
2: #
3: # `louder_rust` is the engine itself: a plain Rust library with a C boundary, and
4: # nothing else. `louder_godot` is the Godot adapter, and depends on the engine as
5: # an ordinary library. Keeping them in one workspace means the engine's
6: # dependencies and the adapter's are built once and share a target directory, so
7: # a build of the Godot extension is a build of the engine with the adapter on
8: # top rather than two independent builds.
9: #
10: # A third adapter, for another language, is a member here that depends on
11: # `louder_rust` the same way `louder_godot` does. It does not change the engine.
12: [workspace]
13: members = ["louder_rust", "louder_godot"]
14: resolver = "3"

1121: // The shape the demo conversation's auto hub 27 has, which is the case that
1122: // needs both halves of the auto rules at once:
1123: //
1124: //   0: -> 27 (auto) -> [0] 29 (private voice)  [1] 28 (public, spoken by B)
1125: //   2: -> 26
1126: //
1127: // B listens to a private voice, which is only B's. When B chooses into the auto
1128: // hub, auto logic takes the first branch available to B, which is 29 because B
1129: // hears it. B cannot see the second branch as unavailable-for-B (B speaks 28),
1130: // so this pins that a multi-choice auto hub still resolves for the actor who
1131: // owns the branch it leads to.
1132: //
1133: // A second actor who does not hear the private voice must not be routed to 29.
1134: // When B leaves the auto hub, that actor is warped through 27 and emulates it as
1135: // itself. It has no claim on branch 0 (29 does not appear to it), and branch 1
1136: // (28, public, spoken by B) is B's, so emulation falls back to the first branch
1137: // that appears: 28. That is the whole point of the split: B sees their own
1138: // private voice, the other actor sees only the public line.

1146:     {"type":"connection","from_id":0,"from_port":0,"to_id":26},
1147:     {"type":"connection","from_id":26,"from_port":0,"to_id":27},
1148:     {"type":"connection","from_id":27,"from_port":0,"to_id":29},
1149:     {"type":"connection","from_id":27,"from_port":1,"to_id":28}
1150:   ])";
1151: }
