60: /// Load a conversation from a JSON document.
61: ///
62: /// The document must be a list of objects. Records whose `type` is `hub`
63: /// become hubs, `connection` records become choices, and `camera` records are
64: /// ignored. Problems with individual records are reported through events
65: /// rather than failing the whole load, so one malformed hub does not cost the
66: /// caller the rest of the conversation.
67: pub fn load_conversation(
68:   context: &mut LouderContext,
69:   conversation: LouderName,
70:   document: &str,
71: ) -> LoadError {
72:   if context.get_conversation(conversation).is_some() {
73:     return LOUDER_LOAD_ALREADY_LOADED;
74:   }
75: 
76:   let records = match parse_records(context, conversation, document) {
77:     Ok(records) => records,
78:     Err(error) => return error,
79:   };
80: 
81:   let mut loaded = Conversation::new(conversation);
82:   // Connections are collected before being resolved because a choice takes its
83:   // text from the hub it links to, which may not have been read yet.
84:   let mut connections: Map<LouderPosition, Vec<(usize, LouderPosition)>> = Map::new();
85: 
86:   for record in records {
87:     let Some(object) = record.as_object() else {
88:       report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, BLANK_POSITION);
89:       continue;
90:     };
91: 
92:     match record_type(object) {
93:       Some("hub") => {
94:         let position = position_of(object);
95:         let hub = read_hub(context, object);
96:         if loaded.add_hub(hub) {
97:           // Two hubs claimed the same position; the later one won.
98:           report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, position);
99:         }
100:       }
101:       Some("connection") => match read_connection(object) {
102:         Some((from, port, to)) => connections.entry(from).or_default().push((port, to)),
103:         None => report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, position_of(object)),
104:       },
105:       // The camera record only remembers where the editor was scrolled to.
106:       Some("camera") => {}
107:       _ => report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, position_of(object)),
108:     }
109:   }
110: 
111:   // Ports are numbered in the document and may arrive out of order, so each
112:   // group is sorted and compacted into dense indices. A gap in the numbering
113:   // therefore closes up rather than leaving a hole choices cannot be indexed
114:   // through.
115:   for (from, mut ports) in connections {
116:     ports.sort_unstable_by_key(|(port, _)| *port);
117:     ports.dedup_by_key(|(port, _)| *port);
118: 
119:     for (_, to) in ports {
120:       let target_text = match loaded.hub(to) {
121:         Some(hub) => hub.text().to_string(),
122:         None => {
123:           // The choice points at a position that does not exist.
124:           report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, from);
125:           continue;
126:         }
127:       };
128: 
129:       let target_text_name = context.intern_optional(&target_text);
130:       let Some(hub) = loaded.hub_mut(from) else {
131:         continue;
132:       };
133:       hub.push_choice(Choice::new(target_text, target_text_name, to));
134:     }
135:   }
136: 
137:   context.add_conversation(loaded);
138:   LOUDER_LOAD_OK
139: }
140: 
141: /// Load the speaker config from a JSON document.
142: ///
143: /// The config is a singleton: it is not tied to any one conversation, so a
144: /// speaker declared here is visible from all of them. Records replace any
145: /// speaker of the same name rather than failing, so a config can be reloaded.
146: ///
147: /// A record the engine cannot read is reported and skipped rather than costing
148: /// the caller the rest of the config. The config belongs to no conversation, so
149: /// its events carry no conversation name.
150: pub fn load_config(context: &mut LouderContext, document: &str) -> LoadError {
151:   let records = match parse_records(context, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, document) {
152:     Ok(records) => records,
153:     Err(error) => return error,
154:   };
155: 
156:   for record in records {
157:     let Some(object) = record.as_object() else {
158:       report(context, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, BLANK_POSITION);
159:       continue;
160:     };
161: 
162:     if record_type(object) != Some("speaker") {
163:       continue;
164:     }
165: 
166:     let name_text = string_field(object, "name");
167:     if name_text.is_empty() {
168:       // A speaker with no name is not a speaker the host could ever ask for.
169:       report(context, LOUDER_NAME_NONE, LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT, BLANK_POSITION);
170:       continue;
171:     }
172: 
173:     let name = context.intern_name(&name_text);
174:     let flags = SpeakerFlags::from_bits_truncate(int_field(object, "flags"));
175:     let listening = context.intern_list(&split_lines(&string_field(object, "listening")));
176:     let additional = read_additional(context, object, SPEAKER_KEYS);
177: 
178:     context.set_speaker(Speaker::new(name, flags, listening, additional));
179:   }
180: 
181:   LOUDER_LOAD_OK
182: }

1045: /// The special variable an expression writes to, and the value it writes.
1046: ///
1047: /// The engine has one writable special variable, `%speaker`, and a write to it
1048: /// belongs to the move that made it rather than to the hub: it changes who the
1049: /// hub appears to speak as for that move, and leaves the hub itself alone.
1050: ///
1051: /// The value is only read when it can be read without side effects, since the
1052: /// write is evaluated before the move is announced and a second write to story
1053: /// state would be counted twice.
1054: pub fn special_write(
1055:   context: &mut LouderContext,
1056:   scope: &EvaluationScope,
1057:   expr: &Expr,
1058: ) -> Option<(String, LouderVariant)> {
1059:   let Expr::Assign(name, value) = expr else {
1060:     return None;
1061:   };
1062: 
1063:   if !is_special(name) || has_side_effects(value) {
1064:     return None;
1065:   }
1066: 
1067:   let evaluator = Evaluator::new(scope.clone());
1068:   match evaluator.evaluate_expr(context, value) {
1069:     Ok(Evaluation::Value(value)) => Some((name.clone(), value)),
1070:     _ => None,
1071:   }
1072: }
1073: 
1074: /// Reconstruct the text of an ask's arguments for the host to read back.
1075: fn args_to_text(args: &[Expr]) -> String {
1076:   args.iter().map(expr_to_text).collect::<Vec<String>>().join(" ")
1077: }
1078: 
1079: fn expr_to_text(expr: &Expr) -> String {
1080:   match expr {
1081:     Expr::Int(value) => value.to_string(),
1082:     Expr::Float(value) => value.to_string(),
1083:     Expr::Bool(value) => value.to_string(),
1084:     Expr::Str(value) => value.clone(),
1085:     Expr::Var(name) => name.clone(),
1086:     Expr::Special(name) => name.clone(),
1087:     Expr::Ask(args) => format!("($({}))", args_to_text(args)),
1088:     Expr::Neg(inner) => format!("-{}", expr_to_text(inner)),
1089:     Expr::Not(inner) => format!("!{}", expr_to_text(inner)),
1090:     Expr::Call(name, args) => {
1091:       format!("{}({})", name, args.iter().map(expr_to_text).collect::<Vec<String>>().join(", "))
1092:     }
1093:     Expr::Binary(op, lhs, rhs) => format!(
1094:       "({} {} {})",
1095:       expr_to_text(lhs),
1096:       bin_op_text(*op),
1097:       expr_to_text(rhs)
1098:     ),
1099:     Expr::Assign(name, value) => format!("{name} = {}", expr_to_text(value)),
1100:     // Pre versus post, and up versus down, are separate facts, so both are
1101:     // rendered: the node carries one as its variant and the other as the sign of
1102:     // its step.
1103:     Expr::PreInc(name, step) => format!("{}{name}", step_text(*step)),
1104:     Expr::PostInc(name, step) => format!("{name}{}", step_text(*step)),
1105:   }
1106: }

1110:     "--"
1111:   } else {
1112:     "++"
1113:   }
1114: }

280: /// Turn a JSON value into a variant.
281: ///
282: /// Objects and arrays have no variant of their own, so they are interned as
283: /// their compact JSON text, which keeps them readable without inventing a
284: /// fourth variant kind.
285: fn read_variant(context: &mut LouderContext, value: &Value) -> LouderVariant {
286:   match value {
287:     Value::Null => LouderVariant::Int(0),
288:     Value::Bool(value) => LouderVariant::from_bool(*value),
289:     Value::Number(number) => match number.as_i64() {
290:       Some(value) => LouderVariant::Int(value as i32),
291:       None => LouderVariant::Float(number.as_f64().unwrap_or(0.0) as f32),
292:     },
293:     Value::String(text) => LouderVariant::String(context.intern_optional(text)),
294:     other => LouderVariant::String(context.intern_optional(&other.to_string())),
295:   }
296: }
297: 
298: /// Report a load problem as an event, so that one bad record does not abort the
299: /// whole load.
300: ///
301: /// `position` is the record at fault, or [`BLANK_POSITION`] when the problem was
302: /// with the document as a whole.
303: fn report(context: &mut LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, error: LoadError, position: LouderPosition) {
304:   context.push_event(LouderEvent {
305:     conversation,
306:     channel: LOUDER_NAME_NONE,
307:     actor: super::actor::LOUDER_ACTOR_NONE,
308:     data: EventData::LoadError(LoadFailure { position, error }),
309:   });
310: }
311: 
312: /// Parse `document` as the list of records both loaders expect.
313: ///
314: /// A problem with the document as a whole is reported as a load error event
315: /// before the error is returned, so that a host reading events rather than
316: /// return codes still learns that the load failed and why.
317: fn parse_records(
318:   context: &mut LouderContext,
319:   conversation: LouderName,
320:   document: &str,
321: ) -> Result<Vec<Value>, LoadError> {
322:   let parsed: Value = match serde_json::from_str(document) {
323:     Ok(parsed) => parsed,
324:     Err(_) => {
325:       report(context, conversation, LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON, BLANK_POSITION);
326:       return Err(LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON);
327:     }
328:   };

1: //! Writing what the engine holds back out as JSON.
2: //!
3: //! This is the other half of [`super::json_loader`]: documents come in through
4: //! that module and leave through this one, in the shape a save file or a
5: //! debugging dump wants. Nothing here parses. Every document is assembled from
6: //! the context as it stands, and both are written in a fixed order so that
7: //! asking twice gives the same bytes.
8: 
9: use super::context::LouderContext;
10: use super::name::{LouderInternContext, LouderName};
11: use super::object::LouderVariant;
12: 
13: /// Escape `text` as a JSON string, quotes included.
14: fn quote(text: &str) -> String {
15:   let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len() + 2);
16:   out.push('"');
17:   for c in text.chars() {
18:     match c {
19:       '"' => out.push_str("\\\""),
20:       '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
21:       '\n' => out.push_str("\\n"),
22:       '\r' => out.push_str("\\r"),
23:       '\t' => out.push_str("\\t"),
24:       c if (c as u32) < 0x20 => out.push_str(&format!("\\u{:04x}", c as u32)),
25:       c => out.push(c),
26:     }
27:   }
28:   out.push('"');
29:   out
30: }
31: 
32: /// Render a variant as JSON, resolving strings against the context.
33: fn variant(context: &LouderContext, value: &LouderVariant) -> String {
34:   match value {
35:     LouderVariant::Int(i) => i.to_string(),
36:     LouderVariant::Float(f) => {
37:       // Whole floats print without a trailing `.0` so that round-tripping an
38:       // integer stored as a float does not change how it reads.
39:       if f.fract() == 0.0 && f.is_finite() {
40:         format!("{}", *f as i64)
41:       } else {
42:         f.to_string()
43:       }
44:     }
45:     LouderVariant::String(name) => match context.resolve_name(*name) {
46:       Some(text) => quote(text),
47:       None => quote(""),
48:     },
49:   }
50: }
51: 
52: /// Render a set of name/variant pairs as a JSON object, in ascending key order.
53: fn object(
54:   context: &LouderContext,
55:   keys: &[LouderName],
56:   values: impl Fn(LouderName) -> LouderVariant,
57: ) -> String {
58:   let mut out = String::from("{");
59:   for (index, key) in keys.iter().enumerate() {
60:     if index > 0 {
61:       out.push(',');
62:     }
63:     let name = match context.resolve_name(*key) {
64:       Some(text) => quote(text),
65:       None => quote(""),
66:     };
67:     out.push_str(&format!("{}:{}", name, variant(context, &values(*key))));
68:   }
69:   out.push('}');
70:   out
71: }
72: 
73: /// Render a list of names as a JSON array of strings.
74: ///
75: /// A name with nothing behind it is written as null rather than as an empty
76: /// string, so that a list entry the engine cannot account for stays visible as
77: /// a gap instead of reading as text.
78: fn name_list(context: &LouderContext, names: &[LouderName]) -> String {
79:   let mut out = String::from("[");
80:   for (index, name) in names.iter().enumerate() {
81:     if index > 0 {
82:       out.push(',');
83:     }
84:     match context.resolve_name(*name) {
85:       Some(text) => out.push_str(&quote(text)),
86:       None => out.push_str("null"),
87:     }
88:   }
89:   out.push(']');
90:   out
91: }
92: 
93: /// The whole story state of a conversation, as a JSON object.
94: ///
95: /// Every bucket is included: the conversation's own saved and unsaved variables,
96: /// and the shared ones.
97: pub fn state(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName) -> String {
98:   let keys = context.all_state_keys(conversation);
99:   object(context, &keys, |key| context.get_state(conversation, key))
100: }
101: 
102: /// The story state and every actor of a conversation, as a JSON document.
103: ///

105: /// describe. The document has a `state` object and an `actors` array.
106: pub fn snapshot(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName) -> Option<String> {
107:   context.get_conversation(conversation)?;
108: 
109:   let state = state(context, conversation);
110: 
111:   let mut actors = String::from("[");

113:   for (index, entry) in entries.iter().enumerate() {
114:     if index > 0 {
115:       actors.push(',');
116:     }
117: 
118:     let channel = match context.resolve_name(entry.channel()) {
119:       Some(text) => quote(text),
120:       None => "null".to_string(),
121:     };
122: 
123:     let additional = object(context, &entry.additional().sorted_keys(), |key| {
124:       entry.additional_get(key).cloned().unwrap_or_default()
125:     });
126: 
127:     actors.push_str(&format!(
128:       "{{\"id\":{},\"position\":{},\"channel\":{},\"priority\":{},\"flags\":{},\"speak_as\":{},\"listen_to\":{},\"additional\":{}}}",
129:       entry.id(),

132:       entry.priority(),
133:       entry.flags().bits(),
134:       name_list(context, entry.speak_as()),

136:       additional
137:     ));
138:   }
139:   actors.push(']');
140: 
141:   Some(format!("{{\"state\":{},\"actors\":{}}}", state, actors))
142: }

5: /// Where a story state variable is stored, decided by its name.
6: ///
7: /// Two independent rules combine into the four cases of the engine guide: a
8: /// leading underscore means "not saved", and an uppercase first letter means
9: /// "shared across conversations".
10: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
11: pub enum VariableScope {
12:   /// Saved, and private to the conversation it was written in.
13:   LocalSaved,
14:   /// Saved, and visible to every conversation.
15:   SharedSaved,
16:   /// Discarded on save, and private to the conversation.
17:   LocalUnsaved,
18:   /// Discarded on save, and visible to every conversation.
19:   SharedUnsaved,
20: }
21: 
22: /// Classify a variable by the naming rules of the engine guide.
23: ///
24: /// `_a` and `__1` are local and unsaved, `_A` and `__Shared` are shared and
25: /// unsaved, `a` is local and saved, and `A` is shared and saved. A name with
26: /// no letters at all falls back to local and saved.
27: pub fn classify_variable(name: &str) -> VariableScope {
28:   let significant = name.trim_start_matches('_');
29:   let shared = significant
30:     .chars()
31:     .next()
32:     .is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_uppercase());
33:   let saved = !name.starts_with('_');
34: 
35:   match (shared, saved) {
36:     (false, true) => VariableScope::LocalSaved,
37:     (true, true) => VariableScope::SharedSaved,
38:     (false, false) => VariableScope::LocalUnsaved,
39:     (true, false) => VariableScope::SharedUnsaved,
40:   }
41: }
42:

53: /// A loaded conversation is handed out as a copy; what the engine holds

1071: /// Load a conversation from a JSON document in `buffer`.
1072: ///
1073: /// The buffer holds `max_size` bytes. JSON is text, so those bytes have to be
1074: /// UTF-8 for the engine to read them; [`LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON`] is returned
1075: /// if they are not, and re-encoding them is the host's job rather than the
1076: /// engine's.
1077: ///
1078: /// - The JSON is expected in array form; [`LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_A_LIST`] is
1079: ///   returned otherwise.
1080: /// - Standard JSON syntax errors are returned as
1081: ///   [`LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON`].
1082: /// - Standard properties are left default or read from matching JSON fields.
1083: /// - Additional JSON fields are treated as additional data on hubs.
1084: /// - [`LOUDER_LOAD_ALREADY_LOADED`] is returned if `conversation` is already
1085: ///   occupied by another loaded conversation.
1086: /// - Problems with individual records do not fail the load. They are emitted as
1087: ///   events instead, carrying the offending hub's position in
1088: ///   [`Motion::source_position`] and the [`LoadError`] in
1089: ///   [`Motion::target_position`].
1090: #[unsafe(no_mangle)]
1091: pub extern "C" fn load_conversation_json(ctx: *mut LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, buffer: *mut u8, max_size: usize) -> LoadError {
1092: if ctx.is_null() || buffer.is_null() {
1093:   return LOUDER_LOAD_MALFORMED_JSON;
1094: }
1095: 
1096: let bytes = unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(buffer, max_size) };
1097: let document: &str = match decode(bytes) {
1098:   Ok(document) => document,
1099:   Err(error) => return error,
1100: };

1102: unsafe { louder::json_loader::load_conversation(&mut *ctx, conversation, document) }

1: //! The rules a host turns on and off.
2: //!
3: //! The rules themselves are a set of bits. A host reads and writes the whole
4: //! configuration at once through
5: //! [`LouderContext::rules`](super::context::LouderContext) and
6: //! [`LouderContext::set_rules`](super::context::LouderContext), and gets the
7: //! names to hand it from [`DialogueRules::from_name`] and
8: //! [`DialogueRules::names`] rather than hard-coding the bits.
9: 
10: bitflags::bitflags! {
11:   /// The dialogue rules a host can turn on and off.
12:   ///
13:   /// Both are on by default, because each is what makes the engine usable
14:   /// without every conversation having to be written around its absence. They
15:   /// are gathered here rather than left as separate switches so that a host
16:   /// reads and writes the whole configuration in one call, and so that adding a
17:   /// rule does not add another pair of engine calls.
18:   #[repr(C)]
19:   #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
20:   pub struct DialogueRules: i32 {
21:     /// The spoke rule. A lone choice on a hub may be taken by an actor who
22:     /// neither speaks nor hears the voice behind it, since there is nobody else
23:     /// to hand it to and no branch for it to be lost on. Without it, linear
24:     /// dialogue hands the lead back and forth between every actor instead of
25:     /// letting one of them carry it.
26:     const SPOKE = 0b00000001;
27: 
28:     /// The singleplayer rule. A hub that only the actor who just chose can see,
29:     /// reached from a hub that had several choices, resolves on its own instead
30:     /// of being shown. How many choices it carries is beside the point: it is
31:     /// the consequence of a choice rather than one, so there is nothing in it
32:     /// for that actor to decide. This is the "continue" of a more traditional
33:     /// singleplayer dialogue system, made implicit, so that a private branch
34:     /// can be routed through without being written as a decision.
35:     const SINGLEPLAYER = 0b00000010;
36:   }
37: }
38: 
39: impl DialogueRules {
40:   /// The name of each rule set, in declaration order.
41:   ///
42:   /// [`DialogueRules::from_name`] is the way back the other way, and returns
43:   /// `None` for a name this build does not know, so a host carrying a
44:   /// configuration written for a newer engine is not stopped by one.
45:   pub fn names(&self) -> Vec<&'static str> {
46:     let mut names: Vec<&'static str> = Vec::new();
47:     if self.contains(DialogueRules::SPOKE) {
48:       names.push("SPOKE");
49:     }
50:     if self.contains(DialogueRules::SINGLEPLAYER) {
51:       names.push("SINGLEPLAYER");
52:     }
53:     names
54:   }
55: }

55: Tags are lists of short strings attached to each hub that can be used to query the conversation as a whole.
56: 
57: Good for finding the "start" node, or filtering for start candidates and picking a specific starting point based on game state.
58: 
59: ## Speakers
60: Speakers are single names on each hub. If present, they determine who the hub is visible to.
61: 
62: Speakers are themselves defined by their own name, flags, "listening" and additional data:
63:  - Name: The name used to reference this speaker in hubs and by actors.
64:  - Flags: Speakers can be flagged "public". If a speaker is not defined,
65:  they are assumed to be public. 
66:    - Public speakers. Hubs with public speakers appear to all conversation actors. Non-public speakers 
67:    only appear to actors who are explicitly "listening" to them. See section on "Listening"
68:  - Listening: Speakers can reference other speakers as "listening." If an actor is speaking
69:  as a speaker, they implicitly listen to these speakers as well.
70:  - see Homogeneous Objects.
71: 
72: How speakers interact with hubs/choices appearing is explained in the Choices
73: section.
74: 
75: The speaker can also be left blank. In this case, the speaker is the first
76: speaker in the speak as list of whichever actor is intending to move onto
77: this hub. For example, if an actor is making a choice to a hub with no speaker, and speaking as "A", 
78: "A" is projected onto the hub temporarily and events emitted by their motion
79: will appear as if that hub had speaker "A" the whole time, but if another actor
80: with speaker "B" makes the same choice, later, they will move onto the hub
81: and emit events as if the hub had speaker "B". These are called "anonymous" hubs.
82: 
83: ## Text Content
84: Arbitrary text content of the hub. Typically this is what you would want to
85: display as the content of your dialogue, along with the Speaker.
86: 
87: ## Choices
88: Choices have some text content describing how the choice should appear when this
89: hub appears. Each choice references another hub by its position.
90: 
91: Actors traverse through dialogue by "making choices" or "selecting choices",
92: which changes their position and all relevant information to match the linked
93: hub under an available choice.
94: 
95: Choices should only appear to an actor if their linked hub would appear, and the
96: speaker of the linked hub matches one of the four cases:
97: 1. The speaker is in the actor's "speak as" list
98: 2. The speaker is in the actor's "listen to" list and no other actor has that speaker in it's "speak as" list.
99: 3. The speaker is public and no other actor has that speaker in it's "speak as" list.
100: 4. The speaker is in the actor's "speak as" list, "listen to" list, or is public, and that choice is the only choice on this hub. This is called the "spoke rule" and it prevents linear dialogue from switching "leading" actors too frequently. 
101: 
102: If the hub has "recipients", these checks are done on the recipient speakers instead of the hub's speaker.
103: 
104: ## Expressions
105: Snippets of code sent to the engine's expression parser. Can directly manipulate
106: story state.
107: 
108: Expressions are either classified as "conditions"- those which check story state,
109: or Statements- those which modify story state.
110: 
111: Conditions are checked every time a hub is checked for whether it should appear,
112: if any condition on a hub evaluates to 0, false, or an empty string (see Variants),
113: the hub will not appear.

- Singleplayer implicit auto: from branching hub, destination visible only to chooser, spoken by chooser.
- Expressions: condition vs statement classification, read-only evaluator for queries, full evaluator for travel.

201: /// The hubs in a channel.
202: ///
203: /// Hubs that name no channel belong to the default channel, so searching for it
204: /// also finds them.
205: pub fn next_hub_by_channel(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, channel: LouderName, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
206:   let main = context.main_channel();
207:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| match hub.channel() {
208:     LOUDER_NAME_NONE => main == channel,
209:     hub_channel => hub_channel == channel,
210:   });
211:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
212: }
213: 
214: /// The hubs spoken by a speaker.
215: pub fn next_hub_spoken_by(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, speaker: LouderName, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
216:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| hub.speaker() == speaker);
217:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
218: }
219: 
220: /// The hubs heard by a speaker.
221: ///
222: /// This searches the speaker's own listening speakers as well as the speaker
223: /// itself, which are the hubs that may appear to an actor speaking as this
224: /// speaker.
225: pub fn next_hub_heard_by(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, speaker: LouderName, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
226:   let mut heard = vec![speaker];
227:   if let Some(entry) = context.get_speaker(speaker) {
228:     heard.extend(entry.listening().iter().copied());
229:   }
230: 
231:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| heard.contains(&hub.speaker()));
232:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
233: }
234: 
235: /// The hubs with a priority within the given bounds.
236: ///
237: /// The bounds are inclusive.
238: pub fn next_hub_by_priority(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, priority_min: i32, priority_max: i32, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
239:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| {
240:     hub.priority() >= priority_min && hub.priority() <= priority_max
241:   });
242:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
243: }
244: 
245: /// The hubs carrying all, or exactly, the given tags.
246: ///
247: /// With `exact` set the hub must carry exactly the listed tags; otherwise
248: /// carrying extra tags is fine.
249: pub fn next_hub_from_tags(context: &LouderContext, conversation: LouderName, include: &[LouderName], exact: bool, start: LouderPosition) -> LouderPosition {
250:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| {
251:     let tags = hub.tags();
252:     include.iter().all(|tag| tags.contains(tag)) && (!exact || tags.len() == include.len())
253:   });
254:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
255: }
256:

259:   let wanted = include.bits();
260:   let positions = matching_hubs(context, conversation, |hub| {

262:   });
263:   next_after(&positions, start, BLANK_POSITION)
264: }

I finished the review, including the rest of `engine/tests/engine_test.cpp` (through `main`). I haven't changed anything. Here's my working model of the engine, followed by the things I'm still unsure about.

## What I understand

- `LouderContext` owns the global speaker config, per-conversation hubs/state/coverage, actors and their state, shared story state, the event queue, and the ask cache.
- A hub owns speaker/recipients/tags/channel/priority/text/expressions/callbacks/flags/additional data. A choice is just a port to another hub and borrows the target hub's text.
- Visibility is: auto hubs only to debug actors; otherwise ownership (`Open` / `Private(recipients)` / `Spoken(speaker)`) matched against the actor's “heard” set (`speak_as` names, their listening lists, and explicit `listen_to`), plus conditions.
- Choice rules are the four guide cases: speak as an owner; heard owner not held by another actor; public owner not held by another actor; or the spoke rule on a lone choice. Recipients override the speaker for both visibility and ownership.
- Movement resolves auto hubs first (first available choice, cycle = `DeadEnd`; emulating followers get a “first appearing branch” fallback), commits position/channel/priority/additional data, emits motion + choice appearances + condition reports, runs first-visit statements/callbacks, then warps followers.
- The singleplayer rule makes a destination implicitly auto when it follows a branching hub, is visible to the chooser, is spoken by the chooser, and is not visible to other in-flow actors.
- Expressions are split into read-only conditions (evaluated through a `const`-safe pass) and statements (run once on first coverage). `%speaker` is the only writable special, and it changes only the apparent speaker of the arrival.
- The C FFI is the real boundary; `louder_godot` is just an adapter, and the engine registers no Godot classes itself.

## Questions

**1. Channel reset on a hub with no channel — intentional?**
`Simulator::apply_travel` (`simulator.rs:781-815`) computes `fake_channel` → `hub.channel` → `main_channel`, and then always writes the result to the actor. The guide says the current channel is “the channel of the last hub that appeared to this actor **with a channel**” and that an actor “stays in that channel until another hub changes it again” (`index.md:162-164, 217-218`). Additional data is already implemented that way (only replaced when the hub has some), so the channel behaviour looks inconsistent. A move into a channel-less hub currently pulls the actor back to `main`. Is that deliberate, or should a missing hub channel leave the actor's channel unchanged?

**2. Compound assignments silently lose their operator.**
`expression.rs:430-437` and `456-464` accept `+=`, `-=`, `*=`, `/=`, `%=`, but `Expr::Assign` has only a name and a value (`expression.rs:36`), so every one of them behaves as plain `=`. `expr_to_text` also renders them as `=`. Is intent “unsupported, but parsed leniently”, or is this pending? Silently turning `x += 1` into `x = 1` seems worse than a parse error.

**3. Asks suspend but are never resumed.**
`Expr::Ask` always returns `Evaluation::Suspended` (`expression.rs:742-768`). Nothing reads the verdict stored by `Context::answer_ask` (`context.rs:160-180`), and there is no pending-work structure to retry a move. In conditions, asks are read-only `0` (`expression.rs:913-970`); in first-visit statements, `run_first_visit` discards the suspension and still marks the hub covered (`simulator.rs:940-948, 867-877`). So today an ask is queued but has no effect on the outcome. The guide says evaluation waits and the action completes when matching verdicts arrive (`index.md:125-134`). Is this deliberately a stub? If resumption is planned, where should the suspended work live — a per-ask record of the pending move/expression replayed from `answer_ask`, or a general pending-task queue?

Related: the `Evaluator` doc says writes are deferred until the whole expression evaluates, so retries are safe (`expression.rs:582-588`), but `Assign`/`PreInc`/`PostInc` write immediately. On a retry, `x++ + $(ask)` would increment twice. Is the deferral still part of the ask design?

**4. Spectator/debug fan-out.**
The guide says spectators see all non-auto hubs, that a choice's resulting hub “appears to all spectator actors regardless of their channel”, and that debug actors see auto hubs and all choices (`index.md:181, 211-215`). In the code:
- `hub_appears` applies ownership and conditions to spectators too (`simulator.rs:326-353`), so a private hub or a false condition hides it from a spectator.
- On a normal move, only the mover and in-flow followers get events (`announce`/`warp_followers`); spectators and debug actors get nothing.
- The only special debug announcement is the auto hub itself (`announce_auto_to_debug`).

So in the guide's worked example, the debug spectator should see hub 4 after actor 0 finishes, but the engine never emits it. Are spectator/“theater audience” events pending, or is the example superseded? And should spectators bypass conditions/ownership, or only channels?

**5. Forced moves: missing modifiers and validation.**
The guide's modifier list includes force-by-channel, force-warp with an optional choice index, and force-channel-change (`index.md:316-329`). The FFI only has `force_position`/`force_warp` for one actor, with optional channel/priority overrides at move time; there is no force-by-channel or direct channel change. Also, `force_move` checks only that the conversation and target hub exist (`simulator.rs:1393-1423`), even though “Changing position” says landing somewhere that may not appear is an error (`index.md:198-202`). Is force supposed to be a debug escape hatch that deliberately skips visibility, or should it validate? And is force-by-channel the intended way to start every actor in a channel at the “Start” hub?

**6. `add_actor` start position/state.**
`add_actor` only checks that the conversation is loaded (`lib.rs:1211-1229`); it accepts a nonexistent `start_position` and always starts the actor on `main` with priority 0, even if the start hub has a channel, priority, or additional data. The guide says functions taking a position should error on invalid objects, and that channel is the last hub that appeared *with* a channel (`index.md:217-218, 331-333`). Should “add at position” run the normal arrival logic (fail on invisible/auto, apply the hub's channel/additional data), or is the intended usage “add at blank, then force_position”?

**7. Follower membership “before or after their choice”.**
`warp_followers` selects actors whose channel equals the leader's **pre-move** channel (`simulator.rs:1094-1101`). The guide says actors “in the same channel as the leader before or after their choice” (`index.md:177`). If the leader enters a channel-setting hub, an actor already waiting in that new channel will not warp. Is the pre-move channel intended, with “or after” only describing the follower staying behind, or should the union of the old and new channels be considered? This interacts with question 1.

**8. The singleplayer rule has an extra privacy condition.**
`implicit_auto_hub_at` requires that no other in-flow actor can see the destination (`simulator.rs:1022-1031`). The guide only says “reached from a hub that had multiple choices” and “spoken by the actor that made the choice” (`index.md:145-153`). Is the extra “only this actor can see it” condition intentional (my reading is that it keeps the rule from eating public consequences), or should the guide be updated?

**9. The event surface is thinner than the guide.**
The guide says events carry choice text/link and an associated motion, statement values, channel/priority changes with motion, and actor-added payloads (`index.md:268-280`). The engine's `ChoiceAppearance` has only position/index; `ChannelChange` has no motion; there is no priority-change variant; `Statement` carries only the expression name; `ActorAdded` has no payload (`event.rs:74-101`). Are hosts expected to query the rest, or are these payloads still planned? Separately, `SpeakerChange`, `TextChange`, `SpeakerAdded`, `SpeakerRemoved`, and `ConversationAdded` exist with `make_*` constructors but are never emitted; are they dead variants, or pending? In particular, should a `%speaker` write emit a `SpeakerChange` event?

**10. Host functions are not connected.**
`impl ExpressionHost for LouderContext` always returns `None` (`expression.rs:571-580`), so `foo()` always evaluates to 0 and there is no FFI registration hook. `eval_condition` also ignores call arguments and returns 0 (`expression.rs:962-967`), which means a call in a condition is false during the visibility pass and could become true during travel if a host hook ever exists. Are host-callable functions planned, or are asks the only intended host interaction?

**11. Config loading and actors.**
The guide's modifier says “load actors and speakers from a speaker config … and add them to a conversation” (`index.md:319`), and the worked example says actors are added automatically from the config. `load_config` only registers speakers globally; it takes no conversation and never creates actors (`json_loader.rs:141-182`). Is auto-adding actors out of scope for now, or should there be a separate “load config into conversation” call? Also, I read choice text as deliberately borrowed from the target hub, with the hub's `choices` field ignored; is that the intended model?

**12. Snapshot/restore.**
`json::snapshot` writes state and actors, but not `covered` hubs, which gate one-shot statements/callbacks (`json.rs:102-142`). There is also no loader for `state` or `snapshot` JSON. Is this a debugging dump only, or the basis of save/restore? If it's for save/restore, the covered set and speaker/config identity matter.

## Smaller confirmations

- **Ask polling:** `get_ask`/`ask_count` include answered asks, and `answer_ask` reorders them. Should hosts poll from 0 until `Missing` (as the tests imply), or did you intend an accessor for “first unanswered”?
- **Load error codes:** duplicate hub positions, malformed records, and unknown record types all report `LOUDER_LOAD_NOT_AN_OBJECT` (`json_loader.rs:92-108`). Is a distinct “duplicate position” code wanted, or is the coarse code acceptable?
- **`hub.set_text`:** it leaves `text_name` stale (`hub.rs:183-187`). Is mutation through the Rust API meant to be unsupported, so hosts only use load/FFI?
- **`next_hub_by_channel`:** hubs with no channel are treated as `main` (`query.rs:201-211`). That's reasonable, but it is a different notion from “the channel an actor is in”; confirm it's intentional.
- **Housekeeping:** `Cargo.toml` uses `edition = "2024"` and depends on `strum`/`strum_macros`, which are unused. `EventPosition` is unused. `out.log` is a stale build log from stub-era warnings. The `load_conversation_json` doc still says load errors live in `Motion::source_position`/`target_position` (`lib.rs:1086-1089`), but they now use `LoadFailure`. Are these cleanup items, or am I missing a consumer?
